diagnostic initial anchor to(10,0) and use values(f0,f1,f2)=(6,4,1).
The actual attempted points are(10,0),(8,-2),(6,0). In row/column

## 6. Law B: longitudinal payment permits legality but exact closure caps it

B adds initial longitudinal surplus1, so v_0^echo=2e0+u0 rather than
e0+u0. It no longer has A's fixed positive remote-cycle obstruction.
Still theta_echo=1 gives from(29)

    sum_(t<n)h_t g_t=S*g_n,
    x_n=X_echo-S*g_n.                                    (32)

Consider ANY legal static realization of gradients with this identity,
allowing arbitrary values, anchors, dimensions and minimizers. Let
x_cl be a closest minimizer and R=||X_echo-x_cl||>0. Such a closest point
exists because the nonempty minimizer set of a finite continuous convex
function is closed, and a minimizing-distance sequence is bounded.
The original terminal-to-minimizer row gives

    2(f_n-f_star)
      <=2<g_n,x_n-x_cl>-||g_n||^2
      =2<g_n,X_echo-x_cl>-(1+2S)||g_n||^2
      <=R^2/(1+2S).                                 (33)

For the last inequality expand the nonnegative square
||X_echo-x_cl-(1+2S)g_n||^2. Dividing by R^2 yields

    TRUE normalized payoff <=1/(1+2S)=H_base(h).     (34)

This bound uses the true closest radius, not distance to a conveniently
selected origin. All longitudinal means and initial correlations in B
are paid by the ORIGINAL terminal row. No common-value choice, moving
minimum, metric amplitude or successful remote-cycle repair avoids it.
The conclusion is a consequence of EXACT(32), not a universal signed-
memory impossibility theorem. A law with nonzero terminal memory or
nonclosing weighted displacement is outside this scope.

### 6.1 A fully legal nonzero-memory finite calibration

## 7. Law C: a legal over-retirement bridge and an unpaid terminal return

since72>53. Initial gradient is unaligned with X_echo, the middle gradient

### 7.2 Last actual two-row cycle rejects the declared law on palindromes

For ANY positive palindrome, its first and last positive steps are
equal, say b>0. Thus alpha0=beta_0, Psi_0=u0 and (16) in C gives

    v_(k-1)^echo=e0-2*(e0+u0/2)=-e0-u0,

    v_k^echo-v_(k-1)^echo=2e0+u0.                              (45)

The actual last update is x_n-x_(last)=-b*sigma*v_(k-1)^echo.
By the exact ORIGINAL two-row cycle(27),

      =sigma^2*[||2e0+u0||^2
                       +b<2e0+u0,-e0-u0>]
      =sigma^2*(5-3b).                               (46)

It is positive for EVERY sigma>0 when b<5/3. This is an exact

the repeated zero queries neither change the point nor pay the cycle.

In the unchanged hierarchy(37), b=1+a_word and

    a_word=beta_word/c_word<1/lambda=sqrt(2)-1,
    b<sqrt(2)<5/3.                                  (47)

The second strict inequality follows by squaring the positive numbers,

bound on H_m. It is only the exact frozen terminal-ratio obstruction
for C. Changing these ratios or allowing terminal memory is outside

## 8. What the changed geometry did, and what remains open

This construction leaves the old projective regular simplex by real
point weights with an exact full-field Gram and frame normalization.
It leaves the old parabolic and aligned-initial-gradient laws by signed
retired suffix memories and arbitrary initial transverse correlation.
B and C actually admit nonzero-memory finite static realizations;
there is no assertion that first-cycle payment always kills signed memory.

But a combinatorial change alone does not control the original directed
cycles or objective. A's early-to-late cancellation hides a positive
REMOTE cycle. B's added longitudinal payment fixes that local failure
in a finite example, but its exact overall displacement identity pays
all apparent objective gains at the terminal optimum row. C escapes
that identity and realizes a finite gain; it spends more than its last
small physical step can supply on the target diagnostic hierarchy.

These obstructions are invariant to the stated gradient scale but NOT
universal under a changed signed coefficient law. They exclude no
arbitrary nonuniform real point weights, arbitrary signed memory,
new initial anchors or terminal longitudinal reversals. In particular
the next interface must break EXACT(32), and a nonclosing return must
pay its actual last-step and remote cycles with newly derived coefficients.
Simply retaining expected sign or a terminal-row advantage is insufficient.

The original affirmative target still requires, for EVERY epsilon>0,
a threshold N(epsilon) independent of h such that for EVERY n>=N and
EVERY nonnegative physical word h there is one admissible static
function with attained minimum and TRUE closest-radius payoff at least
(1-epsilon)U_n^ZJ. A finite bridge such as(41) is not this theorem.
A negative resolution still requires an all-function objective UPPER
bound with a fixed relative benchmark improvement on unbounded physical
horizons. No such upper bound is claimed here.

Any arbitrary-word decomposition, scalable signed return rule, and
uniform asymptotic original-B comparison for a law beyond these three
remain UNPAID THEOREM-LEVEL gaps. No target candidate exists to verify
in this allocated round. No finalization, revocation, publication,
shutdown, original-Goal completion or automatic renewal is authorized.

# Whole-field nonregularity for every positive nonuniform point metric

Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED appendix to max2 round61. No fact promotion,
formal gate or computation. This gives an all-weight argument in addition
to the explicit two-step metric demonstration in87225e1b68c94b70.
It is not used as a separately established premise.

Let p_wm be a prime, and put N=p_wm^2+p_wm+1. Points of the projective
plane over Z/p_wm Z are nonzero triples modulo nonzero scalars, and
lines are nonzero forms modulo scalars, with incidence by annihilation.
There are N points and N lines because each nonzero class has p_wm-1
representatives. A nonzero form has a two-dimensional kernel, giving
p_wm+1 points per line. Distinct line forms have a one-dimensional
common kernel, hence one intersection point. Dually each point is on
p_wm+1 lines and two distinct points share one line. All counts follow
by eliminating one or two coordinates with nonzero coefficients.

Let B be the real point-by-line incidence matrix. With I the identity,
J_ff the all-ones matrix and 1 the all-ones column, the point-side
counts give

    B B^T=p_wm I+J_ff, B1=(p_wm+1)1.                  (A1)

B is invertible since for nonzero z the quadratic form of B B^T is
p_wm||z||^2+(sum z)^2>0.

Give EVERY point P an arbitrary STRICTLY POSITIVE real weight omega_P.
Let W=diag(omega_P), Omega_w=sum_P omega_P, omega_max=max_P omega_P,
s_P=sqrt(omega_P), and P_w=I-s*s^T/Omega_w. Define every line column

    A_L=P_w W^(1/2)1_L/sqrt(p_wm*omega_max),
    A=(A_L)_L, G_wm=A^T A.                           (A2)

Thus the exact weighted Euclidean Gram has entries

    (G_wm)_LM=[sum_(P in L intersection M)omega_P
          -(sum_(P in L)omega_P)(sum_(P in M)omega_P)/Omega_w]
                                               /(p_wm*omega_max).

Using(A1), P_w*s=0 and W^(1/2)J_ff W^(1/2)=s*s^T gives the complete
field frame identity

    A A^T=P_w W P_w/omega_max.                       (A3)

Also A1=0. Conversely A c=0 implies Bc is constant, since
P_w W^(1/2)Bc=0 and the kernel of P_w is span(s). Invertibility of
B and B1=(p_wm+1)1 imply c is constant. Therefore

    ker A=span(1), range A=s perpendicular.           (A4)

Claim: the WHOLE-FIELD Gram G_wm is the Gram of a regular simplex
if and only if all omega_P are equal. Here regular simplex means

    G_wm=kappa_reg*(I-11^T/N) for some kappa_reg>0.   (A5)

This definition is equivalent to constant diagonal/off-diagonal entries
for the rank N-1 Gram with null column1: the row-sum condition forces
diagonal+(N-1)*off-diagonal=0, giving exactly(A5).

First suppose(A5). By A1=0,
A A^T A=A G_wm=kappa_reg A. By(A4), A A^T is multiplication by
kappa_reg on s perpendicular and zero on span(s); consequently
A A^T=kappa_reg P_w. Equation(A3) yields

    P_w(W-c_reg I)P_w=0, c_reg=kappa_reg*omega_max>0. (A6)

For any two distinct points i,j let
z_ij=e_i/s_i-e_j/s_j, where e_i,e_j are their coordinate unit vectors.
Then z_ij is perpendicular to s. Its quadratic form in(A6) is

    (omega_i-c_reg)/omega_i
                   +(omega_j-c_reg)/omega_j=0.      (A7)

Put zeta_i^wm=(omega_i-c_reg)/omega_i. Every distinct pair satisfies
zeta_i^wm+zeta_j^wm=0. Choose any three distinct indices, possible since N>=7.
The first two pair equalities give zeta_j^wm=zeta_k^wm=-zeta_i^wm; the third gives
-2zeta_i^wm=0. Thus zeta_i^wm=zeta_j^wm=zeta_k^wm=0. Pairing any other index with i gives
that index's zeta-value0 as well. Hence omega_i=c_reg for EVERY point.

Conversely if all omega_P equal c>0, then omega_max=c,
P_w=I-11^T/N and A=P_w B/sqrt(p_wm). The line-side counts give
B^T B=p_wm I+J_ff, and B^T1=(p_wm+1)1. Therefore

    G_wm=(1/p_wm)[B^T B-((p_wm+1)^2/N)11^T]
         =I-11^T/N,                                 (A8)

since N=p_wm^2+p_wm+1 makes 1-(p_wm+1)^2/N=-p_wm/N. This proves
the converse and identifies the normalization without a spectral
or finite-field-to-real shortcut.

Apply this argument to the round61 weights

    omega_P=1+1_(P=P0)
                +sum_(r<floor(k/2))b_r 1_(P in L_r)/(1+S),
    S=sum_(r<k)b_r, b_r>0, k>=2.

The sum over the first half is less than S, so every point other than
P0 has weight<2 whereas P0 has weight>=2. They are nonuniform.
Thus the complete real field Gram is NOT a regular simplex for EVERY
word to which the paired law applies, not only the written p_wm=2
calibration. This strengthens the geometry check, but changes none
of the three coefficient laws or their already joint-derived failures.
It gives no all-word lower witness, full-class upper bound or accepted
new fact. There is no extra mathematical program or formal submission.

Glossary: p_wm,N,P,L,B,I,J_ff,1 are the explicitly defined projective
prime, number of points/lines, points, lines, real incidence matrix,
identity, all-ones matrix and all-ones column. omega_P,W,Omega_w,
omega_max,s,P_w,A_L,A,G_wm are the real weighted construction(A2).
kappa_reg is the positive regular-simplex coefficient in(A5); c_reg
is kappa_reg*omega_max. e_i,z_ij,zeta_i^wm are the coordinate unit vectors,
test vectors and diagonal ratios in(A7). The final b_r,S,k,P0,L_r
are positive physical steps, their total, their count, a distinguished
point and the selected lines in the explicitly restated word weights.

# Silver certificate deformation: source reconstruction and three viability screens

Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED ANALYTICAL DRAFT. No formal submission, accepted new
fact, full-class bound for the perturbed word, or resolution of the original
minimax target is claimed. This is max4's one allocated round after completed64,
with completed ceiling65 / attempted ceiling66. TASK.md and guidance
8fd9094550caff70 govern; plan cbe64562a9a92f5d records the work. Latest
guidance56f0c49630564f24 was read and explicitly leaves this assignment unchanged.

The outcome is three exact, narrowly scoped failed screens. The second screen
allows arbitrary terminal-gradient coefficients; the third changes the two
right-child weights independently and passes the first two levels. Neither
survives the original-coordinate test at the third level. The source itself
does give a sharp GD objective certificate for ORIGINAL Silver, but its missing
extension is precisely a primitive/history inequality already present in the
project. No new theorem for the perturbed word follows merely from retrieval.

## 1. Model, accepted inputs, and unchanged physical word

Use fact1252b8823bb5a290's normalized convention: L=1,
x_star=g_star=f_star=0, x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i) h_t g_t, and

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.

All formal vectors (x_0,g_0,...,g_n) remain independent when testing a
certificate. For a nonnegative physical word h, W_n(h) is the supremum of
2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over the entire smooth-convex class
and every finite dimension. Thus W=2R in the project's notation. Every ordered
row exists; unspecified multipliers below are zero, not deleted constraints.

The other accepted inputs are fact04219554e41d9750, Sections10--11
(primitive and objective identities, including their full-coordinate scope),
and fact5fd396eba75651e7, Section10 (the original benchmark comparison only).
No unverified previous obstruction or numerical optimizer is a premise.

Fix one enclosing integer m and set

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j), d_j=a c^j, 0<=j<m,
    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, n_m=2^m-1.

Every child uses this SAME epsilon, beta, c, a. In particular testing H_3
below means the actual initial seven-step subword of H_m, not resetting m=3.
The terminal gradient is a formal sample, not another physical step.

## 2. Complete source and hypothesis mapping

### 2.1 Altschuler--Parrilo and the original full interpolation certificate

Source: Jason M. Altschuler and Pablo A. Parrilo, *Acceleration by Stepsize
Hedging II: Silver Stepsize Schedule for Smooth Convex Optimization*,
arXiv:2309.16530v1, Theorem1.1, Definition2.1, Theorem3.4, Lemmas3.5--3.7,
and AppendixA. The exact downloaded HTML reports v1, dated28September2023.
The proof, including the corrections, signs, and AppendixA expansions, was read.
URL: https://arxiv.org/html/2309.16530 .

The source's nonnegative residual is

    P_ij=2(f_i-f_j)+2<g_j,x_j-x_i>-||g_i-g_j||^2 = -2 Q_ji.

Consequently both a transpose and a factor2 are required when transferring
its multipliers. Its theorem is for a convex L-smooth function with a chosen
minimizer, arbitrary initialization and dimension, ORIGINAL Silver word
H^0_(j+1)=(H^0_j,1+lambda^(j-1),H^0_j), and n=2^j-1.
Write A=lambda^j, S=A-1, and

    C_AP=(1+sqrt(4A^2-3))/2, r_AP=1/(2C_AP).

The source identity is

    sum sigma_ij P_ij
      =||x_0||^2-||x_n-C_AP g_n||^2-2C_AP f_n.       (2.1)

It gives W_n(H^0_j)<=1/C_AP, not r_AP. This is a direct GD objective
bound, not a proximal bound. Its multiplier recursion glues the left copy
with weight1 and the right copy with weight lambda^2=1+2lambda, adds
lambda times each actual right step in the two endpoint-to-right rows,
and corrects the three endpoint/optimum incidences. The final optimum
incidences are sigma_(star,i)=h_i for i<n, sigma_(star,n)=C_AP,
sigma_(n,star)=S. Negative correction entries must be aggregated with their
inherited entries; the final multipliers are nonnegative.

Here is a direct reconstruction in the ORIGINAL Q convention, using only
the accepted primitive identity, which also checks the source's full array.
For any word h of sum S and terminal n define

    V_h=2 sum_(i<n) h_i(f_i-f_n)
          +sum_(i<n) h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-S(S+1)||g_n||^2.

On the original Silver word define the finite multiplier array Lambda^0
from the empty array as follows. For a join, p is the bridge index, z is the
parent terminal, and j ranges over the nonterminal right-child queries.
Let A=1+sum(child), d=A/lambda and let h_j denote its right steps. Embed
both complete child arrays and add

    Lambda^0_parent = Lambda^0_left + lambda^2 Lambda^0_right
       +2lambda [ sum_j h_j (Q_(j,p)+Q_(j,z))
                                      +Q_(z,p)+d Q_(p,z) ].   (2.2)

Here and below a sum of Q terms denotes its coefficient array. All four
added coefficient types are nonnegative. Fact04219554e41d9750, identity(5),
applies because d(2A+d)=A^2. It yields the polynomial identity

                    V_h + sum Lambda^0_ij Q_ij = 0.           (2.3)

The empty identity is0=0. The actual parent capacity is2A+d=lambda A;
induction proves A=lambda^j, so this is exactly the original physical Silver
word at every level. It is an identity on all formal gradients, with zero
PSD remainder, not a restriction to actual or coincident gradients.

To recover (2.1), take sigma_ij=Lambda^0_ji/2 on finite pairs and the
three optimum-incidence types displayed above. The finite part of the
source's Theta+Xi+Delta recursion is exactly (2.2) after this transpose and
factor2; its endpoint coefficients are 2lambda and 2lambda d in Q convention.
The remaining identity follows from

    V_h-2 sum_(i<n) h_i Q_(i,star)
      =||x_0||^2-||x_n||^2-2S f_n-S(S+1)||g_n||^2,             (2.4)
    C_AP^2-C_AP=S(S+2).

Indeed adding -2C_AP Q_(n,star)-2S Q_(star,n) to (2.4) gives the
right-hand side of (2.1). This checks all coefficients without invoking
the source's computer-algebra script. In project normalization the full
PSD identity is the square ||x_n-C_AP g_n||^2/C_AP, with finite multipliers
Lambda^0/C_AP, prefix-to-optimum weights2h_i/C_AP, terminal-to-optimum
weight2, and optimum-to-terminal weight2S/C_AP.

The SAME finite baseline array has the sharper completion

    ||x_0||^2-2D f_n+sum Lambda^0_ij Q_ij
       +2 sum_(i<n) h_i Q_(i,star)+2A Q_(n,star)
                         =||x_n-A g_n||^2,  D=2S+1=2A-1.    (2.5)

For an explicit expansion, substitute (2.3) and (2.4), then use
A^2-A=S(S+1) and D=S+A. Thus every coefficient in (2.5) is nonnegative,
and division by D gives a full original-space objective certificate:

                         W_n(H^0_j)<=1/(2lambda^j-1).        (2.6)

The matching Huber function has slope t=1/D, value x^2/2 for |x|<=t,
and t|x|-t^2/2 otherwise. Start at1, with minimizer0. Since every step is
nonnegative and each prefix sum is at most S, every query lies in the
positive affine piece: x_i=1-t sum_(k<i) h_k and x_n=(S+1)/D>=t.
Its value satisfies2f(x_n)=1/D. This proves tightness of the baseline
completion within this joint reconstruction. It supplies no perturbed-word
certificate. No separate unverified intermediate result is being adopted.

### 2.2 Chikhi: product identity, joint potential, and proximal output

Source: Salah R. Chikhi, *Last-Iterate Performance of Gradient Descent and
Relaxed Proximal Point via s-Composability*, arXiv:2609.13765v1,
Definition1, Proposition1 with proof, Theorem1 with equality construction,
Lemma2 with proof, and Section5/Theorem4 with proof.
URL: https://arxiv.org/html/2609.13765v1 .

Its definition requires a positive schedule and BOTH

    eta=1/(1+S)=product_(i<n)(h_i-1),
    ||x_0-x_star||^2 >= ||x_n-x_star||^2
                    +S(S+1)||g_n||^2+2S(f_n-f_star).          (2.7)

The source obtains Silver's s-composability from Grimmer et al., Lemma6.
No uninspected theorem of that paper is needed here: (2.3)--(2.4) reconstruct
the necessary baseline inequality from the accepted project identity.
For a varying schedule the history expression in Chikhi's Lemma2 is

    sum h_i[2(f_i-f_n)+||g_i||^2+2<g_i,x_0-x_i>]
           -||x_n-x_0||^2-S(S+1)||g_n||^2.

Expanding x_0-x_i=sum_(t<i)h_t g_t cancels every off-diagonal product
against the squared displacement, leaving exactly V_h. Adding the
nonnegative source residuals sum h_i P_(star,i) means subtracting
2sum h_i Q_(i,star), which gives (2.7) by (2.4). Thus an appeal to this
history mechanism for H_m is precisely the already-unpaid primitive
inequality; the new paper does not furnish it for a perturbed word.

For a proper closed convex F with an attained minimum and mu>0, the
source's relaxed proximal iteration is

    z_i=prox_(mu F)(x_i), x_(i+1)=x_i+h_i(z_i-x_i),
    z_n=prox_(mu F)(x_n).

Its exact theorem is sup mu(F(z_n)-F_star)/||x_0-x_star||^2=1/[4(S+1)]
for a positive s-composable schedule. The equality example is F(x)=t|x|
with t=R/[2mu(S+1)], x_0=R, and z_n=R/2. For original Silver this is
1/(4lambda^j). Its terminal point is z_n, not our x_n.

The normalized envelope f=mu F_mu satisfies g_n=x_n-z_n and

                   f(x_n)-f_star=mu(F(z_n)-F_star)+||g_n||^2/2.

Dropping the last term would misidentify the objective. The source's joint
terminal inequality instead gives, with t=||g_n||,

    mu(F(z_n)-F_star)+(S+1)t^2<=R t,
    f(x_n)-f_star <= R t-(S+1/2)t^2 <= R^2/[2(2S+1)].        (2.8)

This is the paid conversion, with W<=1/(2S+1). For an arbitrary smooth
f, the same result follows directly from (2.7) and the original optimum
interpolation inequality; no assumption that a particular smooth f has
already been supplied as an envelope is necessary. Formula(2.5) gives its
explicit original-row certificate whenever (2.3) is available.

The perturbed H_m does not satisfy the definition's product identity.
Set v=a/(c-2), so0<v<1. The physical sum identity, checked by substitution
in A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j, is

    A_j=v c^j+(1-v)2^j.

Counting each level's multiplicity, or inducting in the physical word, gives

    product_(i<n_m)(h_i-1)=beta^(2^m-1)c^(-m),
    A_m product_(i<n_m)(h_i-1)
      =beta^(2^m-1)[v+(1-v)(2/c)^m] <1.                      (2.9)

This prevents using the named s-composability theorem as stated. It does
NOT disprove the joint potential without the product identity.

### 2.3 Wang--Ma--Yang--Zhou: the older metrics

Source: Bofan Wang, Shiqian Ma, Junfeng Yang and Danqing Zhou,
*Relaxed Proximal Point Algorithm: Tight Complexity Bounds and Acceleration
without Momentum*, arXiv:2410.08890, Lemma5.1 and Theorems5.2--5.3 with the
displayed proofs. URL: https://arxiv.org/html/2410.08890 .

For arbitrary convex L-smooth h and original Silver, Theorem5.2 states the
tight GD objective bound (h(x_n)-h_star)/(L R^2)<=1/(4lambda^j-2), and
gradient bound ||grad h(x_n)||/(L R)<=lambda^(-j). Its finite array in
Lemma5.1 is Lambda^0 transposed and divided by2: its residual is -Q_ji
and its identity(34) has right-hand side V_h/2. The weighted optimum completion
is reproduced explicitly in (2.5); no typography in the extracted proof
is being used as an algebraic premise.

Theorem5.3 applies this to an envelope and bounds
F(z_n)-F_star+(mu/2)||grad F_mu(x_n)||^2 by R^2/[mu(4lambda^j-2)].
The discussion calls the proximal objective bound obtained by dropping
the residual term non-tight. That is the older gap addressed by Chikhi.
It is not a new sharper bound for the GD objective of a perturbed word.

## 3. A paid terminal-coefficient relaxation

For a word of sum S define the generalized history form

    V_(h,K)=2 sum h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-K||g_n||^2.

Suppose a proposed nonnegative finite array has the exact full identity
V_(h,K)+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij=M(g), where M is a quadratic form in ALL
formal gradients. This equation defines M; it does not presume PSD.
For K>=0 put

    b_K=(1+sqrt(1+4K))/2, F_K=S+b_K.

Expanding (2.4) with K in place of S(S+1) gives

    ||x_0||^2-2F_K f_n+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij
          +2sum h_i Q_(i,star)+2b_K Q_(n,star)
                          =M(g)+||x_n-b_K g_n||^2.           (3.1)

All added coefficients are nonnegative. If M>=0, division by F_K would give

    W_n(h)<=1/F_K=2/[2S+1+sqrt(1+4K)].

Relative to Huber1/(2S+1), the loss is exactly

    chi_K=(2S+1)/(S+b_K).                                   (3.2)

If only a radius-inflated joint inequality with constant r_rad were proved,
the corresponding upper would pay the additional factor r_rad. Neither a
smaller K nor a different radius coefficient is free. The screens below test
the actual M, not a presumed child oracle or an informal potential estimate.

## 4. One full-coordinate coefficient expansion for the screens

Consider (h,1+d,h), with child sum s, A=s+1, T=2A+d, parent sum T-1.
Let p denote its bridge query and z its terminal. Let r_vec=sum_j h_j g_j
over the nonterminal right child. Its two copies of V_(h,K) have factor1
and kappa. Introduce the nonnegative correction array

    E=u sum_j h_j Q_(j,p)+v_w sum_j h_j Q_(j,z)
                                      +p_w Q_(z,p)+z_w Q_(p,z).

The symbol v_w is a multiplier weight, distinct from v=a/(c-2).
For a nonempty right child, all function values cancel in
V_(parent,K_+)-V_left-kappa V_right+E iff

    u+v_w=2(kappa-1),
    u s+p_w-z_w=2(A+d).                                     (4.1)

When the right child is empty, choosing these identities remains a valid
definition of the continuation. Empty sums are zero; no formal endpoint is
identified with a different endpoint. The resulting original quadratic is

    M_def_parent=M_def_left+kappa M_def_right+R,
    R=(u-v_w)||r_vec||^2/2
       +(u d-z_w)<r_vec,g_p>+(p_w-v_w)<r_vec,g_z>
       +(p_w d-z_w)<g_p,g_z>+C_p||g_p||^2+C_z||g_z||^2,       (4.2)
    C_p=K-d-d^2+(u s+p_w)/2-(d+1/2)z_w,
    C_z=-K_++kappa K+v_w s/2+(p_w+z_w)/2.

For a check that this retains remote and mixed terms, the gradient parts of
the four sums in E, before multiplication by u,v_w,p_w,z_w, are

    sum h_j Q_(j,p): d<r_vec,g_p>+||r_vec||^2/2
                       +sum h_j(1-h_j)||g_j||^2/2+s||g_p||^2/2;
    sum h_j Q_(j,z): -||r_vec||^2/2
                       +sum h_j(1-h_j)||g_j||^2/2
                       +s||g_z||^2/2-<r_vec,g_z>;
    Q_(z,p): d<g_z,g_p>+<g_z,r_vec>+(||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2)/2;
    Q_(p,z): -(d+1/2)||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2/2
                                       -<g_p,r_vec>-<g_p,g_z>.

Their value parts immediately give (4.1). Adding the history diagonals gives
(4.2). Thus this is an identity on the original space, with no assumed
coincidence of gradients and no quotient-space PSD test.

## 5. The first actual levels already detect the deformation

For every enclosing m>=3, c<3, so0<d_j<A_j at every level. To see this,
A_j>v c^j=d_j/(c-2) and c-2<1. Also

    t_j=d_j/A_j=(c-2)/[1+(1/v-1)(2/c)^j]

is strictly increasing in j. Put t_star=1/lambda. At the first three levels,

    d_0=a, A_0=1; d_1=beta, A_1=2+a;
    d_2=beta c, A_2=4+a(2+c),
    t_0<t_star, t_1<t_star, t_2>t_star.                      (5.1)

The first inequality follows from a<1/lambda. For the second,

    lambda d_1-A_1=epsilon[-lambda+2/(lambda(1+epsilon))]<0.

For the third, use lambda^3=5lambda+2 and expand only the displayed scalar:

    c(lambda d_2-A_2)
      =epsilon[(lambda+4)-(4lambda+2)epsilon
                                      -(5lambda+2)epsilon^2] >0.       (5.2)

The bracket is positive already for0<epsilon<=1/4: use2<lambda<3 to
bound it below by6-14/4-17/16>0. No schedule recurrence or numerical
evaluation was executed to obtain these textual identities.

## 6. Screen1: equal-weight source transport, canonical terminal coefficient

This law preserves the source's four coefficient ratios, substitutes the
actual A,d, and fixes the scalar gluing factor by exact value flow:

    kappa=(3A+d)/(A-d), k=kappa-1=2(A+d)/(A-d),
    u=v_w=p_w=k, z_w=k d,
    K=A(A-1), K_+=T(T-1).                                  (6.1)

All coefficients are nonnegative. At original Silver, d=A/lambda and
kappa=lambda^2, so this is exactly (2.2), not an unrelated seed.
Substitution in (4.2) leaves

    R=Delta(||g_z||^2-||g_p||^2),
    Delta=kappa A^2-T^2=kappa d^2-A^2,
    Delta/A^2=(1+t)(t^2+2t-1)/(1-t), t=d/A.                (6.2)

At the actual first step t=t_0<t_star, Delta<0. The empty child remainders
are zero, so the formal direction g_0=0,g_1=1 gives M_def_1=Delta<0.
The canonical recursive child certificate therefore fails at its first
perturbed step. Its value-flow and signs alone are not a certificate.
Stop this screen here; Screen2 tests the substantive terminal repair.

## 7. Screen2: arbitrary terminal-gradient coefficients do not repair this law

Retain the finite multiplier recursion(6.1), but now allow ANY real
K_0=0,K_1,...,K_m, consistently in every same-depth child. Use K_j in the
child and K_(j+1) in the parent history form. This includes every weakening
of the canonical terminal coefficient, with its objective cost paid by(3.2)
when K_m>=0. No feasibility of an intermediate child is assumed below.

The actual full M_def_j remains diagonal: its child matrices are diagonal and
(4.2) becomes

    R=(K_j+A_j-kappa_j d_j^2)||g_p||^2
       +[-K_(j+1)+kappa_j K_j
                       +(kappa_j-1)(A_j+d_j)/2]||g_z||^2.   (7.1)

Let T_j^end be the actual terminal diagonal of M_def_j and set
E_def_j=T_j^end+K_j, D_def_j=E_def_j-A_j(A_j-1). These are scalar coefficient
identities, not executed recurrences. From(7.1),

    E_def_0=D_def_0=0,
    E_def_(j+1)=kappa_j E_def_j+(kappa_j-1)(A_j+d_j)/2,
    D_def_(j+1)=kappa_j D_def_j+Delta_j,
    actual bridge diagonal = E_def_j+A_j-kappa_j d_j^2
                            =D_def_j-Delta_j.                  (7.2)

Every K_j has canceled from the bridge diagonal. By(5.1) and(6.2),
Delta_0<0, Delta_1<0, Delta_2>0. Therefore

    D_def_2=kappa_1 Delta_0+Delta_1<0,
    (M_def_3)_(3,3)=D_def_2-Delta_2<0.                              (7.3)

This is the ORIGINAL coordinate g_3, the physical bridge of H_3. Thus the
free-terminal-coefficient repair fails on an elementary principal diagonal;
it is not merely a negative modular remainder that a child might absorb.
For orientation, at the first step K_1=E_def_1 makes the terminal diagonal zero
and leaves the other diagonal positive, so the repair really passes that
first failure before encountering(7.3).

Even inflating only the final radius/objective level cannot repair the fixed
full array at the target horizons. Already in H_4 the two H_3 copies have
negative diagonals at g_3 and g_11, equal to a_neg and kappa_3 a_neg,
where a_neg=D_def_2-Delta_2<0. Choose g_3=1,g_11=-1 and every other gradient
zero. Their actual physical steps both equal1+d_2, so the displacement
sum h_i g_i is zero. With x_0=0, g_n=0, the finishing square in(3.1) is
zero for ANY terminal coefficient b_K, whereas M=(1+kappa_3)a_neg<0.
All later enclosing joins leave this initial H_4 direction untouched.
Adding any multiple of ||x_0||^2 also vanishes on it. This is only an
obstruction to the fixed array(6.1), not to a reoptimized full certificate.

## 8. Screen3: unequal right weights preserving the primitive null vector

Return to K=A(A-1), K_+=T(T-1), but permit the two right weights to differ.
This repair changes the actual finite array. A concrete source-calibrated law is

    u=2lambda,
    v_w=[u d+2(A+d)]/A,
    kappa=1+(u+v_w)/2,
    z_w=2A, p_w=4A+2d-u(A-1)
                  =2lambda+2(d-A/lambda).                  (8.1)

It satisfies both value-flow equations(4.1), and at d=A/lambda recovers
u=v_w=p_w=2lambda, z_w=2A and kappa=lambda^2: exactly the source.
All actual coefficients are nonnegative at EVERY level. The weights
u,v_w,z_w,kappa are positive. At j=0, p_w=4+2d_0>0; at j=1,
A_1<lambda implies p_w>2lambda-2>0; for j>=2, monotonicity in(5.1)
gives d_j>A_j/lambda and hence p_w>2lambda.

The full canonical primitive form vanishes on common gradients: choose
all g_i equal and choose the affine-consistent f_i, so all finite Q's and
V_h are zero. Thus every PSD primitive matrix has the all-ones vector e
in its kernel. In(8.1), direct substitution in(4.2) preserves M_def_j e=0
algebraically at every level, even before asserting PSD.

More explicitly, set delta=t_star-d/A, Y=r_vec-s g_z and Z=g_p-g_z.
The entire correction is

    R=delta[(lambda+1)||Y||^2-2lambda A<Y,Z>
                                +A^2(2+t_star+d/A)||Z||^2].  (8.2)

The bracket is positive definite in Y,Z: its leading diagonal is positive
and its determinant divided by A^2 is

    (lambda+1)(2+t_star+d/A)-lambda^2 >= lambda>0.

Thus(5.1) proves PSD of M_def_1 and M_def_2 by the actual embedded child sums.
Both their signs and their full original remainders pass. At level j=2,
choose ALL left-child gradients, including g_p, equal to1, and ALL
right-child gradients, including g_z, equal to0. The left M_def_2 vanishes on
its common-gradient kernel; the right term vanishes on zero. Formula(4.2)
or(8.2) gives the full original value

                 M_def_3=A_2^2-2A_2 d_2-d_2^2<0.               (8.3)

The vector uses g_0=g_1=g_2=g_3=1 and g_4=...=g_7=0. It is a
formal PSD test, not an asserted interpolable primal trajectory. No
gradient equality was imposed in the certificate's domain.

The failure is not a poor choice of the remaining scalar u. In the more
general four-correction template(4.1), suppose the canonical children are
PSD. Full parent PSD would require M_def_parent e=0. The bridge row of R e is
(A+d)(2A-z_w)/2, so z_w=2A. The right aggregate row requires
u d-v_w A+2(A+d)=0. On the same left-common/right-zero vector the full
value is then A^2-2Ad-d^2, independently of u,v_w,kappa,p_w. Thus no
scalar choice in this Huber-kernel-preserving template repairs this level.
This is a short original-coordinate test, not an all-depth impossibility
argument for unrestricted primitive or objective certificates.

For completeness, merely changing the objective level of this fixed full
array also cannot help. Embed the vector w from(8.3) in the first H_3 of
H_m, setting all later coordinates to zero. Every enclosing correction
vanishes on w, because the enclosing bridge and entire right block vanish;
the negative value is unchanged. Its final gradient is zero. The whole
matrix still has M_def_m e=0. Write D=2S_m+1 and
theta=(sum_(i<n_m)h_i w_i)/D. Set g=w-theta e and x_0=0. Then
M_def_m(g)=M_def_m(w)<0 and x_n-A_m g_n=0. The completion(2.5), divided by D,
is negative in this original direction, and any extra coefficient on
||x_0||^2 vanishes. Changing the full multipliers or the potential shape
is outside this screened law.

## 9. Precisely unpaid interface and benchmark loss

The accepted benchmark is the ORIGINAL all-split recurrence of
fact04219554e41d9750, with U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. Fact5fd396eba75651e7 gives

           B_(2^m-1)/(1+2S_m)<237/250,  m>=1536, tau=1/4.   (9.1)

It is a comparison with the Huber LOWER example; it is not a full upper.
A new full certificate with W_(2^m-1)(H_m)<=chi/(1+2S_m), uniformly with
some fixed chi<250/237, would give the fixed relative improvement
1-237chi/250 on this true unbounded sequence. If the new certificate used
the valid generalized-history route(3.1), its exact required inequality is

    V_(H_m,K_m)+sum Lambda^m_ij Q_ij=M_def_m>=0
    on EVERY formal gradient, with every Lambda^m_ij>=0,
    (2S_m+1)/(S_m+(1+sqrt(1+4K_m))/2)<=chi<250/237.           (9.2)

Neither(9.2) nor a more general objective certificate was obtained. A
radius loss must additionally be multiplied into chi. Proving(9.2) is the
substantive all-function inequality, not a technical continuity estimate.

These screens show exactly what the selected source transport cannot pay:
understeps at the first levels create child slack, but the selected endpoint
and right-aggregate corrections leave an explicit original negative direction
when the next bridge exceeds A/lambda. A future continuation must change
coefficients that act on that direction, for example by changing inherited
finite arrays in their enclosing context or by using a genuinely different
full objective completion. Such a change must prove all signs and the entire
PSD remainder; it cannot invoke the primitive history inequality as a premise.

This does not exclude unrestricted nonnegative multipliers, other source
deformations, collective corrections involving left-interior coordinates,
nonprimitive potentials, or a full-class Huber-like upper for H_m. It is not
an actual lower witness against H_m. The original minimax target remains OPEN.

## 10. Retrieval, records, and resources

Primary PDF and HTML files and their extracted source text are under downloads/.
The source text reader is extract_source_text.py, a standard-library HTMLParser
that preserves MathML alttext; it performs no mathematical evaluation.
The three pdftotext attempts returned exit127, `pdftotext: command not found`.
No installation was attempted. Source HTML retrieval and extraction succeeded.
Matlas was called once for the active Silver/joint-potential mechanism; its
generic constant-step hits were not used. Exact recommended primary papers
were then read. Their statements, definitions, and applicable proofs, rather
than search snippets or numerical tables, supplied the source mapping above.

The primary source's recurrence was never executed. No mathematical program,
numerical evaluation, arithmetic replay, solver, benchmark DP, formal gate,
consult, subagent, or installation was used. The algebra above is textual
reasoning. No external theorem or draft in this report has been promoted to a
fact; no unverified component is an accepted predecessor. This complete joint
draft is a finding, with accepted inputs identified in Section1.

Exactly three continuation laws were screened. The one allocated analytical
round is complete; no further allocation or automatic renewal is inferred.
No publication, finalization, revocation, shutdown, or Goal completion action
was taken.

# R6 centered/path-simplex handoff

2026-10-09. **UNVERIFIED. Original target OPEN.** Allocation `0d40dd1ca38f0e52` is finished: one native analytical invocation, two substantive lines, mathematical programs/arrays/solvers/formal submissions zero, no added agent/model. Parent owns budget and registration. Only `PROOF_ATTEMPT.md` and this file were written in this directory.

## Exact objective retained

Prove uniformly over every sufficiently large horizon and every prescribed nonnegative physical word that $W_n(h)\ge(1-o(1))/B_n$, where $W=2R$ and $B_n$ is the exact all-integer-split Zhang--Jiang recurrence, or give a full-class asymptotic separation. Both remain unresolved. No candidate is ready for formal submission.

## Line 1: actual R5 audit and a failed selection rule

The complete R5 centered simplex was rederived: static $F_K$, every old/future projection row, unique minimum 0, original variance radius, and exact terminal value. With $p_i=1/A_i$, $P_i=\sum_{j\ge i}C_j/p_j$, and $\delta_i=d_i-p_iP_{i+1}\ge0$,

\[
 \bar P=S+1-\sum\delta_i,\quad
 2F_n=2\sum\delta_i+(A_M-1),\quad
 R^2=\sum_{i<j}p_ip_j\left(\sum_{k=i}^{j-1}C_k/p_k\right)^2.
\]

All tight guards force $X_n=w_M$. For one checkpoint $1+d$, ordinary masses $C_1-1,C_2-1$, and $r=p_1/p_2\le d/C_2$, the exact payoff is

\[
 [2dr-(2C_2-1)r^2]/C_1^2.
\]

Its optimum is $d^2/[C_1^2(2C_2-1)]$, attained at $r=d/(2C_2-1)$. The tight rule yields $d^2/(C_1^2C_2^2)$; its relative value tends to zero as the tail mass grows. Thus equality-backward selection is quantitatively invalid. This is not a failure theorem for the full centered family or its baseline pool.

## Line 2: an actual neighboring simplex, and the decisive tested scope

For $M$ phases, take $w_1=a_1e_1$, $w_i=-b_ie_{i-1}+a_ie_i$, and $w_M=-b_Me_{M-1}$. Set $Q_i=C_i+d_i$ for $2\le i<M$, $Q_M=L\ge C_M$, and require

\[
 b_2Q_2=d_1a_1,\quad b_{i+1}Q_{i+1}=Q_i a_i,\quad
 C_i a_i(a_i+b_{i+1})\le b_i^2d_i.
\]

The simplex has positive null coefficients proportional to $(d_1,Q_2,\ldots,Q_M)$, spans dimension $M-1$, and has unique minimizer 0 for its static projection envelope. Start $X_0=C_1a_1e_1$. Every phase cancels the preceding coordinate exactly. The full report checks all old, neighboring, distant-future and terminal rows, including all ordinary steps.

The optimum amplitude ratios for fixed partition and $L$ give

\[
 E=\frac{d_1^2}{C_1^2}\frac{2L-2C_M+1}{L^2}
      \prod_{i=2}^{M-1}\frac{d_iQ_{i+1}}{C_i(Q_i+Q_{i+1})}.
\]

This removes the dense full-tail guard and carries no omitted initial-radius or spectral cost. The product losses remain real.

On the exact inherited-$m$ hierarchy $H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j)$, with $\varepsilon=1/(4m)$, $\beta=1-\varepsilon$, $c=(1+\sqrt2)(1+\varepsilon)$, $a=\beta/c$, and $D_m=1+2\sum H_m$, a uniform analytical bound was obtained:

**For every $m\ge1536$, every nonempty subsequence of the rightmost root-to-leaf checkpoints, every number of selected levels, every skipped-level pattern, every $L\ge C_{\rm tail}$, and all admissible amplitudes in this exact-cancellation construction, $E<4/(5D_m)$.**

The proof retains skipped subtrees in the physical masses. Writing $V_j=1+\sum H_j=\alpha c^j+(1-\alpha)2^j$, $\alpha=a/(c-2)$, a level gap $g$ before level $r$ gives phase mass $C\ge c^{g-1}V_r$. Internal nonterminal factors lose at least $1/2$; the optimized last factor is bounded by $7/[10(2V_r-1)]$. The resulting depth factor is at most $[c(c-2)/2]^{k-2}$, and the explicit leading upper bound is $12252303/16000000<4/5$. Single-checkpoint selections are below $3/4$ of Huber.

The parent auxiliary draft was read completely: for this same enclosing-$m$ word, the complete scalar-quadratic pool is at most $9/(16D_m)$. It remains UNVERIFIED. Combined with the supplied accepted comparison $B_{2^m-1}/D_m<47/50$, the specified standard noncrossing Huber witness, all scalar quadratics, and all the above rightmost-spine path selections still give only $1/D_m$. This is the exact baseline-deficient scope: no optimization over every Huber scale, start or crossing itinerary was proved.

## Precise open boundary

No theorem controls all centered-simplex choices or all path-simplex partitions on this word. In particular, checkpoints elsewhere in the recursive tree were not reduced to a rightmost-spine set. No universal arbitrary-word selection is proved. The report must not be summarized as a failure of the full simplex family, a worst-case upper bound, or a negative answer to the original problem.

The bound does rule out automatic renewal of the natural rightmost-spine selection inside this exact-cancellation repair: its full depth-varying family has now been tested analytically and remains below an already insufficient Huber baseline.

This invocation is stopped. No automatic renewal or formal submission is requested; no truth store, service, worker or Goal state was changed.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001402 -->
# R6: centered-simplex selection, slack loss, and a path-simplex test

2026-10-09. **UNVERIFIED analytical draft. Original target OPEN.**

Allocation `0d40dd1ca38f0e52`: one native analytical invocation, two substantive proof lines, mathematical programs/arrays/solvers/formal submissions zero. No additional agent or model was called. The two lines below do not establish the required all-word selection theorem. They also do not give a worst-case upper bound for the full smooth-convex class.

The complete current `OPERATOR.md`, original `PROBLEM.md`, R5 joint-history proof and handoff, and `research/R5_DIRECT_HANDOFF_20261008.md` were read. An initial read at the project-root handoff path failed with `Cannot find path`; the parent supplied the correct research-subdirectory path, which was then read completely. The auxiliary parent draft `research/R6_QUADRATIC_BASELINE_BOUND_20261009.md` was read completely. No worker-local memory or raw numerical array was accessed.

## 1. Fixed target and actual baseline witnesses

Set the smoothness constant to 1. For a prescribed finite nonnegative physical word $h=(h_0,\ldots,h_{n-1})$, let $S=\sum h_t$ and

\[
 W_n(h)=\sup\frac{2(f(x_n)-f_\star)}{\|x_0-x_\star\|^2},
 \qquad x_{t+1}=x_t-h_t\nabla f(x_t).
\]

The supremum is over all finite dimensions, convex differentiable functions with 1-Lipschitz gradient and an attained minimum, and original initial/minimizer pairs. Thus $W=2R$. The exact benchmark is $U_n^{\rm ZJ}=1/B_n$, where $C_0=B_0=1$ and

\[
 C_n=\max_{k+l=n-1}\Omega(C_k,C_l),\qquad
 B_n=\max_{k+l=n-1}G(C_k,B_l),
\]
\[
 \Omega(x,y)=\frac{x+y+\sqrt{x^2+6xy+y^2}}2,
 \qquad G(x,y)=2x+\frac y2+\frac{\sqrt{y^2+8xy}}2.
\]

Every integer split is included. The missing affirmative statement is

\[
 \forall\epsilon>0\ \exists N_\epsilon\ \forall n\ge N_\epsilon\
 \forall h\in[0,\infty)^n:\quad W_n(h)\ge(1-\epsilon)/B_n,
 \tag{1}
\]

with $N_\epsilon$ independent of $h$. No such threshold is produced here.

The standard noncrossing Huber witness and the full scalar-quadratic pool give

\[
 W_n(h)\ge\frac1{2S+1},\qquad
 W_n(h)\ge\lambda\prod_{t=0}^{n-1}(1-\lambda h_t)^2
 \quad(0\le\lambda\le1).
 \tag{2}
\]

For the first, take derivative $\operatorname{clip}(x,-1,1)$, minimum 0, and start $2S+1$; all iterates stay on the positive affine ray, giving normalized value $1/(2S+1)$. For the second, take $f(x)=\lambda x^2/2$, start 1 and minimizer 0. These are lower witnesses, not an upper certificate for $W$. Throughout, “Huber baseline” means exactly this standard noncrossing witness; it does not mean every possible Huber scale, starting point or crossing itinerary.

## 2. Line 1: the actual R5 simplex and a quantitative failure of tight-guard selection

### 2.1 Full construction and projection audit

Partition the complete physical word into $M\ge2$ phases. For $i<M$, phase $i$ contains an ordinary block of mass $C_i-1\ge0$, followed by a checkpoint $1+d_i$, with $d_i>0$. The last block has mass $C_M-1\ge0$. Then

\[
 S=\sum_{i=1}^M C_i+\sum_{i=1}^{M-1}d_i-1.
\]

Choose $A_i>0$ with

\[
 p_i:=A_i^{-1},\quad \sum_i p_i=1,\quad
 P_i=\sum_{j=i}^M A_jC_j,\quad A_i d_i\ge P_{i+1}\quad(i<M).
 \tag{3}
\]

Let $u=(\sqrt{p_i})_i$, $V=u^\perp$, and

\[
 w_i=\sqrt{A_i}e_i-u,\qquad K=\operatorname{conv}\{w_i\}_{i=1}^M.
\]

The inner products are $\langle w_i,w_j\rangle=-1$ for $i\ne j$ and $\|w_i\|^2=A_i-1$. Also $\sum p_iw_i=0$, and the vertices span $V$. Thus 0 is in the relative interior of this simplex. Use the single static function on $V$

\[
 F_K(x)=\max_{g\in K}\{\langle x,g\rangle-\tfrac12\|g\|^2\}.
 \tag{4}
\]

Completing the square gives the unique maximizing gradient $P_Kx$; projection is nonexpansive, so $F_K$ is convex and 1-smooth. Since $K$ contains a relative neighborhood of 0, every nonzero $x\in V$ has positive value in (4) by testing a sufficiently small positive multiple of $x$. Consequently its unique minimizer in $V$ is 0. At a proposed query $x$, a proposed vertex $w_i$ is its gradient exactly when

\[
 \langle x-w_i,w_j-w_i\rangle\le0\quad\hbox{for every }j.
 \tag{5}
\]

Put

\[
 \bar P=\sum_i p_iP_i,\qquad
 X_0=\sum_i\sqrt{p_i}P_i e_i-\bar P u.
\]

Its original squared radius is

\[
 R^2=\sum_i p_iP_i^2-\bar P^2
     =\sum_{i<j}p_ip_j(P_i-P_j)^2>0.
 \tag{6}
\]

The last inequality follows because every $C_i>0$, so the $P_i$ strictly decrease. Prescribe gradient $w_i$ in phase $i$. If $q_j$ is the physical step mass already spent with gradient $w_j$, and $s=\sum q_j$, then exact GD gives

\[
 \langle X,w_j\rangle=P_j-\bar P+s-A_jq_j.
\]

Writing $z_j=P_j-A_jq_j$, the row (5) for $j\ne i$ is $z_j-z_i+A_i\le0$; the self-row is identically zero. Throughout an ordinary block, including the query at its checkpoint, $q_i\le C_i-1$, so $z_i-A_i\ge P_{i+1}\ge0$. Future phases have $z_j=P_j\le P_{i+1}$. Every earlier phase has permanently

\[
 z_j=P_j-A_j(C_j+d_j)=P_{j+1}-A_jd_j\le0.
\]

In the last phase the terminal inequality is $z_M-A_M\ge0$. These statements check all old, future and adjacent rows, including the terminal query. The row against 0 follows from its positive convex representation. No step is deleted, and zero steps inside blocks cause no change to the argument.

The exact terminal numerator and resulting witness are

\[
 N:=2F_K(X_n)=2S+1+A_M-2\bar P,
 \qquad E_{\rm cen}=N/R^2.
 \tag{7}
\]

The R5 construction itself passes this source-bound algebraic audit. It remains an unverified research construction under the project's verification policy.

### 2.2 What the negative centering cancels, and what tight guards lose

Define the nonnegative actual guard slacks

\[
 \delta_i=d_i-p_iP_{i+1}\quad(i<M).
\]

Interchanging finite sums gives

\[
 \bar P=\sum_i C_i+\sum_{i<M}p_iP_{i+1}
       =S+1-\sum_{i<M}\delta_i,
\]
\[
 N=2\sum_{i<M}\delta_i+(A_M-1).
 \tag{8}
\]

Also $P_i-P_j=\sum_{k=i}^{j-1}C_k/p_k$. Hence the variance (6), for fixed $p$, cancels the terminal block mass $C_M$ entirely. This is a genuine cancellation, but $C_M$ still affects feasibility and (8); it cannot be discarded from selection.

If every guard is tight, then $\bar P=S+1$. The terminal supports are $-1$ on every earlier vertex and $A_M-1$ on the last vertex. Since the vertices span $V$, these are exactly the supports of $w_M$, so

\[
 X_n=w_M\qquad\hbox{when all guards are tight}.
 \tag{9}
\]

This restriction has an explicit unbounded quantitative cost already on arbitrary two-block physical words. For $M=2$, let $r=p_1/p_2>0$ and $T=2C_2-1\ge1$. Equation (3) becomes $r\le d_1/C_2$; (6)--(7) become

\[
 R^2=C_1^2/r,\qquad N=2d_1-Tr,
 \qquad E_{\rm cen}(r)=\frac{2d_1r-Tr^2}{C_1^2}.
 \tag{10}
\]

The unconstrained maximizer $r=d_1/T$ is feasible because $T\ge C_2$. Therefore

\[
 \sup E_{\rm cen}=\frac{d_1^2}{C_1^2(2C_2-1)}.
 \tag{11}
\]

The tight choice $r=d_1/C_2$ instead gives

\[
 E_{\rm tight}=\frac{d_1^2}{C_1^2C_2^2},\qquad
 \frac{E_{\rm tight}}{\sup E_{\rm cen}}
       =\frac{2C_2-1}{C_2^2}\longrightarrow0
       \quad(C_2\longrightarrow\infty).
 \tag{12}
\]

These are full physical words: either ordinary block may have any finite length and any nonnegative step sizes with the displayed mass. This rules out treating tight backward selection as a lossless quantitative theorem. It does not rule out the complete centered-simplex family, and it does not say that these particular words defeat either baseline in (2).

I did not obtain a selection of nonzero slacks and a partition for arbitrary words that compares (7) with $1/B_n$. The exact recurrence roots do not follow merely from (8). No unknown child-attainability condition or all-word inequality is assumed here.

## 3. Line 2: a neighboring-simplex repair with an all-depth quantitative test

The dense R5 history guard requires an early checkpoint to pay $\sum_{j>i}A_jC_j$. The following actual simplex replaces that sum by local transfer constraints. It has only neighboring negative inner products, an explicit unique minimum, and a one-coordinate original start. This change does not carry a claimed dominance over R5.

### 3.1 Static geometry and parameter selection for any fixed partition

Use the same $M\ge2$ phases. Let

\[
 Q_i=C_i+d_i\quad(2\le i<M),\qquad Q_M=L\ge C_M.
\]

Here $L$ is a dimensionless construction parameter; the smoothness constant remains 1. Choose $a_1>0$. In $\mathbb R^{M-1}$, use

\[
 w_1=a_1e_1,\quad
 w_i=-b_i e_{i-1}+a_i e_i\ (2\le i<M),\quad
 w_M=-b_Me_{M-1}.
 \tag{13}
\]

Require positive coefficients and

\[
 b_2Q_2=d_1a_1,\qquad
 b_{i+1}Q_{i+1}=Q_i a_i\quad(2\le i<M),
 \tag{14}
\]
\[
 C_i a_i(a_i+b_{i+1})\le b_i^2d_i\quad(2\le i<M).
 \tag{15}
\]

For $M=2$, (14) reads $b_2L=d_1a_1$, and (15) is empty. For larger $M$, an explicit positive solution is

\[
 b_2=d_1a_1/Q_2,\qquad
 a_i=b_i\sqrt{\frac{d_iQ_{i+1}}{C_i(Q_i+Q_{i+1})}},\qquad
 b_{i+1}=Q_i a_i/Q_{i+1}.
 \tag{16}
\]

These choices make (15) equalities. The direction scale $a_1$ cancels from normalized performance.

The positive coefficients proportional to $(d_1,Q_2,\ldots,Q_M)$ annihilate $\sum\theta_iw_i$, by (14). The vectors span $\mathbb R^{M-1}$, because the first $M-1$ vectors form a triangular full-rank system. Their only linear dependence therefore has positive coefficients. They are affinely independent and 0 is an interior point of their simplex $K=\operatorname{conv}\{w_i\}$. Use exactly (4). Its unique minimizer is 0, and its gradient is $P_Kx$, as proved above.

### 3.2 Every physical query and every cross-history row

Start at

\[
 X_0=C_1a_1e_1,\qquad \|X_0\|^2=C_1^2a_1^2.
 \tag{17}
\]

During the first ordinary block, at spent mass $t\in[0,C_1-1]$, the point is $(C_1-t)a_1e_1$. Its residual against $w_1$ is a nonnegative multiple of $e_1$, with nonnegative own support, nonpositive support on $w_2$, and zero support on every other vertex. Thus all rows (5) hold. The checkpoint lands at $-d_1a_1e_1=-b_2Q_2e_1$.

Inductively, phase $i$, $2\le i<M$, starts at $-b_iQ_ie_{i-1}$. At ordinary spent mass $t\in[0,C_i-1]$, its point and own residual support are

\[
 X=-b_i(Q_i-t)e_{i-1}-t a_i e_i,
\]
\[
 H_i(t):=\langle X-w_i,w_i\rangle
   =b_i^2(Q_i-t-1)-(t+1)a_i^2.
\]

The residual support on the next vertex is $(t+1)a_i b_{i+1}$. Condition (15), together with $t+1\le C_i$, gives

\[
 H_i(t)\ge(t+1)a_i b_{i+1}\ge0.
 \tag{18}
\]

The residual support on the immediately preceding vertex is

\[
 -a_{i-1}b_i(Q_i-t-1)\le0,
\]

because $Q_i-t-1\ge d_i>0$. The residual support on every nonneighboring vertex is 0. Subtracting the nonnegative own support $H_i(t)$ checks (5) against every old and future vertex, and against 0. At the checkpoint query the same inequalities apply. The total mass $Q_i=C_i+d_i$ of this phase cancels its old coordinate exactly, leaving

\[
 -Q_i a_i e_i=-b_{i+1}Q_{i+1}e_i.
\]

This completes the induction without an unexamined old coordinate.

In the last phase, $X=-b_M(L-t)e_{M-1}$, $0\le t\le C_M-1$. Its residual against $w_M$ is a nonpositive multiple of $e_{M-1}$. Its own support is $b_M^2(L-t-1)\ge0$, its previous-vertex support is nonpositive, and every other support is 0. Thus all final rows also hold. All physical steps, including zero ordinary steps and arbitrarily large finite checkpoints, are preserved in this one static function.

### 3.3 Exact original-radius payoff

At the terminal query,

\[
 2F_K(X_n)=(2L-2C_M+1)b_M^2.
\]

Using (14)--(16) and (17), the equality choice gives the actual witness

\[
 E_{\rm path}
 =\frac{d_1^2}{C_1^2}\,
   \frac{2L-2C_M+1}{L^2}
   \prod_{i=2}^{M-1}
      \frac{d_iQ_{i+1}}{C_i(Q_i+Q_{i+1})}.
 \tag{19}
\]

An empty product is 1. Choices strictly satisfying (15) only reduce the product for this exact-cancellation construction; (19) is its optimum over the positive local amplitude ratios with the partition and $L$ fixed. This follows directly by telescoping $b_M/a_1=(d_1/L)\prod_{i=2}^{M-1}(a_i/b_i)$, while each ratio has the independent upper bound in (16).

There is no auxiliary initial-radius charge and no separate global curvature inequality: 1-smoothness follows from the static projection representation. The factors in (19) are nevertheless real transfer losses. They cannot be removed when comparing with the original target. For a single checkpoint, maximizing over $L\ge C_2$ gives $L=2C_2-1$ and exactly (11).

### 3.4 A genuine all-depth test on the inherited-parameter hierarchy

The next bound tests a whole family of selections on the actual baseline-deficient word. It does not test every possible partition of that word.

Fix an enclosing integer $m\ge1536$, and keep its parameters fixed at every recursive depth:

\[
 \varepsilon=\frac1{4m},\quad\beta=1-\varepsilon,\quad
 c=(1+\sqrt2)(1+\varepsilon),\quad a=\beta/c.
\]

Let $H_0$ be empty and $H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j)$. Write

\[
 V_j=1+\sum H_j,\quad D_m=2V_m-1,\quad
 \eta=c-2,\quad \alpha=\frac a{c-2}.
\]

Solving the finite mass recurrence gives exactly

\[
 V_j=\alpha c^j+(1-\alpha)2^j.
 \tag{20}
\]

Elementary bounds valid here are

\[
 \frac{12}{5}<c<\frac{49}{20},\quad
 0<\eta<\frac9{20},\quad \frac9{10}<\alpha<1.
 \tag{21}
\]

For the upper bound on $c$, use $1+\sqrt2<29/12$ and $\varepsilon\le1/6144$. For the lower bound on $\alpha=\beta/[c(c-2)]$, use $\beta>999/1000$ and $c(c-2)<441/400$; this ratio exceeds $9/10$. Its upper bound follows from $c(c-2)>1$ and $\beta<1$.

Select any nonempty subsequence of checkpoints on the rightmost recursive root-to-leaf path. Denote their levels by

\[
 m>r_1>r_2>\cdots>r_k\ge0,
 \qquad r_0=m,\quad g_i=r_{i-1}-r_i\ge1.
\]

The checkpoint at level $r_i$ is $1+d_i$, $d_i=a c^{r_i}$. The exact physical phase masses, including every skipped checkpoint and every skipped subtree, are

\[
 C_i=V_{r_{i-1}}-V_{r_i}-d_i\quad(1\le i\le k),
 \qquad C_{k+1}=V_{r_k}.
 \tag{22}
\]

Indeed after the preceding selected checkpoint the remaining right subtree is $H_{r_{i-1}}$; its suffix after the next selected checkpoint is $H_{r_i}$. Formula (22) subtracts precisely that suffix and the selected checkpoint from the physical mass.

We first show

\[
 C_i\ge c^{g_i-1}V_{r_i},\qquad
 \frac{d_i}{C_i}\le\eta c^{-(g_i-1)}.
 \tag{23}
\]

For $g_i=1$, the recurrence $V_{r+1}=2V_r+a c^r$ gives $C_i=V_r$. For $g_i\ge2$, (20) gives $V_{r+g}\ge\alpha c^gV_r$, since $V_r\le c^r$, and $a c^r\le\eta V_r$, since $V_r\ge\alpha c^r$. Therefore

\[
 C_i\ge(\alpha c^{g_i}-1-\eta)V_{r_i}
       \ge c^{g_i-1}V_{r_i}.
\]

The last inequality follows from
$c^{g_i-1}(\alpha c-1)> (12/5)(29/25)>29/20>1+\eta$.
The second inequality in (23) follows immediately.

For a nonterminal transfer, $2\le i<k$, the exact cancellation masses satisfy

\[
 Q_i=C_i+d_i\ge V_{r_i},\qquad
 Q_{i+1}=V_{r_i}-V_{r_{i+1}}<V_{r_i}.
\]

Thus

\[
 \frac{Q_{i+1}}{Q_i+Q_{i+1}}<\frac12.
 \tag{24}
\]

For the final transfer put $V=V_{r_k}$, $T=2V-1$, $Q=Q_k\ge V$. For every $L\ge V$,

\[
 \frac{2L-T}{L(Q+L)}\le\frac7{10T}.
 \tag{25}
\]

To see this, write $x=L/T$, $q=Q/T\ge1/2$. The maximum of $(2x-1)/[x(q+x)]$ on its positive range is
$2/[1+q+\sqrt{1+2q}]$, found by differentiating the displayed rational function. This is at most $2/(3/2+\sqrt2)<7/10$. The restriction $L\ge V$ can only lower the maximum.

Finally, $D_m<2c^m$. If $r\ge1$, then

\[
 2V_r-1>(9/5-5/12)c^r=(83/60)c^r>(4/3)c^r;
\]

for $r=0$, $2V_0-1=1=c^0$. In particular $D_m/T<2c^{m-r_k}$.

For $k\ge2$, substitute (23)--(25) into the full payoff (19), with $M=k+1$. Since $\sum g_i=m-r_k$,

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 D_m E_{\rm path}
 &<\frac75\,
     \frac{\eta^{k+1}c^{k+1-g_1}}{2^{k-2}}\\
 &\le\frac75c^2\eta^3
           \left(\frac{c\eta}{2}\right)^{k-2}
 <\frac45.
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{26}
\]

The last bound uses $c\eta/2<441/800<1$ and

\[
 \frac75\left(\frac{49}{20}\right)^2
             \left(\frac9{20}\right)^3
 =\frac{12252303}{16000000}<\frac45.
\]

For $k=1$, the exact one-checkpoint optimum is (11). If $r_1\ge1$, then

\[
 D_m E_{\rm path}
 <\frac32\eta^2c^{2-g_1}
 \le\frac32c\eta^2
 <\frac{11907}{16000}<\frac34.
 \tag{27}
\]

If $r_1=0$, then $g_1=m\ge4$, and instead

\[
 D_m E_{\rm path}<2\eta^2c^{2-m}
 \le2(9/20)^2/(12/5)^2=9/128<3/4.
\]

Consequently every nonempty subsequence of this rightmost spine, at every depth, every terminal parameter, and every admissible amplitude choice within this exact-cancellation family satisfies

\[
 \boxed{E_{\rm path}<\frac4{5D_m}.}
 \tag{28}
\]

This is a uniform analytical bound on an infinite, depth-varying selection family; the number of selected checkpoints may grow with the enclosing horizon, and skipped levels are included through their exact physical mass. It is not a ceiling for all path-simplex checkpoint partitions.

### 3.5 Why this test is on a word that also defeats both baselines

For exactly the enclosing-$m$ word above, the supplied accepted result `931b9653ce0f7201` states

\[
 \frac{B_{2^m-1}}{D_m}<\frac{47}{50}\qquad(m\ge1536).
 \tag{29}
\]

The parent R6 draft, read completely during this call, gives for this same word

\[
 D_m\sup_{0\le\lambda\le1}\lambda
 \prod_{j=0}^{m-1}[1-\lambda(1+a c^j)]^{2^{m-j}}
 \le\frac9{16}\qquad(m\ge4).
 \tag{30}
\]

Its evidence status is **UNVERIFIED auxiliary draft**, not a newly accepted fact. The exponent $2^{m-j}$ is the squared GD multiplicity, not a single copy of each level. The source proof splits at the first negative factor and controls all frequencies; it is not a sampling assertion. Its key intermediate bound, for first negative level $j\ge1$, is $T=a c^{j+1}\lambda p_j(\lambda)\le2/e<3/4$, followed by $|p_m|\le T^{2^{m-j}}/(z c^{m-j+1})$, $z=a c^j\lambda>a/(1+a)$. These retain the actual enclosing parameters and multiplicities. This call checked that algebra against the source, without opening another research line or submitting it for verification.

Conditionally on the stated auxiliary bound, the specified standard noncrossing Huber witness equals $1/D_m$, the complete scalar-quadratic pool is at most $9/(16D_m)$, and every path witness covered by (28) is smaller than $4/(5D_m)$. Their combined value is therefore exactly $1/D_m< (47/50)/B_{2^m-1}$. This excludes that entire natural recursive selection family as a route to (1) on a hierarchy deficient for this precisely stated baseline pool. No optimization over all Huber scales, starts or crossing itineraries is asserted.

It does not exclude other checkpoint partitions of the path simplex, does not exclude the original centered simplex, and does not upper-bound $W(H_m)$. In particular it provides no negative resolution of the original minimax problem.

## 4. What remains and final status

Line 1 establishes an exact loss of the all-tight backward rule and retains the full centered construction with all its slacks. Line 2 replaces its dense future-history guard by a real locally coupled simplex, with every old/future row and the original radius checked; its exact transfer losses then defeat every selection on the rightmost recursive spine of the genuine inherited-parameter hierarchy.

The unproved issue is still a quantitative selection over **all** physical words and **all** relevant partitions that attains the exact $B_n$ profile, or a different construction that does so. In particular neither a greedy slack rule nor a reduction of arbitrary checkpoint sets to rightmost-spine sets was proved. Such a reduction must not be inferred from (28), and no unknown minimax inequality has been promoted to a lemma.

There is no complete affirmative proof, no uniform $o(1)$, and no full-class asymptotic separation. All mathematical derivations in this report remain UNVERIFIED. Scaling the constructed functions by any prescribed smoothness constant restores the original $L$-smooth class without changing normalized values. The finite dimension is always $M-1$, the minimum is attained and unique for both simplexes, and the actual last iterate and initial radius were used throughout.

This bounded invocation ends with the two requested files. No formal submission, truth-store edit, worker/service action, publication, finalization, revocation, shutdown or Goal mutation occurred. Parent owns the allocation closeout.

# R6: close the arbitrary-word selection theorem for the actual centered simplex

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926. Fresh ONE gpt-6-astra/ultra native analytical invocation, consult OFF, <=TWO substantive proof lines, mathematical programs/arrays/solvers/formal submissions ZERO. Parent owns budget. Read OPERATOR.md, COMPLETE original PROBLEM.md, and R5_DIRECT_HANDOFF_20261008.md. Original Goal ACTIVE/OPEN. Human explicitly prioritizes direct original-target closure inspired by Zhang--Jiang; no further fixed-horizon toy portfolio or equivalence-only deliverable.

The precise goal is lim W_n^*/U_n^ZJ=1, or a rigorous full-class asymptotic separation. W=2R. For a positive resolution, prove for every epsilon>0 and all sufficiently large n, ALL h in[0,infinity)^n satisfy W_n(h)>=(1-epsilon)/B_n with threshold independent of h. Preserve every physical step, arbitrary finite dimension, attained minimum and original radius/last iterate. C0=B0=1, C_n=max Omega(C_k,C_l), B_n=max G(C_k,B_l) over ALL k+l=n-1; Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt(x*x+6*x*y+y*y))/2 and G(x,y)=2*x+y/2+sqrt(y*y+8*x*y)/2.

## Real adversary available, selection unproved

Read the COMPLETE joint_history_closure_20261008_r5/PROOF_ATTEMPT.md, especially its SECOND line, and HANDOFF.md. Check the construction analytically before extending it; it is UNVERIFIED. It uses a static F_K(x)=max_{g in K}(<x,g>-||g||^2/2), so its gradient is projection onto K.

Partition any physical word into m phases. Each i<m has ordinary mass sigma_i followed by checkpoint 1+d_i, d_i>0; C_i=1+sigma_i and C_m=1+tail mass. Choose A_i>0 with sum_i 1/A_i=1, P_i=sum_{j>=i}A_j C_j, and A_i d_i>=P_{i+1}. Put u_i=A_i^(-1/2), w_i=sqrt(A_i)e_i-u in V=u-perp. Their centered simplex has off-diagonal inner product -1 and unique minimizer 0 in V. With barP=sum_i P_i/A_i, X0=sum_i(P_i/sqrt(A_i))e_i-barP*u, the proved draft itinerary gives

  R^2=sum_i P_i^2/A_i-barP^2,
  2F_end=2S+1+A_m-2barP,
  W>=2F_end/R^2,

where S=sum of ALL physical steps. All projection rows are in the R5 proof. Backward existence of A_i is NOT the desired quantitative theorem.

## Your task: actual universal selection, not a parameter reformulation

Choose checkpoints and A_i from an arbitrary full word and PROVE that the resulting payoff, together with actual global Huber and scalar quadratic witnesses, attains the exact asymptotic target. Use the geometry/cancellation of the negative off-diagonal terms to avoid the paid independent-ridge coupling loss. Seek a direct induction tied to Zhang--Jiang's retained inequalities and exact Omega/G roots, or a global combinatorial/probabilistic selection whose losses are genuinely uniform and o(1). A minimax/SDP/Bellman expression for the same missing selection claim is not a solution.

A second substantive line may modify this REAL simplex geometry to remove a specifically proved quantitative loss. It must retain all old/cross projection rows, actual minimizer/radius, and all physical steps. Do not assume any unknown cellwise inequality, rank-one completeness, or arbitrary near-extremal child extension. If the whole family fails, prove the relevant scope on words where the COMBINED baseline pool also fails; checking only the Huber value does not prove that the scalar quadratic pool fails.

To orient asymptotic countertesting without authorizing a numerical scan: the existing genuine word is H0 empty, H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), with fixed enclosing m, eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt2)(1+eps), a=beta/c, n_m=2^m-1, D_m=1+2sum H_m. Accepted 931b9653ce0f7201 gives B_n/D_m<47/50 for m>=1536. It does NOT give a full scalar-quadratic upper or full W upper on this word. Accepted 5fd396eba75651e7 only optimizes two named spiral witnesses. Do not borrow an incompatible parameter limit. No raw numerical arrays may be read/reanalyzed.

## Bounded deliverable

One analytical invocation/<=two substantive proof lines; mathematical programs/solvers/formal submissions0. Text/primary-source reads, manual reasoning, Markdown/hashes only.
