<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001411 -->
# max65: frozen small-tau full-objective discovery

Status before execution: numerical DISCOVERY only, no theorem or formal gate.
Authorization: TASK.md and master_guidance 6a33fa593da4ce7d. Six charged
whole jobs, 30 seconds externally enforced per job, 180 accounted seconds.
Every slot is reserved before numerical imports, construction, solve,
diagnostics, reconstruction, and raw export. Failures consume their slots.
No external service, installation, benchmark DP, subagent, or fact_submit.

## Exact identity check

Shared finding df56220f87e79c22 and its four public C58 raw files use
c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c, hence tau=1, at each enclosing m=3,4,5,6.
The formula is read from each file, not inferred from an H label. Their
hashes and formulas are preserved in historical_identity_inputs.json.
Accepted e9a55f57461f253c uses c=(7/6)lambda and
a=5(sqrt(2)-1)/7, exactly the same old m=6,tau=1 word. It is finite only.

The NEW cases fix tau=1/4 and, separately for each enclosing m,

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta_word=1-1/(4m),
    c=lambda(1+1/(4m)), a=beta_word/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.

All children keep the SAME enclosing (m,tau). Already the first step
1+(1-tau/m)/(lambda(1+tau/m)) differs strictly between tau=1 and tau=1/4:
for positive u<v, (1-u)/(1+u)>(1-v)/(1+v), by cross multiplication.
Thus the historical cases do not exhaust this new allowance.

## Complete original model and signs

Use L=1 and the SPECIFIED minimizer x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Formal vectors
are (x0,g0,...,gn), with full symmetric Gram G of order d=n+2. There is
no chosen-rank, support, primitive-certificate, scalar, or quadratic
restriction. Set x_i=x0-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t and maximize 2*f_n subject to

    G positive semidefinite, G[0,0]=1,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <= 0
      for every distinct ordered i,j in {star,0,...,n}.

All diagonal Q_ii are identically zero. There are (n+2)(n+1) nontrivial
ordered rows. Both terminal directions and both optimum directions are
retained. g_n is an interpolation variable, not an additional GD update.
Accepted 1252b8823bb5a290, proof Sections1--3, proves necessity and full
interpolation sufficiency and the attained equivalence. Its <=1 norm
formulation has an optimizer at equality1, as its proof establishes;
the required equality formulation therefore has the same optimum.

For conditioning only, put M=1+sum h and D=diag(1,1/M,...,1/M).
The variables are Ghat=Gram(x0,M*g0,...,M*gn), fhat=M*f. Set G=D Ghat D,
impose M Q<=0, Ghat[0,0]=1, and maximize2*fhat_n. This invertible
transformation preserves the full space. If A and F are the original
Q coefficient maps, raw pair multipliers are the original lambda,
q=raw_norm_multiplier/M, and C=q E00+A^T lambda is the original dual
quadratic matrix. The checks are F^T lambda=2 e_n, C PSD, lambda>=0,
and M D C D=raw PSD multiplier. The equality multiplier is unrestricted.
The gap reported in original units is q-2f_n. No approximate matrix is
called an upper certificate or a legal exact interpolant.

## Exact one-step calibration, both sides

For h=(3/2), x1=x0-(3/2)g0. With the Q orientation above, expansion gives

 (1/4)||x0||^2-2f1+Q_(0,star)+Q_(1,star)+Q_(1,0)
       = ||x0/2-g0-g1||^2.

All three multipliers are1, all others0, so Q<=0 proves2f1<=1/4
at norm1. For f(x)=x^2/2, x0=1, x1=-1/2, f1=1/8, attaining1/4.
Alternatively the unit-smooth Huber function with threshold1/4 has
g0=g1=1/4, x1=5/8, f1=1/8, also attaining1/4. These are direct symbolic
calibration arguments, not a new fact submission or an imported theorem.

## Frozen execution order

| Slot | Case | Steps n | PSD dimension | Ordered rows | Scalar variables |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | calibration h=(3/2) | 1 | 3 | 6 | 8 |
| 2 | H_(3,1/4) | 7 | 9 | 72 | 53 |
| 3 | H_(4,1/4) | 15 | 17 | 272 | 169 |
| 4 | H_(5,1/4) | 31 | 33 | 1056 | 593 |
| 5 | H_(6,1/4) | 63 | 65 | 4160 | 2209 |
| 6 | independent original-constraint readback of saved n15 | 15 | 17 | 272 | no solve |

Only one solve invocation per solve job. CLARABEL through CVXPY uses
max_iter=120, tol_gap_abs=tol_gap_rel=tol_feas=1e-9, time_limit=20.0,
max_threads=1; all common BLAS thread limits are1. Arithmetic is IEEE
binary64, not nine-digit or arbitrary precision. There are no solver
fallbacks, tolerance retries, or hidden parameter choices. The fixed
20-second internal solve limit reserves import/build/export time within
the independent 30-second external whole-job limit.

Calibration passes only if a complete candidate returns optimal or
optimal_inaccurate, |2f1-1/4|<=1e-6, |q-1/4|<=1e-6, original primal
and dual min eigenvalues>=-1e-7, max Q<=1e-7, |G00-1|<=1e-7,
min lambda>=-1e-7, max flow/stationarity errors<=1e-7, and |gap|<=1e-6.
These are numerical discovery acceptance checks, not proof tolerances.
On calibration failure, discovery closes unless the one final slot is
explicitly used for a necessary correction. No correction is preplanned.
If saved n15 solve/diagnostics fail, its readback slot is retired. No seventh job.

## Frozen data and diagnostics

Each solve saves the complete original/scaled G and values, every row
and raw multiplier, the PSD and norm duals, sparse coefficient maps,
original/full dual matrices, complete residual, flow, complementarity
and stationarity arrays, spectra, status, iterations, settings, version,
precision, and nested timing records. Raw arrays are written before
additional diagnostics. No eigenvalue is clipped and no Gram is repaired.
Only if the original Gram has strictly positive numerical minimum
eigenvalue is Cholesky reconstruction attempted. Saved Euclidean points
use the chronological physical GD recursion. Reconstruction reports
every original Q again and the exact floating-point residual differences,
Gram error, norm difference, and objective/radius change.

Structural diagnostics use absolute |Q|<=1e-7 for the numerical active
graph, lambda>1e-6 for dual support, and gradient eigenvalue ratios
1e-6 and1e-8 for heuristic ranks. Save all old function values, terminal
incoming/outgoing rows, all cross-block gradient inner products and
normalized correlations, and their extrema. These thresholds define
descriptive numerical summaries only.

Compare on this SAME h with Huber K=1/(1+2 sum h), unit-quadratic
product_(t<n)(1-h_t)^2, and the original B_n. Benchmark inputs are only
the saved exact lower/upper numerators divided by D80 from accepted
04219554e41d9750 Section8. The source CSV SHA256 is
2a7585bc0bade9f85461456c13d60bbe618f85c7cc816e1ddc5b4c1a1d611e68.
The five selected rows are copied verbatim to saved_benchmark_rows.json.
No DP or interval checker is run. B_lo<=B_n<=B_hi implies
1/B_hi<=U_n<=1/B_lo and, for any nonnegative value v,
B_lo*v<=B_n*v<=B_hi*v. Displayed binary64 ratios only approximate these
algebraic endpoint products. Raw numerical primal/dual values are not
certified bounds on W. Analytic Huber and quadratic formulas are genuine
lower witness families; their displayed evaluations are floating point.

The independent n15 readback imports no solver and does not reuse A/F,
saved residuals, or the discovery model's Q implementation. It builds
chronological coefficient vectors from saved h, evaluates each directed
inner product directly from the saved original G, checks PSD, norm,
every physical update, and2f_n, and compares against saved records.
It also regenerates the same algebraic word in binary64 within its slot.

## Resource and scope discipline

run_job.py makes an atomic ledger reservation and then launches a fresh
child process with an external30-second timeout. The timeout includes
imports, all mathematics and raw export. The wrapper performs only
accounting and reading saved results. Build, API solve, solver-native,
child, and wrapper elapsed times are recorded separately; nested times
are NEVER added. Guard1131->1311 is a reservation, not measured runtime.
All files are in this absolute public directory, except the canonical
ledger and the explicitly required local memory append records.

After closure, no residual/eigenvalue/objective evaluation, reconstruction,
alternate profile, or benchmark work is permitted. Only reading saved
outputs, symbolic reasoning, text/hashes and administrative reporting.
These m<=6 cases do not reach the m>=1536 small-tau obstruction domain.
No asymptotic extrapolation, contradiction, or minimax conclusion follows.

# max66: a complete-prefix objective multiplier law and its exact scoped failure

Status: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. No formal submission,
no new fact, and no unrestricted objective upper bound are asserted.
The original exact-profile minimax problem remains OPEN.

Outcome of this draft: on the unchanged tau=1/4 words, the symmetric
kernel fails the full PSD test for every m>=3. Its sole backward-tilt
repair, including every nonnegative parameter choice in the displayed
family, fails at the Huber level for every m>=4. In fact any PSD level
within this same law would have to exceed 1/100, so this law cannot even
provide a vanishing objective bound on that sequence. These are scoped,
unverified statements about a proposed certificate family, not W itself.

One mechanism is tested: a product kernel on every directed queried-prefix
pair, completed by explicit minimizer and terminal multipliers. The base
kernel is symmetric. The ONLY repair increases every backward multiplier
by a second product kernel. Both the concrete positive rule and the whole
nonnegative two-parameter family are addressed. This is a whole-word
objective calculation, not a primitive-capacity assumption or a modular
boundary inequality.

## 1. Exact object, accepted inputs, and notation

For a fixed enclosing integer m>=1, put

 lambda_s=1+sqrt(2), beta_word=1-1/(4m),
 c=lambda_s(1+1/(4m)), a=beta_word/c,
 H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
 h=H_m, n=2^m-1.

Every child in these definitions uses this SAME enclosing m. In particular,
the left word H_(m-1) is not the standalone word with enclosing m-1.
The letter n always denotes the number of actual physical updates. The
terminal gradient is an interpolation variable and supplies no extra update.

The accepted mathematical inputs are:

* fact1252b8823bb5a290: original smooth-convex interpolation, the full
  formal-vector objective certificate convention, and the existence of a
  selected certificate without incoming terminal rows. Its complete proof
  was read. No queried-prefix row deletion follows from that theorem.
* fact04219554e41d9750: the ORIGINAL all-split benchmark recurrence and its
  stated denominator enclosures. Only its unconditional benchmark is used
  in Section 9. No conditional seed substitution or new composition is used.

Normalize L=1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0. The independent formal vectors are

                       x_0,g_0,...,g_n.

Define x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i) h_t g_t, and use the accepted convention

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2, i!=j.

All original directed pairs, including the minimizer and terminal, remain
part of the model. A zero multiplier is an explicit choice for this candidate,
not a claim that its row is generally redundant. The certificate polynomial is

 M_cert=q_cert||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j) lambda_ij Q_ij.       (1)

If every multiplier is nonnegative, all value coefficients cancel, and
M_cert is PSD on this entire space, then W_n(h)<=q_cert. This is the
W=2R normalization with the original specified-minimizer distance. The
contrapositive about a PARTICULAR polynomial is not a lower bound on W.

To avoid collisions with earlier project notation, the new objects below
have suffix66. For an arbitrary nonnegative word of length n, let

 S66=sum_(i<n)h_i, D66=1+2S66,
 p66_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, 0<=i<=n,
 T66=sum_(i<n)h_i*p66_i,
 d66_i=sum_(j<i)h_j*(p66_i-p66_j), 0<=i<n.                 (2)

T66=(S66^2-sum h_i^2)/2. Indeed, both sides sum h_i*h_j over j<i.
Thus T66>0 exactly when at least two steps are positive. Sums below are
finite mathematical formulas; none was evaluated by a program.

## 2. One explicit complete-prefix kernel, with its sole repair

Suppose first that S66>0. For real u66,v66>=0, put, for distinct i,j<n,

 K66_ij=h_i*h_j*(u66+v66*1_(i>j)), K66_ii=0,
 r66_i=sum_(j<n)K66_ij-sum_(j<n)K66_ji
       =v66*h_i*(2p66_i+h_i-S66),
 mu66_i=h_i*[2+u66*(S66*p66_i-T66)+v66*d66_i]
              /(2S66-p66_i),
 ell66_i=mu66_i+r66_i, 0<=i<n,
 mu66_n=2-sum_(i<n)mu66_i.                               (3)

The denominator 2S66-p66_i is at least S66>0, including at zero steps.
Specify EVERY original multiplier as follows:

 lambda_ij=K66_ij for distinct i,j<n;
 lambda_(i,star)=mu66_i for 0<=i<=n;
 lambda_(n,i)=ell66_i for 0<=i<n;
 lambda_(star,i)=0 for 0<=i<=n;
 lambda_(i,n)=0 for 0<=i<n;
 lambda_ii=0.                                           (4)

The base is v66=0. The sole repair is v66>0, which retains every forward
prefix pair and adds mass on every backward prefix pair. These are dense
arrays, not thresholded supports extracted from numerical data. The
choice of no incoming terminal rows is compatible with accepted1252, but
the product form (3) is a NEW RESTRICTION of this candidate only.

Here is a completely explicit, strictly sign-valid choice, so (3) is not
just a feasible-set restatement. If T66>0, let b66 be the smallest positive
step and set

 u66=1/T66,
 mu66_i^0=h_i*(1+S66*p66_i/T66)/(2S66-p66_i),
 mu66_n^0=2-sum_(i<n)mu66_i^0,
 J66=sum_(i<n)h_i*d66_i/(2S66-p66_i),
 v66=min{1/[4S66(S66-b66)], mu66_n^0/(2J66)}.             (5)

The base candidate uses the same u66 and v66=0. Formula (5), without
numerical fitting or optimization, is the one repaired candidate.

All divisions and signs in (5) are valid. For h_i>0, p66_i<S66 and
mu66_i^0>0. Directly from (2),

 sum_(i<n)(2S66-p66_i)*mu66_i^0
    =sum_(i<n)h_i*(1+S66*p66_i/T66)=2S66.                (6)

Because every positive term has 2S66-p66_i>S66, (6) gives
sum mu66_i^0<2, so mu66_n^0>0. At the second positive step d66_i>0,
which gives J66>0. Two positive steps also give S66-b66>0. Consequently
v66>0. Moreover

 mu66_i=mu66_i^0+v66*h_i*d66_i/(2S66-p66_i)>=0,
 mu66_n=mu66_n^0-v66*J66>=mu66_n^0/2>0.                 (7)

For a positive h_i, the definition of mu66_i^0 gives
mu66_i/h_i>=1/(2S66). Since h_i>=b66 and p66_i>=0,

 ell66_i/h_i>=1/(2S66)-v66*(S66-b66)>=1/(4S66)>0.       (8)

At h_i=0, mu66_i=ell66_i=0 and every K66 edge incident to i is zero.
All K66 edges between positive steps are strictly positive in BOTH
directions. Thus every coefficient of (4), including the terminal and
minimizer coefficients, has its stated exact sign. The unrepaired v66=0
choice is also nonnegative, with ell66_i=mu66_i^0 and mu66_n=mu66_n^0.

## 3. Complete flow cancellation and the exact Huber-null equations

For i<n, the full outgoing-minus-incoming flow of (4) is

 mu66_i+sum_j K66_ij-sum_j K66_ji-ell66_i=0.             (9)

Every finite prefix edge contributes once to each of its row/column sums,
so sum r66_i=0. At n the outgoing flow is

 mu66_n+sum_(i<n)ell66_i=2,                             (10)

and no edge enters n. At star, the outgoing flow is zero and the incoming
flow is sum_(i<=n)mu66_i=2. Therefore (1) has zero coefficient on every
formal f_i, including f_n. This is the full original value identity.

The finite-prefix drift of the proposed kernel is exactly

 sum_(j<n)K66_ij*(p66_i-p66_j)
  =u66*h_i*(S66*p66_i-T66)+v66*h_i*d66_i.                (11)

Equations (3) and (11) give

 (2S66-p66_i)*mu66_i
      =2h_i+sum_(j<n)K66_ij*(p66_i-p66_j), i<n.         (12)

We also check the terminal equation rather than assuming it. Multiply
r66_i by p66_i and sum to obtain

 sum_i p66_i*r66_i=sum_(i,j<n)K66_ij*(p66_i-p66_j).

Summing (12) consequently yields

 sum_i(2S66-p66_i)*mu66_i=2S66+sum_i p66_i*r66_i.

Using sum r66_i=0 and ell66_i=mu66_i+r66_i then gives

 sum_(i<n)ell66_i*(S66-p66_i)=S66*mu66_n.               (13)

Together (12)--(13) are all the finite-node Huber-null balance equations.
They were derived here; the unverified finding0fda is provenance, not a
premise. The equations by themselves DO NOT imply PSD.

## 4. Full original matrix, including all cross-block entries

Let C66 be the symmetric (n+1)-gradient coefficient matrix of (1). The
complete full matrix is

              M66(q_cert)=[[q_cert,-mu66^T/2],
                           [-mu66/2,C66]],             (14)

where mu66=(mu66_0,...,mu66_n). This is a matrix on the original n+2
coordinates; it is not a matrix restricted to realizable trajectories.

For clarity we derive its entries from ALL original rows. A term
lambda_ij*||g_i-g_j||^2/2 contributes half the total incident mass to
the diagonal and minus lambda_ij to the corresponding cross coefficient.
Only a forward edge out of i contributes -h_i*lambda_ij to ||g_i||^2
through <g_i,x_j-x_i>. By (9)--(10) the half incident mass is
out_i-1_(i=n). Thus, with h_n=0,

 C66_ii=out_i-1_(i=n)-h_i*sum_(j>i)lambda_ij.            (15)

For 0<=i<j<=n, the difference-square terms contribute
-(lambda_ij+lambda_ji). A term based at i with destination after j
contributes -h_j*lambda_ik. A term based at j with destination at or
before i, or at star, contributes h_i times its multiplier. Therefore

 2C66_ij=-(lambda_ij+lambda_ji)
          -h_j*sum_(k>j)lambda_ik
          +h_i*(mu66_j+sum_(k<=i)lambda_jk).             (16)

All sums in (15)--(16) except the explicitly separated star term are
over finite sample indices. Substituting (3)--(4) gives every entry:

 C66_ii=mu66_i+h_i*[u66*(S66-h_i)+v66*p66_i]
                 -u66*h_i^2*(S66-p66_i-h_i), i<n;
 C66_nn=1;                                            (17)

 2C66_ij=h_i*mu66_j+h_i*h_j*[-(2u66+v66)
                 -u66*(S66-p66_j-h_j)
                 +(u66+v66)*(p66_i+h_i)], i<j<n;       (18)

 2C66_(i,n)=-ell66_i
             +h_i*(mu66_n+sum_(t<=i)ell66_t), i<n.     (19)

These expressions include every left/right cross-block coefficient and
every terminal gradient coordinate. No product of child remainders or
independent-gradient assumption has replaced them. The candidate is formed
on the whole parent word; no child slack was assumed zero or discarded.

Here is also a coordinate-free expansion checking the same whole matrix.
Write z_i=-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t, so x_i=x_0+z_i and z_star=0. The value-free
polynomial is exactly

 q_cert||x_0||^2-sum_(i<=n)mu66_i*<g_i,x_0>
 -sum_(i<=n)mu66_i*<g_i,z_i>+sum_(i<=n)mu66_i*||g_i||^2/2
 +sum_(i<n)ell66_i*[<g_n,z_i-z_n>+||g_n-g_i||^2/2]
 +sum_(i!=j<n)K66_ij*[<g_i,z_j-z_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2].   (20)

In particular, both the entries and their linking to the full matrix are
exact polynomial identities; no numerical matrix is substituted.

Let e66 be the all-ones vector of length n+1. Multiplying the gradient
part of (20) by e66 gives the row identity

 2C66*e66=2h+mu66+drift66,
 drift66_i=p66_i*mu66_i
                +sum_(j finite)lambda_ij*(p66_i-p66_j). (21)

For a direct verification, the squared differences contribute mu66/2
to C66*e66. The bilinear position terms contribute half their row drift
and half the vector sum of their displacements. The latter sum is
-2z_n=2sum h_t*g_t, because full finite flow is 2 at n and all star
outgoing multipliers vanish. This gives (21) on every coordinate.
Equations (12)--(13) now imply

              C66*e66=D66*mu66/2.                     (22)

Consequently M66(1/D66)*(1,e66/D66)=0 exactly. More generally, the
Schur completion in the initial-point coordinate is

 M_cert=q_cert*||x_0-sum_i mu66_i*g_i/(2q_cert)||^2
      +sum_(i,j)[C66_ij-mu66_i*mu66_j/(4q_cert)]<g_i,g_j>,
                                                        (23)

when q_cert>0. At q_cert=1/D66 this Schur remainder annihilates e66,
but will have a strictly negative diagonal in Section 7.

The accepted terminal-square parameterization is respected as well:
alpha_star=mu66_n/2, alpha_i=ell66_i/2. Equations (9)--(10) show that
these sum to one, and their prefix flow is 2alpha_i. Hence the FULL
identity from fact1252 applies with exactly these coefficients, without
any deleted prefix direction. Nothing here assumes its remainder PSD.

## 5. Exact geometry of the unchanged hierarchy

For m>=3, let

 k66=2^(m-1)-1, r66= h_(k66)=1+a*c^(m-1),
 A66=sum H_(m-1), Jleft66=sum_(i<k66)h_i^2,
 E66=A66*r66-Jleft66, Kroot66=3A66+2r66.                (24)

r66 is the physical root step, not r66_i of (3). The whole word is
palindromic, with left/right sums A66, so

 S66=2A66+r66, p66_(k66)=A66,
 T66=2A66^2+2A66*r66-Jleft66,
 d66_(k66)=(A66^2+Jleft66)/2,
 S66*A66-T66=-E66,
 T66-E66=A66*S66.                                     (25)

The identity for d66_(k66) follows by writing
sum_(i<k66)h_i*p66_i=(A66^2-Jleft66)/2 within the actual left block.
Every child step is strictly smaller than r66, because c>1 and it has a
strictly smaller level. Thus 0<Jleft66<A66*r66 and E66>0.

We will need A66>=r66. This is proved inside each enclosing m. Let
s_j=sum H_j and t_j=1+a*c^j. Then

 s_(j+1)=2s_j+t_j, t_(j+1)=c*t_j-(c-1).

For m>=3, 2<c<3 and 0<beta_word<1. Since

 s_2=3+2a+beta_word, t_2=1+beta_word*c,
 s_2-t_2=2+beta_word*(1+2/c-c)>0,                       (26)

the base s_2>t_2 holds. To see the sign without a decimal, c<3 gives
1+2/c-c>-2; multiplication by beta_word in (0,1) keeps the product
strictly above -2, whether that parenthesis is positive or negative.
If s_j>=t_j, then s_(j+1)>=3t_j>c*t_j-(c-1)=t_(j+1).
Induction through j=m-1 gives A66=s_(m-1)>t_(m-1)=r66.

Here c<3 follows from c<=13lambda_s/12 and lambda_s<5/2; the latter is
sqrt(2)<3/2. Also beta_word>=11/12 and lambda_s>12/5, so r66>3 for
m>=3. For m>=4 the simpler estimate

 r66=1+beta_word*c^(m-2)
       >=1+(3/4)*lambda_s^(m-2)>4                     (27)

holds. These are symbolic inductions/inequalities, not executed recurrences.
The first step h_0=1+a is a smallest positive step and is at most A66.

## 6. First-query nonnegativity constrains the entire repaired family

We now allow ANY u66,v66>=0 in (3), not just the explicit choice (5).
Suppose all of (4) are nonnegative. Only one necessary sign, ell66_0>=0,
already imposes a strong condition. Put b=h_0>0 for this section. Since
p66_0=d66_0=0,

 ell66_0=b*[(2-u66*T66)/(2S66)-v66*(S66-b)]>=0.

Therefore

 u66*T66+2S66*v66*(S66-b)<=2,
 u66<=2/T66, v66<=1/[S66(S66-b)].                      (28)

If (28) is violated, an original terminal multiplier is negative, which
already disqualifies that parameter choice. The remaining argument grants
every other sign and still excludes PSD in the stated range.

At the root, (3), (17), and (25) give exact scalar coefficients

 mu66_(k66)=r66*[2-u66*E66+v66*d66_(k66)]/Kroot66,
 C66_(k66,k66)=mu66_(k66)
                   +r66*A66*[u66*(2-r66)+v66].        (29)

Let mu_r denote mu66_(k66) only in the rest of this proof. Because E66>0,
d66_(k66)>0, and (28) holds, (25) gives

 mu_r>=2r66*(1-E66/T66)/Kroot66
      =2r66*A66*S66/(Kroot66*T66)
      >=r66*S66/[Kroot66*(A66+r66)]>0.                (30)

The last inequality uses T66<=2A66*(A66+r66), since Jleft66>=0.

For r66>2, the u66 term in the root diagonal of (29) is nonpositive.
Using (28), (30), and b<=A66 gives

 [v66*r66*A66]/mu_r
 <= A66*Kroot66*(A66+r66)/[S66^2*(S66-b)]
 <= A66*Kroot66/S66^2
 < 3/4.                                               (31)

The last strict sign is exactly

 3S66^2-4A66*Kroot66=4A66*r66+3r66^2>0.

Thus the COMPLETE original root gradient diagonal satisfies

                 C66_(k66,k66)<(7/4)*mu_r.             (32)

On the other hand, A66>=r66 implies

 Kroot66*(A66+r66)<=(10/9)*S66^2,                       (33)

because 10S66^2-9Kroot66*(A66+r66)
=(A66-r66)*(13A66+8r66)>=0. From D66>2S66, (30), and (33),

                     D66*mu_r>9r66/5.                (34)

No step in (28)--(34) assumes PSD or a certificate on an isolated child.
These are necessary constraints for the explicitly defined WHOLE-WORD
kernel with exact nonnegative original multipliers.

## 7. A negative direction in the entire original space

Let q_cert=chi66/D66 with chi66>0. On the two ORIGINAL formal coordinates
(x_0,g_(k66)), the restriction of (14) is precisely

        [[q_cert,-mu_r/2],[-mu_r/2,C66_(k66,k66)]].     (35)

To link this to the full space, take an arbitrary unit Euclidean vector e
and assign

 g_(k66)=e, g_i=0 for every other i including i=n,
 x_0=mu_r*e/(2q_cert).                                (36)

This is an explicit linear embedding of the two-coordinate space into the
original n+2 coordinates. All positions still use their original GD
substitution. Evaluating (20), or completing the square in (35), gives

 M_cert=C66_(k66,k66)-mu_r^2/(4q_cert)
       < (mu_r/4)*[7-9r66/(5chi66)].                  (37)

Hence the full matrix is NOT PSD whenever chi66<=9r66/35. In particular,
for EVERY enclosing m>=4, (27) gives 9r66/35>36/35>1, so the exact Huber
level chi66=1 fails. Its explicit negative value obeys

                    M_cert<-mu_r/20<0.                (38)

This failure applies to the strictly positive rule (5), the symmetric
base, and every other nonnegative u66,v66 completion in this family.
If some other coefficient fails its sign first, that choice is already
excluded before the PSD test. The conclusion is not based on a numerical
eigenvalue or a removed block.

For the base v66=0, (29) gives the stronger bound C66_(k66,k66)<=mu_r.
Equations (34)--(36) therefore exclude the exact Huber level already at
EVERY m>=3, since 9r66/5>27/5>4. The repaired-family assertion above
starts at m=4; no assertion of its PSD feasibility or failure at m=2,3
is required or inferred.

Assignments (36) are formal quadratic-form tests. They are NOT claimed
to be interpolation-feasible transcripts or actual convex functions.
Their role is legitimate because a full dual remainder must be PSD on
ALL original formal vectors. They do not give a lower witness for W.

Any PSD member at a positive level must instead have

                     chi66>9r66/35.                  (39)

If q_cert<=0, the nonzero off-diagonal -mu_r/2 in (35) makes PSD
impossible too. Thus no nonpositive-level exception is being omitted.
Since r66 tends to infinity by (27), (39) excludes every bounded
multiplicative loss relative to 1/D66 within this same multiplier law.
Changing u66,v66 with m or with the chosen level does not evade (28)--(39).
Changing the formulas for mu66 or terminal weights is outside this law.

## 8. Zero physical steps and the small degenerate cases

The target words themselves have every h_i>1. No physical bridge vanishes
at any claimed m. Nevertheless the coefficient definitions have a complete
zero-step interpretation, which is useful for detecting hidden coordinate
identifications.

If S66>0 and T66>0, formulas (2)--(5) apply even with zero steps. Every
zero-step index i<n has h_i=mu66_i=ell66_i=0 and no incident K66 edge.
Equations (17)--(19) make its entire gradient row and column zero.
For nonzero indices, the same formulas depend only on their actual
chronological prefix masses. Thus, after selecting the original indices
of positive steps and the ORIGINAL terminal n, the full matrix is exactly
T_pick^T*Mhat66*T_pick, where T_pick is the coordinate selection map and
Mhat66 uses the compressed positive word. This is a full-space congruence
with zero omitted columns. No equality between formal duplicate gradients
is imposed. This is also the explicit lifting convention in accepted1252.

If S66=0, including n=0, set only lambda_(n,star)=2 and q_cert=1.
Then M_cert=||x_0-g_n||^2 on the entire original space. Every unused
gradient coordinate is a zero column.

If S66>0 but T66=0, exactly one physical step is positive, say h_i=b.
Use u66=v66=0 in (3). The only nonzero multipliers are
lambda_(i,star)=lambda_(n,star)=lambda_(n,i)=1. With D66=2b+1,
the full completion is

 M_cert=(1/D66)||x_0-(D66/2)(g_i+g_n)||^2
                     +(3-2b)||g_i-g_n||^2/4.          (40)

All other original coordinates are free zero columns. Formula (40) is
PSD at the Huber level exactly when 0<b<=3/2; for b>3/2 its difference
direction is negative. This finite calibration is not the claimed
unbounded bridge. For m=1 the target b=1+3/(5lambda_s)<3/2, so the
one-step calibration succeeds. The formula (5) is never divided by zero
in these degenerate cases.

## 9. A stronger limitation on this law, and benchmark orientation

The necessary loss in (39) is not merely a small excess above one.
For m>=4, a=(4m-1)/[(4m+1)lambda_s]>=15/(17lambda_s)>1/3,
because lambda_s<5/2 and 6/17>1/3. For j=m-1, counting the actual
occurrences of each hierarchical step gives

 A66=2^j-1+a*(c^j-2^j)/(c-2).                          (41)

This identity also follows by substitution into s_(j+1)=2s_j+1+a*c^j,
with s_0=0; it is not an evaluated recurrence. Since c>lambda_s>2,
1/(c-2)<=1/(lambda_s-2)=lambda_s<5/2 and 2^j<=c^j. Hence

 A66< c^j+a*c^j/(c-2)
       =a*c^j*[1/a+1/(c-2)]<(11/2)*r66.

It follows that S66<12r66 and, because r66>4,

                       D66<97r66/4.                  (42)

Combining (39) with (42), any member of this law that is actually PSD
at any level necessarily has

 q_cert=chi66/D66>9r66/(35D66)>36/3395>1/100.           (43)

Equation (43) is a LOWER RESTRICTION ON CERTIFICATE LEVELS IN THIS FAMILY.
It is NOT a lower bound on W_n(h), and does not assert that a feasible
member at such a larger level exists.

For completeness, the original benchmark in fact042 is
B_0=C_0=1 and B_n=max_(k+l=n-1)G(C_k,B_l), where
G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2. Its split k=0 gives
B_n>=G(1,B_(n-1))>=B_(n-1)+2. Induction yields B_n>=2n+1.
Therefore a feasible upper level from this law would obey

 q_cert/U_n^ZJ=B_n*q_cert>(2n+1)/100 -> infinity
                                      for n=2^m-1.    (44)

In particular it cannot establish a fixed relative improvement below
the original U_n^ZJ at unbounded true horizons. No new benchmark DP or
asymptotic numerical value has been used. There is no actual full-class
upper estimate to combine with the newly accepted stagewise-family
comparison, and the NEGATIVE alternative of PROBLEM.md is not proved.

# Frozen sparse-cycle pilot, max4 round54

Status: numerical discovery only, no formal gate. Original theorem OPEN.
Authority: TASK_frozen.md, master_guidance4476b1d7b1881436, and the separate
five-job/30-accounted-second public ledger. No old allocation is reopened.
The preceding own worker status has last_rc=0 at 2026-10-01 05:55:38 UTC,
after the round53 handoff; its raw snapshot is archived here. This fresh run
started later. These observations, not the handoff alone, establish prior exit.

## Immutable mathematical law

The TASK-pinned evidence81c766c8e8e55ca5 is recovered byte-for-byte as
frozen_model_81c766c8e8e55ca5.md, SHA256
44f2f496e044fa3a6ca43406570597c66eeeb0e8f12649fea5a58f4a4471bad9.
The live manuscript has later exposition-only hash a59effa1d6ff0c1ef15ced41f7eedc4b6fbc9028f5bcd0f5eab1cf5740906a0d;
the frozen evidence is used for this pilot. Its assertions remain unverified.

For m in {8,10,12,14}, lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/m,
c=lambda(1+1/m), t_j=1+(beta/c)c^j, j=0,...,m-1,
d=t_(m-1)=1+beta*c^(m-2), N=ceil(pi sqrt(d/6)),
theta=pi/(2N), mu=sin(theta)^2, z=cos(theta),
v_i=cos((2i+1)theta), i=0,...,N-1.
F(x)=sum_i(x_i-x_(i+1 mod N))_+^2/8, x_0=v, L=1,
H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,t_j,H_j), n=2^m-1. Every child uses the
same enclosing m. No change of graph, weights, starting law, or smoothness.

## Calibration identities rederived from definitions

Let y_i=x_i-x_(i+1), r_i=max(y_i,0). Differentiating each summand gives
g_i=(r_i-r_(i-1))/4. Subtracting consecutive vertex updates gives
y'_i=y_i-(h/4)(2r_i-r_(i-1)-r_(i+1)). This proves both implementations
represent the same exact GD. The gradient sum and the edge sum telescope.
Conversely, set C_0=0, C_i=sum_(j<i)y_j. A mean-zero edge inverse is
x_i=(sum_j C_j)/N-C_i; an edge-sum drift remains visible at the closing
edge and is NEVER projected away from the independently evolved edge state.

Pair v_i with v_(N-1-i) to obtain mean zero. Using cos^2 u=(1+cos2u)/2
and summing the finite geometric series gives R^2=sum v_i^2=N/2.
F vanishes exactly on constants because the cycle inequalities x_i<=x_(i+1)
force equality. Hence this is the true distance to the minimum set.
For i<N-1, (Dv)_i=2sin((i+1)pi/N)sin(theta)>0; (Dv)_(N-1)=-2z.
The cosine addition formula, including the endpoint with one active edge,
then gives g(v)=mu v. While the amplitude is positive, g(qv)=q mu v.
For each registered m the job checks every first-child factor 1-mu*t_j>0
and mu*d>1; it never assumes these inequalities from the draft.
It computes the actual first child by all physical updates and compares it to
A v, where A=product_(j=0)^(m-2)(1-mu*t_j)^(2^(m-2-j)).
It also checks the running physical scalar product, not just the final product.

At k0=2^(m-1)-1 the expected state is A v; at k0+1 it is -b v,
b=A(mu*d-1). Only the closing edge is positive there, with value2bz,
so F_mid=b^2 z^2/2. For the next two transitions divide by b ONLY in
the diagnostic formula, never in an evolved state. Put u=t_0/4,w=t_1/4.
From X=-v the first update changes X_0 by+2uz and X_(N-1) by-2uz.
The resulting closing edge is q=2z(1-2u); the adjacent edges are
p=z(2u-4mu), using cos3theta=z(1-4mu). Others retain their negative
values. In the next update, the changes at vertices0,1,N-2,N-1 are
w(q-p), wp, -wp, -w(q-p). The closing edge becomes(1-2w)q+2wp;
the adjacent edges become(1-2w)p+wq; the next edges become
wp-8mu*z*(1-2mu), by cos3theta-cos5theta=8mu*z*(1-2mu).
All claimed positive values and expected active-edge counts are checked.

These are a joint elementary diagnostic derivation, not a new verified fact.
The obtain-immediate-conclusions skill is used for this normalization;
query-memory recovered prior shortcut failures. A fresh Matlas query found
only inapplicable continuous-flow statements; primary arXiv1804.03000v2
confirms background terminology already audited in round53. No new external
theorem is used. No new branch or family rejection is based on that search.

## Jobs, runtime, and precision, frozen before any mathematical import

1. m8: NumPy binary64 vertex GD and independently coded standard-library
   binary64 edge GD, every query and both full states. Independent edge start
   uses the displayed sine identity, not differences of the vertex start.
2. m10: one binary64 vertex trajectory, full vectors at every query to support
   the independent precision comparison, plus all scalar diagnostics.
3. m12: one binary64 vertex trajectory; scalar records complete, vectors sampled.
4. m14: one binary64 vertex trajectory; scalar records complete, vectors sampled.
5. One m10 edge trajectory in NumPy longdouble, provided its recorded significand
   exceeds binary64. Recompute pi by longdouble arccos(-1), sqrt2, all powers,
   N, and v independently. Build H recursively instead of trailing-zero indexing.
   Compare the whole trajectory to the archived m10 binary64 vertices at every
   query. This is not an interval proof. If m8 calibration fails, slot5 can
   instead be used ONCE for a documented implementation/calibration correction;
   no other retry. If that calibration still fails, retire discovery slots.

The existing own .venv-pep Python/NumPy runtime is used, single-thread settings.
No installation, compiler, solver, DP, parameter scan, or uncharged smoke test.
The source and this plan are hashed before job1. Each job is charged SIX full
seconds in the canonical ledger BEFORE subprocess creation/import. The separate
standard-library parent enforces a six-second subprocess wall timeout. All
source parsing, imports, mathematics, comparisons, and raw data writing are inside
that bound. Failures and timeouts retain the full charge. No refunds or renewals.
Administrative hashing, ledger updates and byte-preserving compression are
measured separately outside the mathematical child; they do not evaluate any
trajectory or benchmark. Transport wall is child subprocess creation through
return. Child records import, evaluator, and raw-write scopes separately.

## Tolerances, rounding, guards, and counters

N is the ceiling in the arithmetic of that independent run; log the unrounded
argument and its distance to the nearest integer. No forced agreement of N.
Binary64 uses the existing platform/libm/NumPy default rounding; no rounding-mode
change. Metadata reports float type, significand bits, eps, tiny and maximum.
Longdouble is claimed higher precision only if nmant exceeds binary64 nmant.
The float platform's default rounding is recorded, not certified by intervals.

All numeric equality checks use |error|<=2e-11+2e-9*max absolute magnitude of
the compared values. Mean/edge-sum checks compare the sums to zero with that
same tolerance and sum-of-absolute-values scale. Full-vector discrepancies
are checked in maximum coordinate norm. Every residual is saved even if it
passes. Calibration requires finite states, all equality/sector/factor checks,
and all n physical updates in BOTH implementations. A nonfinite state or
overflow invalidates the case and stops it; never clip, rescale, project, change
step, repair the mean, or resume it. Finite calibration residual failures finish
the original m8 trajectory for diagnosis but prevent discovery authorization.
Discovery first-child/central diagnostics are also guarded; failed cases are
labeled invalid and cannot supply a terminal observation. Underflow/cancellation
indicators are reported; any underflow makes the numerical case suspect.

Record at EVERY query: k, previous and next physical step, F, squared vertex
norm and norm, positive/negative edge mass, min/max edge, gradient norm, active
edge count, edge sum, vertex mean/sum, finite, overflow, invalid/divide, NumPy
underflow callbacks, observed subnormal count, cancellation-near-tie count,
and changed active-edge count. Cancellation means |a-b|<=64eps(|a|+|b|)
for nonzero neighboring operands; it is an indicator, not proof of lost accuracy.
NumPy flags do not certify the absence of libm or Python-scalar underflow.
Do not delete ties; max(y,0) is the function definition, not a numerical clip.

Record every m8 query's vertex x, independent edge y, reconstructed edge x,
and vertex differences. m10 and precision m10 full vector logs are complete.
m12/m14 vector samples are EXACTLY k=0,1,2,3,2^(m-1)-1,2^(m-1),
2^(m-1)+1,2^(m-1)+2,n, labeled SAMPLED. All scalar logs remain complete
unless a failure/timeout is explicitly recorded. Count n updates and n+1
query-gradient evaluations per complete trajectory, including the terminal
diagnostic gradient. Count the independent calibration and precision runs
separately. No stored-data comparison is a new trajectory. On timeout the
last line gives a committed lower count; at most one update/query is in flight,
so record that interval rather than inventing an exact completed count.

## Terminal quantities and original benchmark

Use actual F_n to report V_m=4F_n/N. The draft's J_m is recovered from this
same original trajectory as J_m=8N F_n/b^2: positive homogeneity gives
x_n=b Phi_(m-1)(-v), so (Dx_n)_+=b[Psi_(m-1)(-Dv)]_+.
No extra normalized-suffix trajectory is evaluated. Report F_n/F_mid and
V_mid=4F_mid/N, also J_m/(4Nz^2), which is the same retention ratio.

Only accepted04219554e41d9750 is used for the benchmark. Its proof(3.1)
and V_N>=U_N give B_(2^m-1)>=lambda^m. Its unconditional ray enclosure
at b=1,j=0 uses B_1=G(1,1)=4 and gives Z_1<=(2+3/100)^2=41209/10000.
Therefore B_(2^m-1)<= (41209/10000)lambda^m. Evaluate these CLOSED FORM
bounds within each job, and report both products with V_m. No new DP.
An upper product below1 is finite negative evidence for this one trajectory;
a lower product above1 is finite positive evidence for this one trajectory,
subject to numerical accuracy. Opposite directions are inconclusive. No
finite observation establishes an asymptotic theorem or the all-word Goal.

## Archival and closure

All source/output is in this absolute public directory. The parent preserves
original stdout, stderr, and raw query records as lossless gzip before any
interpretation; summaries, hashes, counters, and timings form the raw manifest.
After the allowed jobs, publish one full plan, raw manifest, scoped interpretation,
and budget closure via shared memory. Retire unused slots/seconds. No fact_submit,
no target finalization, no self-renewal, no project-wide shutdown.

# Fixed sparse-cycle pilot: complete finite observations, no theorem

All five registered jobs finished, with exit code0, within their externally
enforced six-second child caps. The m8 dual-coordinate calibration passed at
every query. The independent higher-precision m10 run agreed throughout the
whole trajectory. The observed terminal payoffs are far below the original
benchmark at all four tested indices, even after using an upper bound for the
benchmark denominator. These are numerical observations, not verified facts,
interval enclosures, an asymptotic obstruction, or the original all-word theorem.

The canonical ledger is CLOSED_EXHAUSTED: 5/5 jobs, 30/30 accounted seconds,
zero further authorized jobs or seconds. There was no correction, failed job,
timeout, installation, solver, benchmark DP, formal submission, or extra run.

## Fixed model and calibration

The full pre-job plan is PLAN.md, shared plan3933b8a70dfca924. Its immutable
source hashes are in freeze_manifest.json. All artifacts are under
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/sparse_cycle_discovery_20261001/.
The source-only update to final draft37ec6ea9a52bfbdf is accounted for in
PROVENANCE_ADDENDUM.md; no mathematical object changed and no case was replayed.

For each fixed enclosing m, lambda=1+sqrt2, beta=1-1/m,
c=lambda(1+1/m), t_j=1+(beta/c)c^j, d=1+beta*c^(m-2),
N=ceil(pi sqrt(d/6)), theta=pi/(2N), mu=sin(theta)^2,
v_i=cos((2i+1)theta), and z=cos(theta). Starting at x_0=v, the original
word H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,t_j,H_j) is run with the SAME m in every child.
The function is F(x)=sum_i(x_i-x_(i+1 mod N))_+^2/8, with declared L=1.
There is no recalibration to the least smoothness constant and no state reset.

PLAN.md derives the vertex gradient g_i=(r_i-r_(i-1))/4, with
y_i=x_i-x_(i+1) and r_i=max(y_i,0), and independently derives the edge update
y'_i=y_i-(h/4)(2r_i-r_(i-1)-r_(i+1)). Convexity follows from convexity of the
squared positive part. The bound ||D||<=2 for the cycle incidence D, together
with the 1-Lipschitz positive-part map, gives a gradient Lipschitz constant
at most ||D||^2/4<=1. These elementary formulas are joint diagnostic reasoning,
not separately verifier-accepted facts. The minimum set is the constants and
R^2=||v||^2=N/2, as derived in the plan. The initial gradient equals mu*v.

The first child is calibrated against both its running scalar product and
A_m=product_(j=0)^(m-2)(1-mu*t_j)^(2^(m-2-j)). Its factors are checked positive.
The postcentral state is calibrated against -b_m*v, where b_m=A_m(mu*d-1)>0.
The next two vertex and edge states are checked against formulas rederived
in PLAN.md, including the active counts1,3,5. None of the checks substitutes
the scalar formula for the actual evolved vertex or edge state.

At m8, N=25, both implementations completed255 updates and256 gradient
queries each. Maximum discrepancies over ALL queries were:

| Check | Maximum absolute error |
|---|---:|
| Independently evolved vertex states | 2.303713e-15 |
| Vertex differences versus independent edge states | 4.461709e-15 |
| Gradients | 2.185752e-15 |
| Objective values | 1.070650e-17 |
| Running first-child scalar formula | 2.442491e-15 |
| Vertex sum | 2.220446e-15 |
| Independent edge sum | 9.870577e-16 |

All registered equality, factor, sign, and sector checks passed. All m8 raw
queries retain both full states, the reconstructed edge-coordinate vertex
state, actual values, and diagnostics. Discovery first-child and central
checks also passed; m14's largest first-child coordinate discrepancy was
8.053836e-13 and its central discrepancy was3.167605e-13.

## Terminal quantities from the original trajectories

The table uses the actual terminal F(x_n), n=2^m-1. V_m=4F(x_n)/N.
The draft's J_m is recovered WITHOUT another trajectory:

    J_m = 8N F(x_n)/b_m^2,
    V_m = b_m^2 J_m/(2N^2).

Indeed positive homogeneity gives x_n=b_m Phi_(m-1)(-v), so its positive
terminal edge vector is b_m times the draft's normalized suffix edge vector.
This uses the whole inherited original run. The scalar conversion was
computed inside each charged job. The energy-retention column is actual
F(x_n)/F(x_(2^(m-1))); its independently equivalent formula J_m/(4Nz^2)
was also recorded. No additional normalized-suffix evaluation was performed.

| m | N | Actual terminal F | V_m | Recovered J_m | Suffix energy retention |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 8 | 25 | 2.696941589230061e-8 | 4.315106542768097e-9 | 0.005041962804597343 | 5.061920180871412e-5 |
| 10 | 61 | 5.612589759733588e-9 | 3.680386727694157e-10 | 0.002899848110546708 | 1.189250760194295e-5 |
| 12 | 151 | 1.454064818848868e-9 | 3.851827334698989e-11 | 0.002255608689632351 | 3.734855624232673e-6 |
| 14 | 371 | 3.911092821660896e-10 | 4.216811667558917e-12 | 0.001802529004755589 | 1.214663960849445e-6 |

The corresponding midpoint V values are8.524643590933214e-5,
3.094710426834515e-5,1.031318937660501e-5,3.471587042567719e-6.
The midpoint supplies no terminal lower bound. Four declining J values do
not determine whether J_m tends to zero, stays bounded away from zero, or
changes behavior later.

## Original all-split benchmark, with correct inequality directions

Only accepted fact04219554e41d9750 is used. Its proof gives
B_(2^m-1)>=lambda^m, and its unconditional dyadic-ray enclosure at b=1,j=0
gives B_(2^m-1)<=lambda^m Z_1<=(41209/10000)lambda^m, since B_1=4.
No condition on the seed in that fact is used. All comparison values were
evaluated INSIDE the registered jobs, using these closed forms and no DP.

| m | V_m times B_lower | V_m times B_upper |
|---:|---:|---:|
| 8 | 4.979629211091138e-6 | 2.052055401598547e-5 |
| 10 | 2.475428058328284e-6 | 1.020099148556502e-5 |
| 12 | 1.509993350766138e-6 | 6.222531599172177e-6 |
| 14 | 9.634824306554106e-7 | 3.970414748487882e-6 |

Every observed UPPER product is below1, making these finite instances weak
benchmark witnesses, subject to numerical accuracy. This conclusion does not
misuse a lower product below1. It is not a floating-point-certified rigorous
inequality. The midpoint UPPER products at m12 and m14 are1.666070184560507
and3.268735120544179, respectively; their exceeding1 does NOT establish that
either midpoint beats the exact benchmark. Their lower products are only
0.4042976496785914 and0.7932090369929333.

## Observed sectors and a useful analytical lead

The complete scalar logs show no first-child sector change. At the central
update all N edge signs switch from the initial N-1-edge path to the one
closing edge. Subsequent recorded sector changes each alter exactly TWO
edges and increase the active count by TWO:1,3,5,... through the terminal
active count listed below. Thus these finite numerical runs show only
activation after the reversal, with no observed later deactivation.

| m | Queries with a sector change, including reversal | Total individual edge-status changes | Terminal active count | Last change at physical index |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 8 | 12 | 47 | 23 | 192 |
| 10 | 30 | 119 | 59 | 832 |
| 12 | 74 | 297 | 147 | 3520 |
| 14 | 183 | 735 | 365 | 15040 |

sector_events_copied.json contains every such event copied from the registered
query diagnostics, including original physical index, adjacent steps, and F.
The increasing active set is a potential invariant for an analytical argument,
not an all-depth fact established by these computations.

Energy itself is not numerically monotone. At m10, F goes from
3.6023782185404033e-7 at k=767 to2.292179015911102e-6 at k=768, after the
step839.3417655365569; active counts go53 to55. At m14, F goes from
1.517398814784241e-9 at k=14335 to5.69628975236541e-9 at k=14336, after
step12452.004892731098; active counts go361 to363. A proof must accommodate
these actual large-step bursts even if it succeeds in proving nested activity.
No monotone-energy or total-clock approximation is licensed by the data.

## Independent higher-precision comparison

Job5 recomputed the entire m10 run in independently implemented EDGE
coordinates, including pi, sqrt2, every step constant, N, the initial vector,
and the recursively assembled word. N again rounded to61. NumPy calls the
storage dtype float128, but the recorded significand is64 bits, not113;
eps=1.084202172485504434e-19. The binary64 runs have53-bit significands.
The comparison uses all1,024 queries and all full stored vectors. It is a
whole-trajectory recomputation, not a terminal reevaluation or interval proof.

Maximum absolute vertex discrepancy was1.4540143855644797e-14; maximum
edge discrepancy2.4431411754788357e-14; maximum gradient discrepancy
1.0893171715199127e-14; maximum objective discrepancy5.0606645178198216e-17;
maximum relative objective discrepancy5.171618537049714e-13. There were
ZERO sector-disagreement queries. All registered checks passed.
The higher-precision terminal values were
V_10=3.6803867276941625466e-10 and J_10=0.0028998481105466267767.
This supports accuracy of the finite m10 observations but provides no
independent precision check for m12 or m14 and no mathematical error enclosure.

There were no NumPy overflow, underflow, invalid or division callbacks,
no observed subnormals, and no registered cancellation-near-tie indicators
in any completed case. The flags have exactly the scope stated in PLAN.md;
they do not certify absence of every possible libm/Python-scalar rounding event.

## Raw records, counts, and timing scopes

All original stdout/stderr and query bytes were losslessly compressed BEFORE
interpretation. Every gzip file's checksum and decompressed bytes/checksum
passed archive_audit.json. Original stderr is empty in all five jobs.
The query indices are consecutive and complete. Full vector logs exist for
all256 m8 queries and all1,024 queries of each m10 run. m12 and m14 each
have all scalar queries but only the NINE preregistered vector samples,
explicitly labeled SAMPLED, at0,1,2,3,mid-1,mid,mid+1,mid+2,n.

| m8 calibration | 255 | 256 | 255 / 256 | 1.372325 |
| m10 binary64 | 1023 | 1024 | 0 / 0 | 1.170980 |
| m12 binary64 | 4095 | 4096 | 0 / 0 | 1.822752 |

There are21,756 updates/21,760 query gradients in the four primary binary64
trajectories; the independent calibration adds255/256 and independent
precision adds1,023/1,024. Overall:23,034 physical updates and23,040 query
gradients across SIX trajectories in FIVE charged jobs. The terminal diagnostic
gradient is counted. No comparison of stored data is counted as a new trajectory.

Measured child transport totals11.269026977970498 seconds. Within the children,
recorded import time totals3.269489779995638 seconds, evaluation plus small
nonquery metadata-I/O overhead3.23628609025036 seconds, and raw query writing
3.920053683745209 seconds. Separate summary-write times per job are recorded

0.001919799,0.037622031 seconds, respectively. The child timing files retain

For m14 the broad parent operation was6.243409 seconds while the externally
bounded mathematical child transport was5.381258 seconds. The predeclared
six-second bound applies to the latter, including imports, all evaluation,
and raw data writes; byte-preserving archive operations are separate.

The budget remains charged at30 seconds, with NO refund based on measured
time. The main guard990 is a conservative reservation, not measured cumulative

and ledger_closed_snapshot.json both record closure; no credit renews on return.

## Remaining task and exact scope of closure

This pilot completes the assigned finite discovery. It supplies calibrated
data and a candidate nested-activity mechanism, but no verified estimate for
the all-depth chronological J_m. An analytical proof still has to control the
actual suffix, including its growing active set and nonmonotone large-step
energy excursions, with the inherited state and original physical clock.
Neither a positive lower limit nor a vanishing upper limit for J_m is proved.

The single allocated round ends with these shared records. No fact_submit
was authorized or used, so there is no new fact_id and no formal success claim.
The selected asymptotic family and original all-word exact-profile target
remain OPEN. This closes only this exhausted numerical allocation.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001419 -->
# A critical directed cycle: exact central reversal and the remaining suffix estimate

Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED ANALYTICAL DRAFT, max4 round53, 2026-10-01.
No fact_submit was authorized or made. No numerical evaluations, trajectory
replays, symbolic programs, solvers, benchmark DP, installations, or subagents
were used. All new formulas below are proved together as draft evidence, not
adopted from earlier unverified findings.

This is ONE graph, weight, and initial-state law. It passes the requested early
screens: a vanishing exact radial energy gap, a genuine sector reversal on the
original word, newly activated overlapping edges, and noncommuting Hessians.
The full terminal payoff is NOT bounded at benchmark scale here. Section 9
states the exact remaining scalar energy estimate and its exact original-B
comparison. Section 10 explains why two natural proof attempts do not give it.
In particular, the large midpoint payoff is not a terminal lower bound, and
this document does not rule the family out.

## 1. Fixed law, normalization, and accepted interfaces

Fix an integer m>=8. Write

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/m, c=lambda*(1+1/m), a=beta/c,
    t_j=1+a*c^j, 0<=j<m,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,t_j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    n=2^m-1, d=t_(m-1)=1+beta*c^(m-2),
    N=ceil(pi*sqrt(d/6)), theta=pi/(2N),
    mu=sin(theta)^2, z=cos(theta),
    v_i=cos((2i+1)*theta), 0<=i<N.                         (1)

All children use this SAME enclosing m. The function, vertex count, and start
are fixed before the first query. The graph has exactly N directed edges
i->i+1 modulo N, each of weight one. For x in R^N define

    (D x)_i=x_i-x_(i+1 mod N),
    F(x)=(1/8)*sum_i ((D x)_i)_+^2,
    x_0=v, x_(k+1)=x_k-h_k*grad F(x_k), h=H_m.           (2)

Here s_+=max(s,0), and vector positive parts are componentwise. The declared
smoothness constant is L=1 throughout (including the payoff). Section 2 proves
this bound and also identifies the least constant, which is 1-mu. We do NOT
recalibrate the word by that least constant. There is no vanishing global
scalar multiplying F: its coefficient is the fixed number 1/8 for all m.

Let 1 be the all-ones vector, e_i the i-th coordinate unit vector,
P=I-11^T/N, and R=dist(v,argmin F). The payoff is

    V_m=2*F(x_n)/R^2.                                   (3)

Fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the original W_n normalization and all-directed
residual Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.
Every such inequality is derived directly here, including both terminal
directions and all optimum rows. No terminal-reduction shortcut is used.

For the original benchmark, use accepted b98bb807d514e80d and
04219554e41d9750. In full, C_0=B_0=1 and

    Omega(s,t)=(s+t+sqrt((s+t)^2+4st))/2,
    G(s,t)=2s+t/2+sqrt(t^2+8st)/2,
    C_q=max_(0<=k<q) Omega(C_k,C_(q-k-1)),
    B_q=max_(0<=k<q) G(C_k,B_(q-k-1)), U_q^ZJ=1/B_q.     (4)

Every original split remains. The ray part of accepted04219554e41d9750 says

    lambda^(-m)*B_(2^m-1) -> Z_1, 0<Z_1<infinity.       (5)

Only this unconditional ray assertion is used; none of that fact's conditional
seed hypotheses is asserted to hold. Its statement and the proof's benchmark,
ray enclosure, and convergence sections were read. The prior triangle draft
89154918c70588a0 is motivation only, not a mathematical premise.

## 2. Static function, true radius, exact gap, and smoothness

Put A=D/2. The scalar function s_+^2/2 is convex and continuously
differentiable with derivative s_+. Thus

    F(x)=||(A x)_+||^2/2,
    grad F(x)=A^T(Ax)_+=D^T(Dx)_+/4.                    (6)

The elementary bound sum_i(x_i-x_(i+1))^2<=4 sum_i x_i^2 implies ||D||<=2;
the positive-part map is 1-Lipschitz. Formula (6) therefore proves that F is
convex, everywhere finite, continuously differentiable, and 1-smooth.

Its minimum is zero and its entire minimum set is span{1}. Indeed F(x)=0
means x_i<=x_(i+1) around the entire directed cycle, forcing equality everywhere.
Also 1^T grad F(x)=0, so the mean is preserved at every step, including a zero
step. Pairing i with N-1-i shows sum_i v_i=0. The identity cos^2 u=(1+cos 2u)/2
and the telescoping formula
2*sin(pi/N)*cos((2i+1)*pi/N)=sin((2i+2)*pi/N)-sin(2i*pi/N) give

    R^2=||v||^2=N/2.                                    (7)

There is no hidden choice of a more distant minimizer in this denominator.

For later use, the unweighted combinatorial Laplacian of a path on r vertices, divided by4,
has eigenvectors cos(k*pi*(i+1/2)/r), 0<=k<r, and eigenvalues
sin^2(k*pi/(2r)). This follows by substituting the cosine addition formula at
each interior vertex and the one-neighbor formula at each endpoint. Their
pairwise orthogonality follows by expressing products of cosines as sums and
using the finite geometric series; hence these r vectors form a basis.

The strict active edges of F at any x form a disjoint union of paths, since
all edges of a directed cycle cannot have strictly positive differences. If
H_x=(1/4)*sum_(i:(Dx)_i>0) (e_i-e_(i+1))(e_i-e_(i+1))^T, then

    grad F(x)=H_x x, 2F(x)=x^T H_x x.                    (8)

Every path component has at most N vertices. Its largest eigenvalue is at
most cos^2(pi/(2N))=1-mu. Along a segment, (6) is continuous and affine on
finitely many subsegments; on each its derivative is such a path Hessian.
Integrating along the segment proves that grad F is (1-mu)-Lipschitz.
At the strictly decreasing point v all path edges0,...,N-2 are active and the
closing edge is inactive. Perturbing v a sufficiently small amount in the top
path eigenvector keeps this strict sector. The derivative attains norm1-mu
there. Consequently the LEAST smoothness constant is exactly1-mu.

The exact radial energy gap on the mean-zero space is

    min_(x !=0, sum x_i=0) 2F(x)/||x||^2 = mu.           (9)

Here is a direct proof that does not confuse the gap with strong convexity.
Minimize 2F on the compact mean-zero unit sphere. Its minimum rho is strictly
positive because the only zeroes of F are constants. The multiplier equation
and sum grad F=0 give grad F(x)=rho*x at a minimizer: the multiplier on 1 is
zero, and taking the inner product with x uses <grad F(x),x>=2F(x).
By (8), rho is a positive eigenvalue of a path-component Hessian. The path
spectrum above puts every such positive eigenvalue at least mu. Conversely,
v is strictly decreasing and is the lowest positive cosine eigenvector of
the N-vertex path, so grad F(v)=mu*v and 2F(v)=mu*||v||^2.

Thus (9) is exact, but some sector Hessians have large kernels. In particular
no claim of globally mu-strong convexity is made.

A useful elementary upper value bound is

    sum_i ((Dx)_i)_+^2 <=2||Px||^2, hence 2F(x)<=||Px||^2/2. (10)

To prove it, replace x by Px and partition strict active edges into maximal
decreasing runs. On a run with endpoint values b>=e, the sum of the squared
nonnegative drops is at most (b-e)^2<=2(b^2+e^2). Different runs have disjoint
vertex sets, since at least one inactive edge separates them. Summing yields
(10). Isolated vertices add only nonnegative norm to the right side.

The smoothness constant stays near one while the relevant radial gap mu tends
to zero. Already T_t(v)=(1-t*mu)*v for 0<=t<1/mu shows that no strict norm
contraction independent of N can be transported from the triangle family.

## 3. All original directed rows with the non-isometric incidence

For ANY two points x_i,x_j define y_i=D x_i, r_i=(y_i)_+, and Delta r=r_i-r_j.
Their actual value and gradient residual is exactly

    Q_ij=-(1/4)*sum_e r_(i,e)*(-y_(j,e))_+
         -(1/32)*sum_e (Delta r_e+Delta r_(e+1))^2 <=0,  (11)

with indices modulo N. No condition on chronological proximity or sector is
present, so this includes nonlocal, optimum, and terminal rows.

Proof: for phi(s)=s_+^2/2 a direct check of the four sign combinations gives

    phi(u)-phi(w)+u_+*(w-u)+(u_+-w_+)^2/2=-u_+*(-w)_+.

Sum this at u=(Ax_i)_e,w=(Ax_j)_e and then correct the gradient norm from
||Delta p||^2 to ||A^T Delta p||^2, where p_i=(Ax_i)_+=r_i/2. This yields

    Q_ij=-<p_i,(-Ax_j)_+>
          -(1/2)<Delta p,(I-AA^T)Delta p>.

Here AA^T is the CYCLE Laplacian divided by4, not a projection. For every w,

    <w,(I-AA^T)w>=(1/4)*sum_e(w_e+w_(e+1))^2.

Substitute Delta p=Delta r/2 to obtain (11). This also proves the required
positive semidefiniteness without using an isometric incidence identity.
Ties give zero positive parts and cause no exception.

## 4. The rounding law and the exact first child

The following elementary estimates hold for every m>=8:

    d>144, mu<1/96, 5/4<mu*d<3/2,
    mu*t_j<2/3 for every j<=m-2.                         (12)

First lambda>12/5, beta>=7/8, and (12/5)^3=1728/125>13 give
d>1+(7/8)*13^2>144. Put b=pi*sqrt(d/6). Since sqrt(6)<pi and sqrt(d)>12,
1/b<1/12 and b<=N<b+1<(13/12)b. Thus
theta<=sqrt(6)/(2sqrt(d))<1/8 and theta^2<1/96.
Integration of cos u>=1-u^2/2 gives sin(theta)/theta>=1-theta^2/6>99/100.
It follows that

    mu*d < pi^2*d/(4N^2) <=3/2,
    mu*d >(3/2)*(12/13)^2*(99/100)^2 >5/4.

For the last strict comparison, the numerator216*9801=2117016 exceeds
(5/4)*1690000=2112500. Finally

    t_(m-2)/d =1/c+(1-1/c)/d <5/12+1/144=61/144,
    mu*t_(m-2)<61/96<2/3.

On the positive ray of v, grad F(qv)=q*mu*v for q>=0. By (12), each factor
1-mu*t_j in the first child is positive. Induction over the ACTUAL physical
steps, not a rearranged nonlinear trajectory, gives

    x_(2^(m-1)-1)=A_m*v,
    A_m=product_(j=0)^(m-2)(1-mu*t_j)^(2^(m-2-j))>0.    (13)

The multiplicity comes from the original recursive concatenation. Regrouping
these scalar factors is justified only because the entire first child has
just been proved to stay in this one sector.

For completeness A_m has a uniform positive lower bound. Let
S=sum H_(m-1). Summing the physical multiplicities gives

    S=2^(m-1)-1+[a/(c-2)]*(c^(m-1)-2^(m-1)).            (14)

We have 0<a/(c-2)<1 because c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>beta.
Also d>2^(m-1): the lower bound (7/8)*(12/5)^(m-2) exceeds2^(m-1) at m=8
(using13^2), and its ratio to2^(m-1) increases by6/5 with each increment of m.
Consequently

    S/d <1/(c-2)+1 <lambda+1 <7/2,
    mu*S<21/4.

Since -log(1-u)=integral_0^u(1-s)^(-1) ds<=3u for0<=u<=2/3,

    exp(-63/4)<A_m<=1.                                  (15)

This bound is only for the first child. It is not a suffix or terminal bound.

## 5. First nonlocal cycle and the central sign reversal

The first three queried points of the ORIGINAL run are alpha_i*v with

    alpha_0=1, alpha_1=1-mu*t_0,
    alpha_2=(1-mu*t_1)*(1-mu*t_0).

All three alpha_i are positive and distinct. Their original residuals are

    Q_ij=-(mu*(1-mu)/2)*(N/2)*(alpha_i-alpha_j)^2.        (16)

This follows by substituting F(alpha v)=mu*alpha^2*N/4 and grad F(alpha v)=
mu*alpha*v into the definition of Q. In particular the first three-query
nonlocal cycle has

    Q_01+Q_12+Q_20
      =-mu*(1-mu)*N/4*[(alpha_0-alpha_1)^2
                         +(alpha_1-alpha_2)^2+(alpha_2-alpha_0)^2]<0.

All other directed cycles are already covered by (11).

At the unique largest step, the still-positive point in (13) is updated by
the path Hessian. Equations (12)--(13) give the EXACT post-central point

    x_(2^(m-1))=-b_m*v,
    b_m=A_m*(mu*d-1), A_m/4<b_m<A_m/2.                  (17)

All former path differences are now negative. Only the closing edge is
positive, with value2*b_m*z. Thus

    2F(x_(2^(m-1)))/R^2=2*b_m^2*z^2/N.                 (18)

This is the actual MIDPOINT payoff, not the terminal one. In particular the
quadratic eigenmode is no longer invariant: grad F(-v) has support only at
vertices0,N-1, whereas -mu*v has nonzero interior coordinates for N>3.

## 6. First two changing-sector suffix steps

Positive homogeneity allows the common amplitude b_m to be factored out;
this does NOT reset a state. Define X_0=-v and let X_k follow the entire
physical suffix H_(m-1) using the SAME enclosing m. Then the actual original
state at the corresponding suffix index is b_m*X_k.

Set u=t_0/4, w=t_1/4. We have1/4<u<3/8 and15/32<=w<1/2. At X_0 only
edge N-1 is positive, of value2z. At X_1 the exact positive differences are

    r_(1,N-1)=q=2z*(1-2u)>0,
    r_(1,0)=r_(1,N-2)=p=z*(2u-4mu)>0.                   (19)

All other differences remain their strictly negative values from -v. For
example, the newly activated edge0 has difference
cos(3theta)-(1-2u)*cos(theta)=z*(2u-4mu), by the triple-angle identity.
The inequality mu<1/96 gives p/z>11/24.

At X_2 the three previously active edge values are

    r_(2,N-1)=(1-2w)*q+2w*p>0,
    r_(2,0)=r_(2,N-2)=(1-2w)*p+w*q>0.                  (20)

The next two edges become active with equal value

    r_(2,1)=r_(2,N-3)=w*p-8mu*z*(1-2mu)>0.             (21)

Indeed cos(3theta)-cos(5theta)=8mu*z*(1-2mu), while
w*p/z>(15/32)*(11/24)=165/768>1/12>8mu*(1-2mu).
Every other interior difference is unchanged and negative. N>12 here, so
the named edges and the unaffected interior are distinct.

Let L_i=(e_i-e_(i+1))(e_i-e_(i+1))^T with cyclic indexing. The first two
suffix Hessians are

    H^(0)=L_(N-1)/4,
    H^(1)=(L_(N-1)+L_0+L_(N-2))/4.                    (22)

They do not commute. Applying their commutator to e_1 gives

    (H^(0)H^(1)-H^(1)H^(0))*e_1=(e_(N-1)-e_0)/16 !=0.

This is an actual sequence of strict sectors, not an assumption about which
Hessians might occur. The second suffix step enlarges the active path again.

For a nonlocal cycle crossing these first suffix transitions, (11) gives a
strict Q_(X1,X0): the positive edge0 at X_1 has value p, while its value at
X_0 is -4mu*z, so its first summand is -p*mu*z<0. Since all other directed
residuals are nonpositive, Q_(X1,X0)+Q_(X0,X2)+Q_(X2,X1)<0.

## 7. Exact chronology for all depths, including ties and zero steps

For an arbitrary nonnegative step t let

    T_t(x)=x-(t/4)*D^T(Dx)_+,
    E_t(y)=y-(t/4)*D D^T y_+.

Thus in edge coordinates the exact recursion is

    y'_(i)=y_i-(t/4)*(2(y_i)_+-(y_(i-1))_+-(y_(i+1))_+). (23)

It preserves sum y_i=0. The formula uses positive parts at each ACTUAL state;
no simultaneous diagonalization or time-homogeneous linear semigroup is
asserted. At a tie the positive part is zero, and at t=0 the map is the identity;
the query and all interpolation rows remain present.

For fixed m define the maps for whole child words by

    Phi_0=identity, Phi_(j+1)=Phi_j o T_(t_j) o Phi_j,
    Psi_0=identity, Psi_(j+1)=Psi_j o E_(t_j) o Psi_j.    (24)

Composition is read from right to left. These expressions apply the first
child, then its central step, then the second child to the OUTPUT of the first
two pieces. They neither change m nor reset x or y at a boundary.

Consequently the original terminal state and its exact payoff are

    x_n=b_m*Phi_(m-1)(-v),
    Y_m=Psi_(m-1)(-Dv)=D Phi_(m-1)(-v),
    E_m=|| (Y_m)_+ ||^2,
    V_m=b_m^2*E_m/(2N).                                (25)

Here E_m denotes a scalar terminal edge energy, not the one-step map E_t.
Equation (25) is an exact all-depth formula for the actual trajectory. Merely
writing it down is not the missing terminal estimate.

One actual but nondecisive upper bound follows directly from (23). Put
M(y)=sum_i(y_i)_+ and

    P_m=product_(j=2)^(m-2) (t_j/2)^(2^(m-2-j)).

For0<=t<=2, (23) is a sum of nonnegative multiples of neighboring positive
parts minus y_-, and M(y')<=M(y). For t>2, discard the negative central
coefficient1-t/2 to get M(y')<=(t/2)M(y). Here t_0<3/2, t_1<2, and
t_j>=t_2=1+lambda*(1-1/m^2)>3 for j>=2 and m>=8. Since M(-Dv)=2z,

    0<=E_m<=M(Y_m)^2<=4z^2*P_m^2,
    0<=V_m<=2*b_m^2*z^2*P_m^2/N.                        (26)

This upper bound allows enormous growth. It proves neither a useful family
obstruction nor a benchmark-scale positive lower bound. It is included to
distinguish a proved actual terminal bound from the unproved desired estimate.

## 8. Exact asymptotic scale of the retained incoming amplitude

The fixed dimension law and c=lambda(1+1/m) give

    d/lambda^m -> e/lambda^2,
    N^2/lambda^m -> pi^2*e/(6lambda^2),
    mu*d ->3/2.                                        (27)

Here e=exp(1). For the first limit, (1-1/m)(1+1/m)^(m-2)->e;
this follows by bounding log(1+s) between s-s^2/2 and s for small positive s.
The additive1 in d and the ceiling error in N vanish relative to d and sqrt d.
The limit sin(theta)/theta->1 proves the third limit.

Reindex r=m-1-j in (13):

    A_m=product_(r=1)^(m-1)
          [1-mu-(mu*d-mu)*c^(-r)]^(2^(r-1)).

Then

    A_m -> A_infty
      =product_(r=1)^infinity [1-(3/2)*lambda^(-r)]^(2^(r-1))>0,
    b_m -> b_infty=A_infty/2>0.                         (28)

To justify convergence of the product, its logarithmic summands are bounded
in absolute value by3*2^(r-1)*[mu+(3/2)*lambda^(-r)], using (12). For an
integer cutoff r_cut and r>r_cut, the sum of the second term tends to zero
uniformly as r_cut->infinity because
2/lambda<1. The first term over ALL r is at most3*mu*2^(m-1), which tends
to zero by (27). Each finite set of summands converges by (27). The limiting
series is absolutely convergent, and each factor is strictly positive since
(3/2)/lambda<1. This proves (28), including positivity, without computation.

Equations (18), (27), and (28) show that the midpoint payoff has exact order
1/N. Combined with (5), its ratio to the ORIGINAL terminal benchmark diverges
like a positive constant times N. That observation identifies energy available
at the reversal. It gives no right to transport it through the suffix.

## 9. The precise remaining nonlocal estimate and original-B comparison

Define the fully specified nonnegative number

    J_m=N*|| [Psi_(m-1)(-Dv)]_+ ||^2 = N*E_m.           (29)

There are no free parameters, omitted rows, adjustable amplitudes, or altered
children in (29). The exact terminal comparison is

    V_m/U_(2^m-1)^ZJ = b_m^2*B_(2^m-1)*J_m/(2N^2).    (30)

The coefficient in front of J_m has the positive finite limit

    K_cycle=3lambda^2*b_infty^2*Z_1/(pi^2*e)
           =3lambda^2*A_infty^2*Z_1/(4pi^2*e).          (31)

Hence the following are precise conditional bridges, not proved conclusions
about J_m:

* If liminf J_m>0, this one law gives a fixed positive multiple of the original
  benchmark on H_m.
* It gives the asymptotic lower bound (1-o(1))/B_(2^m-1) exactly when
  liminf J_m>=1/K_cycle.
* Its original-B ratio tends to zero exactly when J_m tends to zero.
* A claimed limiting constant requires an actual limit for J_m; none is proved.

Equivalence in the second bullet means the usual eventual asymptotic lower
inequality, or equivalently liminf of the payoff ratio at least1. All these
statements follow from (30) and convergence of its positive coefficient; no
asymptotic balanced approximation to B has replaced the all-split recurrence.

The exact open analytical task is to estimate (29) for the chronological
positive-part recurrence (23) with the word (24). Even a proof of
liminf J_m>0 is missing. The radial gap, the first-child product, legality,
and the positive midpoint amplitude do not imply it. Success for (29) would
still concern H_m only, not the original theorem over every nonnegative word.

## 10. Two direct attempts and concrete shortcut failures

Attempt A: extend the scalar low-eigenvalue product through the right child.
This fails at its first gradient query. Formula (17) changes the active path
to the single closing edge; grad F(-v) is supported at two endpoints and is
not -mu*v. Equations (19)--(22) further show actual overlap and noncommutation.
Thus the continued polynomial product is not the selected function's GD.

Even replacing the product by a sum-of-steps diffusion law loses actual order.
Starting at X=-v, either first step t_0 or t_1 yields exactly the same strict
three-edge sector in (22). Both lie below2 and exceed8mu. Writing s=t_0,t=t_1,
the two two-step compositions have difference

    T_t T_s X - T_s T_t X=(t-s)*(H^(0)-H^(1))*X !=0.     (32)

Indeed expand (I-tH^(1))(I-sH^(0))X and the reversed expression; the st term
cancels. The two omitted edge differences of X both equal -4mu*z, so
(H^(0)-H^(1))X is nonzero; t_1>t_0. This comparison refutes an order-free
shortcut on the same static function. It is not a second proposed run or a
change to the specified word.

Attempt B: use the exact radial gap and small-step dissipation to retain the
post-central energy. Equation (23) has nonnegative positive-part coefficients
only when t<=2. The actual level2 step is t_2=1+lambda*(1-1/m^2)>3, and later
levels are larger. The positive-mass argument yields only the expanding
factor t/2 in (26); it cannot give the needed lower bound on J_m.

The absence of a lower norm bound from the small radial gap is concrete.
On this SAME directed cycle consider the mean-zero vector w=e_(N-1)-e_0.
Only its closing edge is positive, so grad F(w)=w/2. The actual level1 step
t_1=2-1/m therefore gives

    T_(t_1)(w)=w/(2m), F(T_(t_1)w)=F(w)/(4m^2).         (33)

Meanwhile the graph's exact gap mu tends to zero exponentially. Thus gap
information alone cannot prevent strong damping of a possible edge-localized
component. This test is a counterexample to the generic proposed inference,
not an assertion that the specified suffix equals w.

The telescoping identity also keeps a signed overstep contribution:

    ||X_(k+1)||^2-||X_k||^2
      =-4h_k F(X_k)+h_k^2||grad F(X_k)||^2.             (34)

For steps above2 the smoothness bound does not turn (34) into a usable
lower bound on terminal energy. Continuous-time diffusion results or a
stable explicit discretization cannot be substituted for these large steps.

No counterexample to the actual statement liminf J_m>0 has been obtained.
Attempts A and B fail as proof routes; the selected family remains open.

## 11. Literature depth and scope

Matlas was queried for convex directed squared-positive-part graph energies
and their dynamics. Its returned theorems concerned different consensus or
optimization systems and supplied no applicable suffix theorem. Web search
located the exact primary paper:

Rasoul Shafipour, Ali Khodabakhsh, Gonzalo Mateos, Evdokia Nikolova,
*A Directed Graph Fourier Transform with Spread Frequency Components*,
arXiv:1804.03000v2 (2018), https://arxiv.org/abs/1804.03000v2 .

The PDF was downloaded to downloads/1804.03000v2.pdf and read through the web
tool's PDF text extraction. Section II-A equation(2) defines directed variation
as sum_ij A_ij*(x_i-x_j)_+^2 with nonnegative weights; its directed edge
convention agrees with D above. Proposition1's directed-path and directed-cycle
maximum-variation claims and their Appendix B--C proofs were read, as was the
underlying-undirected-graph comparison in Proposition2 and its proof.
This supplied context for the energy and the decreasing-run argument.
The paper studies variation and basis construction, not the chronological
large-step suffix (23). No external theorem is imported as a premise: (2),
(6), (9)--(11), and all dynamics are rederived in this joint draft.

## 12. Handoff

The required all-depth benchmark-scale terminal estimate is the unmet part of
this assignment. The exact selected law has been tested beyond generic setup:
its full first half, first nonlocal cycle, central sign reversal, first two
active-set transitions, true radius, non-isometric legality, amplitude limits,
and exact original-B interface are given above. A finite actual terminal upper
bound is proved but is nondecisive. No paid benchmark-scale terminal lower law,
family impossibility, full target theorem, or new verified fact is claimed.

This ends the one allocated analytical round. A later continuation would need
a new TASK and resource authorization, and should target (29), not repeat the
triangle family or assume an invariant quadratic mode after (17).
