# Two static scalar spatial laws on the original near-critical word

Status: self-contained analytical draft, UNVERIFIED, no fact_id. Worker max,
allocated round56 after completed55; completed ceiling56/attempted57. No
mathematical numerical evaluation, solver, symbolic program, formal request,
installation, consult, or subagent was used. This document does not establish
the original target. Its two negative screens are actual error upper bounds,
not an interpretation of weak lower certificates.

Controlling assignment: TASK.md; initial master guidance deebd8471c9ff5a7.
Later guidance f8408daf213a461d leaves this bounded analytical task unchanged
and closes every remaining formal grant. Plan finding: 6ec9e4bb4db97c3b.

## 1. Scope recovered before choosing the laws

The following are shared, UNVERIFIED drafts, not premises of the proofs below.

* 1a23bf9f1a7799fd uses c_sc=13/5, a_sc=c_sc^(-17), and the word
  Hsc_(j+1)=(Hsc_j,a_sc*c_sc^j,Hsc_j), without a +1 bridge. It treats a
  specified log-periodic derivative made of unit ramps and plateaus. Its
  extension covers every COMMON plateau parameter ell>=1 on the two rays,
  arbitrary independent ray phases, every initial point, and all crossings.
  Its unequal-ell statement retains the stated calibrated range
  b_r<=ell_sigma<=1+(S_r-log c_sc)/(c_sc-1), m>=r>=17. Neither statement
  covers arbitrary nonperiodic scalar derivatives or the original word here.
* 3d2994746c1d983e uses that same c_sc,a_sc and no-+1 word. It allows
  arbitrary horizon-dependent functions and knots, but every nonzero queried
  secant on EACH ray must fit that ray's fixed band
  M_sigma/c_sc<=|g(x)|/|x|<=M_sigma, with 0<M_sigma<=1. The two ray scales
  and phases are unrestricted relative to one another. Its conclusion is
  still conditional on those per-ray factor13/5 bands.
* 4d3911499920adbe uses that same no-+1 word. Its channels are precisely
  accepted F_ray, all scalar quadratics, its explicitly defined single-cap
  quadratic-prefix/affine-suffix channel, and the continuous two-curvature
  homogeneous family f(x)=u*x^2/2 on x>=0 and v*x^2/2 on x<=0. Its direct
  two-curvature proof is for u,v in (0,1], with zero curvatures addressed by
  continuity if included. It is not a bound for derivatives with additional
  spatial slope changes. Its horizon bounds start at m>=18.
* e08ee0674ce368d3 DOES use the original word, for m>=128. It fixes profiles
  g(sigma*R*t)=sigma*mu*R*G_sigma(t), with G_sigma(t)=t on [0,1],
  0<=t*G_sigma'(t)<=p_grow*G_sigma(t) outside, and a finite mutual ray ratio
  kappa_ray. Its first sector has mu=1/d_r for an integer r>=0,
  1/2<=p_grow<=3/4 and kappa_ray*(p_grow/(1-p_grow))^p_grow<=23/10.
  Its second has 0<mu<=1/d_2, 1/2<=p_grow<=2/3 and the corresponding product
  <=7/4. These are genuine restrictions, not a complete scalar reduction.

Accepted scopes were also checked. Fact 1fca3e5dd555cb4a constructs its
specified outward alternating-ray stages, including offsets and retained
areas; it does not exhaust scalar trajectories. Fact b98bb807d514e80d gives
K_n and the original benchmark recurrences, not an upper bound on all Huber
trajectories or all scalar functions. Fact 1252b8823bb5a290 supplies complete
directed interpolation and its stated unqueried-terminal reduction, with no
scalar losslessness claim. Fact 62a7d9329121fd75 compares its finite triangle
only to scalar quadratics, the complete specified ray channel, and the full
specified complex channel, NOT to all nonlinear scalar functions.

Only fact b98bb807d514e80d is needed as a mathematical predecessor below,
for the definitions and accepted bounds on the ORIGINAL benchmark. Every
new scalar calculation is reproduced in this joint draft. In particular no
shared unverified closure is imported as a lemma.

## 2. Original word, exact prefix products, and benchmark

Fix an integer m>=32 and retain this SAME m in every child. Put

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), t=1/m, beta=1-t,
    c_m=lambda*(1+t), a_m=beta/c_m,
    c=c_m, a=a_m (local aliases), b_j=a*c^j, d_j=1+b_j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j), j=0,...,m-1,
    n=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j.

The letter b_j in this document is the bridge EXCESS, not a physical step.
Let ell(i)=nu_2(i+1), where nu_2(k) is the exponent of2 dividing the positive
integer k. The actual physical step at index i is h_i=d_(ell(i)). Let s_2(i)
be the number of ones in the binary expansion of i, including s_2(0)=0.
For 0<=i<=n define

    Prod_i=product_(0<=r<i)(h_r-1)=beta^i*c^(-s_2(i)).              (2.1)

Here and below all finite sums and products are mathematical definitions;
none was evaluated by a program. To prove (2.1), binary carrying gives
s_2(i+1)=s_2(i)+1-nu_2(i+1). The ratio of its right sides at i+1 and i
is beta*c^(ell(i)-1)=a*c^ell(i)=h_i-1. Both sides start at1. In particular

    Prod_n=beta^n*c^(-m).                                        (2.2)

The S_j recurrence S_(j+1)=2*S_j+1+a*c^j gives

    S_j+1=A*c^j+(1-A)*2^j, A=a/(c-2)=beta/[c*(c-2)].          (2.3)

Since c>lambda>2 and lambda*(lambda-2)=1, we have 0<A<1, and hence
S_m+1<=c^m. These parameters also have 0<a<1, 0<beta<1, and c<3:
lambda<5/2 and c<=(5/2)*(33/32)=165/64<3.

Write C_k=1+S_circle(k), B_k=1+2*S_bullet(k), U_k^ZJ=1/B_k.
Fact b98bb807d514e80d supplies

    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_k=max_(0<=r<k) Omega(C_r,C_(k-r-1)),
    B_k=max_(0<=r<k) G(C_r,B_(k-r-1)),
    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4*u*v))/2,
    G(u,v)=2*u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8*u*v)/2,
    B_k<=1+4*k^(log_2(3)).

Thus, on the original physical length n=2^m-1,

    B_n<=5*3^m.                                              (2.4)

No asymptotic constant or numerically evaluated benchmark is needed here.
All payoffs use L=1 and TRUE distance to the minimizer set:

    V=2*(f(x_n)-min f)/dist(x_0,argmin f)^2.

Multiplying f by an arbitrary L>0 and using steps h_i/L preserves all
positions and this normalized payoff.

## 3. Law I: a flat minimizer interval with two unit-curvature sides

For any width w>=0 let I_w=[-w,0] and define the single static function

              (x+w)^2/2, x<-w,
    f_w(x)=   0,         -w<=x<=0,
              x^2/2,    x>0.

Let p_w(x)=min(0,max(-w,x)) be the interval projection, and put
g_w(x)=x-p_w(x). The derivative is continuous, nondecreasing, and has slopes
one, zero, one in spatial order. Consequently f_w is convex and1-smooth,
with exactly the minimizer set I_w and minimum0. For w>0 it is genuinely
nonlinear. We always normalize using distance to I_w, not distance to its
midpoint or another more distant chosen minimizer.

### 3.1 All directed inequalities and all integrated values

At every point, with y=p_w(x), we have

    x=g+y, f_w(x)=g^2/2, y in I_w.

For any two sampled points i,j, including ANY chosen optimum, the full
signed directed interpolation residual is

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2
        =g_i*(y_j-y_i)<=0.                                  (3.1)

Indeed g_i>0 implies y_i=0>=y_j; g_i<0 implies y_i=-w<=y_j;
and g_i=0 gives equality. This is an explicit all-pairs proof, including
both directions, all optimum rows, repeated points, and every plateau area.
The formula f=g^2/2 integrates the fixed derivative from the actual interval;
it is not a deletion of a previously accumulated area.

### 3.2 Actual GD, zero steps, capture, and exact revisits

Let Dist_i=dist(x_i,I_w), with Dist_0>0. At any outside point, a step h>=0 gives

    Dist_next=(1-h)*Dist_i,                     0<=h<=1,
    Dist_next=((h-1)*Dist_i-w)_+,               h>1.                (3.2)

For h<=1 the point stays on its current side or lands on its boundary.
For h>1 it either lands inside I_w or crosses to the opposite outside side.
An inside point has zero gradient and stays at precisely that same point.
This proves (3.2) directly on both sides of the interval. A zero step repeats
the complete jet. There is no restart at a different minimizer after capture.

Exact outside revisits are also legal: for any w>0,D>0, two consecutive
steps h=2+w/D take D to -w-D and then back to D. Each outside value is D^2/2.
This illustration uses the SAME law, not an additional candidate family.
Its only purpose is to make the exact-revisit convention explicit.

On our original word all h_i>1. Define Ret_0=0 and

    Ret_(i+1)=(h_i-1)*Ret_i+1,
    Ret_i=Prod_i*sum_(r=1)^i 1/Prod_r.                              (3.3)

Repeated application of (3.2) gives the exact formula

    Dist_i=(Prod_i*Dist_0-w*Ret_i)_+.                                 (3.4)

For the induction, ((b*z)_+-w)_+=(b*z-w)_+ when b,w>=0;
applying this to the preceding affine expression proves the next step.
If capture has occurred, the expression remains zero as required.

Consequently, for ALL w>=0 and every initial point outside I_w,

    V_w=(Dist_n/Dist_0)^2<=Prod_n^2=beta^(2*n)*c^(-2*m).             (3.5)

This is an upper bound on the actual error of the entire specified law,
including horizon-dependent widths, either initial side, and arbitrary
initial distances. It is not an upper bound on arbitrary scalar functions.

### 3.3 A concrete nonlinear, noncaptured trajectory and its full spatial order

Here is one exact choice with w>0 and every queried distance positive.
The Prod_i in (2.1) are pairwise distinct. To see this, all are in Q(sqrt(2)).
The nontrivial conjugation fixes beta and sends c to
c'=(1-sqrt(2))*(1+1/m). If beta^(i-j)*c^(-s_2(i)+s_2(j))=1,
divide by its conjugate. Since |c/c'|=lambda^2>1, the integer exponent
of c must be zero. Then beta^(i-j)=1 implies i=j.

Define the positive finite quantities

    Delta_sp=min_(0<=i<j<=n)|Prod_i-Prod_j|,
    Ret_max=max_(0<=i<=n) Ret_i,
    w=min{Prod_n/(2*Ret_n), Delta_sp/(4*Ret_max)}.                    (3.6)

These finite expressions define the width before the run; they are not a
numerical search or an unproved optimization problem. Start at x_0=1 and
choose the closest minimizer0, so the TRUE initial squared distance is1.
Since Ret_i/Prod_i is increasing in i, (3.6) implies

    Dist_i=Prod_i-w*Ret_i>=Prod_i/2>0,
    x_i=Dist_i if i is even, and x_i=-w-Dist_i if i is odd.         (3.7)

Every step thus crosses the whole minimizer interval. Equations (3.2)--(3.7)
give every physical point, derivative, value, and GD update without deleting
any interval or knot.

For completeness the spatial order, which is not chronological, is exact.
Put alpha=-log(beta)/log(c)>0 and order_i=s_2(i)+alpha*i. Then
Prod_i=c^(-order_i), and the order_i are distinct by the preceding argument.
For every pair, |w*(Ret_i-Ret_j)|<=Delta_sp/4<|Prod_i-Prod_j|, so Dist_i-Dist_j has exactly
the sign of Prod_i-Prod_j. Every odd-indexed point is less than -w; every
even-indexed point is positive. Among even indices, increasing order_i means
decreasing x; among odd indices, increasing order_i means increasing x.
This completely specifies the total order by explicit, distinct real keys
for each m, with NO guessed phase comparisons and no ties. The fixed
minimizer interval lies between the two groups. Other widths may have ties;
(3.1)--(3.4) still apply without changing any previously defined value.

The terminal point is -w-Dist_n, since n is odd, and

    f_w(x_n)=(Prod_n-w*Ret_n)^2/2,
    Prod_n^2/4<=V_w=(Prod_n-w*Ret_n)^2<Prod_n^2.                     (3.8)

Thus this nonlinear example has a proved exact payoff, not just a lower
certificate. Nevertheless (2.4)--(3.5) imply uniformly for the whole law

    B_n*V_w<=5*(3/lambda^2)^m ->0.                         (3.9)

The flat-minimum mechanism is weak on the original word even when its
width is chosen from the entire word and every queried point remains outside.

## 4. Law II: a one-sided Huber shelf with an actual inward return

Fix ONE function, independent of m:

              x^2/2, x<=1,
    F_sh(x)=  x-1/2, x>=1,
    g_sh(x)=min(x,1).

It is convex, differentiable, globally1-smooth, with unique minimizer0 and
minimum0. Its negative ray is quadratic and its positive ray eventually
has constant gradient. In particular the two outer ray gradients have no
finite multiplicative comparison bound of the type in e08ee0674ce368d3.
The complete function is fixed throughout the trajectory.

Define y(x)=(x-1)_+. Then at every x

    x=g+y, F_sh(x)=g^2/2+y, g<=1, y>=0, (1-g)*y=0.          (4.1)

The positive proximal value y is retained on every return to the shelf.
For ANY two points, including optimum0 and repeated queries,

    Q_ij=(1-g_i)*(y_i-y_j)=-(1-g_i)*y_j<=0.                (4.2)

This explicit factorization proves ALL directed inequalities for the entire
trajectory, including every incoming and outgoing terminal row. It uses
the integrated value in (4.1), not just the slope or a pointwise envelope.

### 4.1 Parameters of the selected return branch

Use exactly the enclosing-m d_0,d_1,d_2,d_3 from Section2 and put

    w_sh=beta^2*c,
    v_sh=w_sh-1-beta,
    R_sh=S_3+1=8+4*a+2*beta+beta*c.

For every m>=32 the following strict inequalities hold:

    0<v_sh<a*beta,
    c*v_sh<beta^2<1,
    1+beta<w_sh<2+a.                                     (4.3)

Here is an exact proof without numerical evaluation. Since beta>=31/32
and c>12/5, w_sh>(31/32)^2*(12/5)>2, the last inequality following from
12*31^2>10*32^2. Thus v_sh>0. Also

    c*v_sh-beta^2
      =lambda^2*(1-t^2)^2-lambda*(2+t-t^2)-(1-2*t+t^2)
      <=(2-lambda)*t-(lambda+2)*t^2<0.

The inequality uses (1-t^2)^2<=1-t^2 and lambda^2=2*lambda+1.
It proves c*v_sh<beta^2 and hence v_sh<a*beta. Finally

    c*(w_sh-2-a)
      =beta^2*c^2-2*c-beta
      <=lambda^2-2*lambda*(1+t)-(1-t)
      =-(2*lambda-1)*t<0.

Choose any delta in the FULL interval [0,v_sh/a] and start at

    X_0=R_sh+delta, z_sh=v_sh-a*delta>=0.                  (4.4)

The squared distance to the unique minimizer is exactly (R_sh+delta)^2.
The endpoint delta=v_sh/a is permitted and will give a genuine zero landing.

### 4.2 First ten physical steps, with the old shelf intact

The first seven steps are the actual word H_3, with levels

    0,1,0,2,0,1,0.

For 0<=i<=7 let Prefix_sh(i) be the sum of its first i physical steps. Then

    X_i=R_sh+delta-Prefix_sh(i)>=1+delta,
    g_i=1, F_sh(X_i)=X_i-1/2.                             (4.5)

All seven updates are therefore exactly the original GD updates, and
X_7=1+delta. The next three original physical levels are3,0,1, giving

    X_8=delta-b_3,      g_8=X_8,   F_sh(X_8)=X_8^2/2,
    X_9=w_sh-a*delta,   g_9=1,     F_sh(X_9)=X_9-1/2,
    X_10=z_sh,          g_10=z_sh, F_sh(X_10)=z_sh^2/2.      (4.6)

The equations follow in order from
X_8=X_7-d_3, X_9=-a*X_8, and X_10=X_9-d_1.
Their region assumptions are proved, not guessed:

    b_3-delta>=(w_sh-v_sh)/a=(1+beta)/a>1,
    X_9=1+beta+z_sh>1,
    0<=z_sh<=v_sh<1.

Furthermore delta<=v_sh/a<beta implies

    X_7<X_9<X_6, with X_6=2+a+delta.                      (4.7)

The right inequality follows from X_9<=w_sh<2+a<=X_6.
Thus X_9 is a strict inward return into the SAME spatial interval [X_7,X_6]
traversed during the last step of the earlier H_3. The shelf gradient there
is1 and its retained proximal value is y_9=X_9-1>0. Nothing is redefined on
this return. This is not an outward-only fresh-ray construction.

### 4.3 Every remaining query, spatial order, and ties

For EVERY actual physical index 10<=i<=n the exact formula is

    X_i=(-1)^(i-10)*z_sh*beta^(i-10)*c^(2-s_2(i)).          (4.8)

If z_sh=0 this means precisely that the actual point0 is repeated forever.
Assume z_sh>0. Since s_2(i)>=1 for i>=10,

    |X_i|<=c*z_sh<=c*v_sh<beta^2<1.                       (4.9)

Thus every one of these queries really has gradient X_i and value X_i^2/2.
At i=10, s_2(10)=2 gives the correct point z_sh. Binary carrying then gives

    X_(i+1)/X_i=-beta*c^(ell(i)-1)=-(h_i-1)=1-h_i.

This verifies every remaining GD update on the original physical word.
No child clock was restarted. In particular the bound (4.9) certifies every
intermediate query, not merely the endpoints of large blocks. Gradients
continue to vary inside those blocks.

Here is the complete spatial order. X_8<-1 is below every later negative
point. Every positive point of the tail is less than1. The old positive
queries are ordered

    1<=X_7<X_9<X_6<X_5<...<X_0.

The optimum0 separates the positive and negative tail points. For i>=10,
even i means positive and odd i means negative. Within either sign, their
magnitudes are proportional to c^(-order_i), with the SAME explicit keys
order_i=s_2(i)+alpha*i used in Section3.3. Thus increasing order_i gives
decreasing positive coordinates and increasing negative coordinates.
There are no ties among those keys: an equality of two magnitudes would
give beta^(i-j)*c^(-s_2(i)+s_2(j))=1, already proved to force i=j.
There are also no ties with the older groups by their strict separating
intervals. When z_sh=0, all indices i>=10 instead coincide with optimum0,
with gradient and value0. These are all ties in this construction.

The word itself has no zero steps. If a zero step is inserted, its actual
map x->x-0*g_sh(x) is the identity and repeats its jet; (4.2) remains valid.
Formula (4.8) refers to the original positive steps and can be pulled back
along their index injection. No claim transfers B_n to a new padded length.

### 4.4 Exact payoff and a uniform upper screen for this branch

The original n is odd and s_2(n)=m. Therefore

    X_n=-z_sh*beta^(n-10)*c^(2-m),
    F_sh(X_n)=z_sh^2*beta^(2*(n-10))*c^(4-2*m)/2,
    V_sh(delta)=((v_sh-a*delta)/(R_sh+delta))^2
                 *beta^(2*(n-10))*c^(4-2*m).              (4.10)

Over the ENTIRE selected interval 0<=delta<=v_sh/a, the numerator of the
fraction decreases to zero while its positive denominator increases. Its
maximum is attained at delta=0 and equals the displayed expression with
v_sh/R_sh. In particular, using c*v_sh<1, c<3, R_sh>1, and beta<1,

    V_sh(delta)<9*c^(-2*m),
    B_n*V_sh(delta)<45*(3/lambda^2)^m ->0.                 (4.11)

These are actual error upper bounds on a fully specified returning family.
For all m>=32 they already give V_sh<U_n^ZJ: lambda^2>9/2 implies
3/lambda^2<2/3, and 45*(2/3)^10=46080/59049<1. The same comparison for
Law I follows from (3.9). No guessed decimal or numerical phase is involved.

## 5. Counterchecks preventing an overstatement or a reset

### 5.1 Erasing the return's old area violates a directed inequality

On the trajectory (4.4)--(4.8), replace ONLY the value at X_9 by the falsely
reset core value g_9^2/2=1/2, leaving its position and derivative unchanged.
The earlier point X_7 has gradient1 and value X_7-1/2. Its row to point9 is

    Q_(7,9)=(X_7-1/2)-1/2+(X_9-X_7)=X_9-1>0.

This is an exact illegal directed pair, not an appeal to intuition about
history. The correct value X_9-1/2 has retained area X_9-1 and satisfies
every row by (4.2). The construction therefore directly tests the nonzero
proximal-height issue in the assignment.

### 5.2 The branch bound is FALSE for all starts of the same shelf function

Keep exactly F_sh and the same original H_m, but start at

    X_0^Hub=2*S_m+1.

Every actual query stays in the positive shelf, since even the terminal
point is S_m+1>1. Thus all gradients are1, the terminal value is S_m+1/2,
the true initial radius is 2*S_m+1, and

    V_Hub=1/(2*S_m+1).                                   (5.1)

This is the already familiar canonical Huber mechanism, not a third law
or a new claim of useful profile improvement. It prevents extrapolating
(4.11) to the full one-sided Huber family.

One can see its contrast at the ORIGINAL B scale without an external
asymptotic constant. The accepted recurrences imply inductively
C_(2^j-1)>=lambda^j, by choosing equal children and Omega(u,u)=lambda*u.
They also imply B_(2^j-1)>=lambda^j: choose equal child lengths, use
monotonicity of G (each displayed term increases in either nonnegative
argument), and G(lambda^(j-1),lambda^(j-1))=4*lambda^(j-1)
>=lambda^j. The base j=0 is1. From (2.3),

    B_n*V_Hub>lambda^m/(2*c^m)
              =1/[2*(1+1/m)^m]>1/6.                     (5.2)

For the last bound, the binomial theorem gives
(1+1/m)^m<=sum_(k=0)^m 1/k!<3: for k>=2, k!>=2^(k-1), and the resulting
geometric upper bound has sum1, with a strict inequality by k=3.
Thus a vanishing B-scaled bound for ALL starts of this same function would
be contradicted by (5.1)--(5.2). No such broader bound is asserted here.

## 6. Achieved scope and the missing interface

There are exactly two selected derivative laws. Law I has an actual uniform
upper bound for its entire interval-distance family, plus a fully defined
nonlinear example with exact ordering and a two-sided terminal estimate.
Law II has an exact returning trajectory for every m>=32 and every delta in
its specified interval, a full spatial-order description, nonzero retained
proximal heights, all directed inequalities, genuine within-block variation,
and an exact terminal payoff. Its whole selected returning branch is weak.
The zero-landing endpoint and inserted zero-step convention preserve all jets.

Neither diagnostic supplies a new witness with B_n*V approaching1. Even a
successful diagnostic on all these H_m would leave the every-n/every-h
target as a separate obligation. The present result is narrower: the two
specified screens do NOT exclude all nonlinear scalar functions, do NOT

No proposed bang-bang, monotonicity, symmetry, or spatial-order reduction
has been declared lossless. The old closed sectors are not enlarged by name.

The surviving interface is to construct a static scalar derivative with
additional retained spatial information (or another initialization on a
candidate function) whose ACTUAL original-word payoff is large enough at

of feasible interpolation variables alone would not resolve this interface.
All new mathematics in this document remains UNVERIFIED and cannot be used

## 7. Retrieval and resource record

# Exact record-chain profiles for the two frozen spiral functionals

Status: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. Worker max, round60.
This document analyzes explicitly defined scalar quantities. It does not
promote the frozen spiral construction to a fact or assert that an invalid
parameter choice gives an actual smooth-convex adversary. There is no
fact_submit allocation in this round.

Assignment: TASK.md; originating steering:06950132d6f04c7a.
Later steering4c8674478c3e4a53 was read; it assigns all-selection work
to another worker and expressly preserves this round60 assignment. The complete
frozen candidate cdd7f057606aeb18 was read at
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/shared_evidence/cdd7f057606aeb18.md,
SHA256 366cd6a12c8e4f56cf3c875c104f3e9bfdc185bf26c955e4f7e83d5bef7b607c.
Its formulas(5),(5a), not its unverified geometric conclusions, define the
quantities below. All new derivations belong to this joint draft.

The accepted input is fact04219554e41d9750, specifically its original
benchmark definition, unconditional ray limit, uniform enclosure, and
already certified interval table. No conditional seed from that fact is
assumed. No mathematical program, numerical evaluation, optimization,
parameter scan, benchmark recurrence, or formal verification was run.

## 1. Quantities, exact benchmark, and statement of the calculation

Use the original W_n(h): the supremum of
2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L*||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite dimension,
convex differentiable L-smooth function f, minimizer x_star, and
x_0!=x_star, under x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)*grad f(x_t).

The word notation H_j,a,c and scaling coefficients K,E follow TASK.md
locally; they do not change same-letter definitions in other facts.
New profile coefficients below use the suffix rc to avoid those
project-glossary collisions.

Put lambda=1+sqrt(2). For a fixed real tau>0 and integer m>tau, put

 beta=1-tau/m, c=lambda(1+tau/m), a=beta/c,
 H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.

All children retain these SAME enclosing(m,tau) parameters. Write
n=2^m-1 for the physical length, b_j=1+a*c^j for the level-j step,
and S_j=sum H_j. Thus tau=1 is the original diagnostic word; every
other tau defines a new word, with no transfer of its old witnesses.

For clarity, the benchmark is exactly

 C_0=B_0=1,
 Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
 G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
 C_n=max_(0<=i<n) Omega(C_i,C_(n-i-1)),
 B_n=max_(0<=i<n) G(C_i,B_(n-i-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

Every split is retained. Accepted04219554e41d9750 gives

 B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m -> Z_1>0,
 lambda^(-j)B_(2^j-1)<=Z_1
       <=lambda^(-j)(sqrt(B_(2^j))+3/100)^2.             (1.1)

Section7 below obtains, solely from that fact's existing certificate,

                    59/25 < Z_1 < 237/100.            (1.2)

For 0<kappa<1 and epsilon>0, define the following scalar quantities:

 Theta=arctan(1/epsilon),
 chi=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)*exp(kappa*Theta),
 d=1-kappa^2,
 T_sp=(exp(d*Theta/kappa)-1)/d,
 A=epsilon+chi*(1+(1+kappa)*T_sp)/epsilon.                (1.3)

The frozen geometry is claimed only for
0<kappa<=epsilon<=1/2. Outside that range, (1.3) remains a definition
of numbers and supplies no geometric theorem here.

For a legal checkpoint decomposition with ordinary block sums s_i,
selected steps b_i, and terminal ordinary sum s_tail, define

 F5 = product_i [(b_i-1)/(chi*s_i+max(epsilon*b_i,A))]^2
                         /(1+2*s_tail),
 F5a= product_i [b_i/(chi*s_i+2*A+epsilon*b_i)]^2
                         /[2*(1+s_tail)].             (1.4)

These are exactly frozen(5) and frozen(5a) with the minimal stated
a_sp=2A/chi. Empty selections mean F5=1/(1+2*S_m) and
F5a=1/[2*(1+S_m)]. We do not replace either terminal factor.

There is ONE checkpoint-selection rule throughout this document:

 R_m=max{A/epsilon, 1+2/epsilon^2},
 J_m=max{1,ceil(log(R_m/a)/log c)}.                    (1.5)

If J_m>=m, take the empty selection. Otherwise take ALL first-occurrence
records at levels j=J_m,J_m+1,...,m-1. No optimality among checkpoint
sets is asserted. The ceiling and exceptional prefix are part of the
rule, not adjustable errors.

We analyze exactly TWO scaling regimes:

 I. kappa=K/m, epsilon=E/m, with fixed 0<K<=E.
    For sufficiently large m these are in the frozen valid domain.

 II. The same scaling with fixed 0<E<K, interpreted only as a scalar
     calculation and a CONDITIONAL target for a future geometric theorem.
     Section9 states an abstract cost/radius interface for that theorem.

All asymptotic claims keep tau,K,E fixed as m increases. In either
regime restrict initially to integers m>max{tau,2K,2E,1}; the exact
finite product identities also hold at any other depth at which the
displayed definitions and stated parameter domain apply.

Define constants

 nu=pi/(2*log lambda), gamma=4+2*sqrt(2), q_rc=2*gamma,
 theta=nu/K.

The joint scalar conclusion is

 If K<=nu:
   lim B_n F5 = lim B_n F5a = (Z_1/2)*exp(-tau).       (1.6)

 If K>nu:
   lim B_n F5 = lim B_n F5a
     =Z_1/(2*lambda)*exp[-tau
           +(q_rc*tau-pi*K-2*E)*(1-nu/K)].              (1.7)

In the first case the selection is eventually empty. In the second,
k=m-J_m is the exact checkpoint count and k/m->1-theta>0. The
derivation controls all growing products; in fact the logarithmic
error in (1.7) is O(log(m)/m), with constants depending on the fixed
tau,K,E. Equations(1.6),(1.7) are limits of the chosen SCALAR
certificates divided by 1/B_n, not limits or upper bounds for W_n.

Specific consequences proved below:

 * In Regime I, at tau=1, BOTH limits are strictly below1/2 for EVERY
   fixed admissible K,E. A nonempty selected chain has limit below1/2
   throughout 0<tau<=1. This is not a statement about all checkpoint
   patterns, parameters varying beyond these scalings, or actual W_n.
 * In Regime I the explicit choice K=E=2nu has limit
   Z_1/(2lambda)*exp[lambda^2*tau-(pi+2)*nu].
   It exceeds5369/4250>1 for every fixed tau>=2.
 * In Regime II the fixed choice K=14/5,E=1/100 has limit strictly
   above1001/1000 for every fixed tau>=1. Thus a geometry with precisely
   these effective costs and a sufficiently small total additional
   radius/transfer loss would reach the benchmark on this family.
   The current frozen construction does NOT supply that geometry.

## 2. Exact masses and every physical index

Let

 zeta=a/(c-2)=beta/[c(c-2)], v=c(c-2).

Then 0<zeta<1, because with x=tau/m in(0,1),

 c(c-2)=1+gamma*x+lambda^2*x^2>1>beta.

The recurrence S_(j+1)=2S_j+1+a*c^j, S_0=0, gives exactly

 S_j=zeta*c^j+(1-zeta)*2^j-1, 0<=j<=m.              (2.1)

Indeed the formula is zero at j=0 and its next-step difference is
1+zeta(c-2)c^j=1+a*c^j. This proves(2.1) by induction.

Index the physical steps of H_m from0 through n-1. The FIRST occurrence
of b_j is at t_j=2^j-1, or physical step number2^j in one-based
notation. This follows by induction from the concatenation defining
H_(j+1). The word before t_j is H_j. Between t_j and t_(j+1) is H_j,
of length2^j-1; after t_(m-1) is H_(m-1).

For J=J_m<m, the exact decomposition selected by(1.5) consequently is

 prefix H_J, checkpoint b_J,
 gap H_J, checkpoint b_(J+1),
 gap H_(J+1), checkpoint b_(J+2), ...,
 gap H_(m-2), checkpoint b_(m-1), tail H_(m-1).        (2.2)

Equivalently, checkpoint j=J has incoming sum S_J, every checkpoint
j>J has incoming sum S_(j-1), and the tail sum is S_(m-1).
There are k=m-J checkpoints. The ordinary lengths add to n-k;
adding those k original steps gives exactly2^m-1. All unselected
records and all their physical copies remain ordinary steps.

The ceiling in(1.5) gives a*c^J>=R_m. Since c>1, every selected
checkpoint satisfies simultaneously

 b_j>=2*(1+epsilon^(-2)),
 epsilon*b_j>=epsilon+A>A.                           (2.3)

Thus both original thresholds hold at EVERY selected physical position.
The maximum in F5 is exactly epsilon*b_j, not an asymptotic replacement.

## 3. Exact all-depth products, including the exceptional first factor

For J<m, substituting(2.2),(2.3) into(1.4) gives the exact identities

 F5 = 1/(1+2*S_(m-1))
       *[a*c^J/(chi*S_J+epsilon*(1+a*c^J))]^2
       *product_(j=J+1)^(m-1)
          [a*c^j/(chi*S_(j-1)+epsilon*(1+a*c^j))]^2,   (3.1)

 F5a=1/[2*(1+S_(m-1))]
       *[(1+a*c^J)/(chi*S_J+2*A+epsilon*(1+a*c^J))]^2
       *product_(j=J+1)^(m-1)
          [(1+a*c^j)/(chi*S_(j-1)+2*A
                                      +epsilon*(1+a*c^j))]^2. (3.2)

Products with no factors are1. In particular these formulas include
J=m-1, a single checkpoint with equal prefix and terminal sums.
The numerator in(3.1) is exactly b_j-1 throughout.

For useful normalized forms put

 delta_l=[(1-zeta)*2^l-1]/(zeta*c^l),
 u_rc_j=1/(a*c^j),
 D_first=chi*(1+delta_J)+epsilon*(c-2)*(1+u_rc_J),
 D_j=chi*(1+delta_(j-1))+epsilon*v*(1+u_rc_j), j>J.

Then(3.1) is exactly

 F5=(c-2)^2*v^[2*(k-1)]
       /[(1+2*S_(m-1))*D_first^2*product_(j>J)D_j^2]. (3.3)

For(3.2), replace D_first by D_first+2A/(zeta*c^J),
replace D_j by D_j+2A/(zeta*c^(j-1)), change the terminal factor
1+2S to2(1+S), and multiply the numerator by
product_(j=J)^(m-1)(1+u_rc_j)^2. These are exact identities.

There is only ONE factor c-2 in the unsquared numerator; the later
factors are v=c(c-2). Treating the first incoming block as H_(J-1)
would falsely multiply the eventual answer by lambda^2.

## 4. The activation phase, including its logarithmic correction

The allowance in(1.3) has a convenient exact identity:

 A=epsilon + sqrt(1+epsilon^2)/[epsilon*(1-kappa)]
                *[exp(Theta/kappa)-kappa*exp(kappa*Theta)].   (4.1)

To verify it, 1+(1+kappa)T_sp=(exp(dTheta/kappa)-kappa)/(1-kappa)
and chi*exp(dTheta/kappa)=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)exp(Theta/kappa).
This calculation uses only the definitions of numbers.

With kappa=K/m and epsilon=E/m, the integral formula for arctan gives
Theta=pi/2-E/m+O(m^(-3)). Hence(4.1) gives

 log A=pi*m/(2K)+log m-log E-E/K+O(1/m),
 log(A/epsilon)=pi*m/(2K)+2log m-2log E-E/K+O(1/m).     (4.2)

For example, factor exp(Theta/kappa) out of the square bracket in
(4.1). Its relative correction is exponentially small in m, uniformly
for these fixed K,E; the remaining factors have ordinary Taylor
remainders O(1/m). The positive exponential term also dominates the
separate additive epsilon. This justifies taking the logarithm.

The other term1+2/epsilon^2 in R_m grows polynomially, whereas
A/epsilon has positive exponential rate. Therefore it is the latter
that equals R_m for all sufficiently large m. Since
log c=log lambda+tau/m+O(m^(-2)) and a->1/lambda, the ceiling gives

 J_m=(nu/K)*m+[2/log lambda]*log m+O(1).              (4.3)

The bound O(1) includes rounding and all fixed tau,K,E contributions.
In particular:

 * K<nu gives J_m>m eventually;
 * K=nu ALSO gives J_m>m eventually, due to the positive log m term;
 * K>nu gives 1<=J_m<m eventually and k/m->1-nu/K.

Thus the borderline cannot be declared an active chain by dropping
the polynomial prefactor of A. The activation allowance is fully
paid in the selected phase theta=nu/K, even though individual
activation corrections later disappear from the product.

## 5. Uniform accumulated errors in the nonempty case

Suppose K>nu. The constants in this section may depend on the fixed
tau,K,E but not on m or the selected index. In particular c stays in
a compact subset of(2,infinity), a,zeta stay bounded away from0,
1-zeta=O(1/m), and J grows linearly with m.

For every l>=J the exact mass error obeys

 |delta_l| <= [(1-zeta)/zeta]*(2/c)^l+(1/zeta)*c^(-l).

Consequently

 sum_(l>=J)|delta_l|
 <= [(1-zeta)/zeta]*(2/c)^J/(1-2/c)
       +(1/zeta)*c^(-J)/(1-1/c),
 sum_(j>=J)u_rc_j=c^(-J)/[a*(1-1/c)].                  (5.1)

These are bounds on the WHOLE growing index set, by extending it to
an infinite geometric series with nonnegative terms. They tend to0
exponentially. There is no multiplication of an uncontrolled
pointwise o(1) by the checkpoint count.

Set d_first=chi+epsilon*(c-2), d_bulk=chi+epsilon*v. Both are at least
chi>=1. The relative difference D_first/d_first-1 is bounded in
absolute value by |delta_J|+epsilon*(c-2)*u_rc_J; the corresponding
bulk difference is bounded by |delta_(j-1)|+epsilon*v*u_rc_j.
The sum of all these bounds tends to0 by(5.1), with delta_J counted
twice when needed. They are eventually at most1/2 individually.
The integral identity for log implies |log(1+t)|<=2|t| for |t|<=1/2.
It follows that the total error in replacing their logarithms by
log d_first and log d_bulk tends to0, with the explicit control(5.1).

It remains to pay the different numerator and ADDITIVE activation
in F5a. Divide(3.2) by(3.1), and for its exact incoming sums s_j put
V_j=2A/(chi*s_j+epsilon*b_j). Then

 F5a/F5=(1+2*S_(m-1))/[2*(1+S_(m-1))]
              *product_(j=J)^(m-1)[(1+u_rc_j)/(1+V_j)]^2. (5.2)

For all sufficiently large m, S_l>=zeta*c^l/2 for l>=J by(5.1).
Because A*c^(-J)<=epsilon*a from the EXACT cutoff,

 sum_j V_j
 <=[4A/(zeta*chi)]*c^(-J)*[1+1/(1-1/c)]
 <=[4epsilon*(c-2)/chi]*[1+1/(1-1/c)].                (5.3)

The first term in the bracket pays the exceptional prefix, and the
geometric sum pays every later block, including the repeated H_J.
Using log(1+t)<=t for t>=0 in(5.2) yields

 |log(F5a/F5)|
 <=1/(1+2*S_(m-1)) + 2*c^(-J)/[a*(1-1/c)]
       +[8epsilon*(c-2)/chi]*[1+1/(1-1/c)] ->0.      (5.4)

This is O(1/m) apart from exponentially small terms. The maximum
activation in F5 is inactive EXACTLY by(2.3); the separate activation
in F5a has been summed explicitly. Neither assertion is made for
earlier physical records excluded by(1.5).

## 6. Leading constants and the benchmark limit

The elementary expansions needed for the growing product are

 log v=gamma*tau/m+O(m^(-2)),
 log chi=pi*K/(2m)+O(m^(-2)),
 log(chi+epsilon*v)=(pi*K/2+E)/m+O(m^(-2)).           (6.1)

The remainders are uniform on the finite selected index set because
the quantities in(6.1) depend only on m, not the checkpoint index.
Multiplying an O(m^(-2)) remainder by at most m factors gives O(1/m).
Combining(3.3),(5.1),(6.1),

 F5=(c-2)^2/[ (1+2*S_(m-1))*d_first^2 ]
           *[v/(chi+epsilon*v)]^[2*(k-1)]*exp(o(1)).   (6.2)

Now d_first->1, (c-2)^2->lambda^(-2), and(2.1) gives

 lambda^m/(1+2*S_(m-1)) -> lambda*exp(-tau)/2.        (6.3)

For(6.3), divide S_(m-1) by zeta*c^(m-1); its error tends to0 by
(5.1), zeta->1, and (lambda/c)^m=(1+tau/m)^(-m)->exp(-tau).
The product exponent in(6.2) tends to

              (q_rc*tau-pi*K-2E)*(1-nu/K).             (6.4)

Equations(1.1),(6.2)--(6.4) prove(1.7) for F5; (5.4) proves the
same limit for F5a. The factor Z_1/(2lambda) includes BOTH the
exceptional first record and the terminal H_(m-1). Neither can be
removed. The beta parameter cancels from each large-scale transfer
because a/zeta=c-2; it has not been approximated independently in
m different factors.

For an explicit convergence description, (4.3) changes the product
logarithm by O(log(m)/m), (6.1) contributes O(1/m), and(5.1),(5.4)
give the displayed summable errors. Accepted04219554e41d9750 also gives

 0<=Z_1-lambda^(-m)B_(2^m-1)
 <=8sqrt(2)*2^(-m)+(3/25)*2^(rho/2)*lambda^(-m/2)
                              +(9/10000)*lambda^(-m),
 rho=log_2(lambda).

Thus the claimed O(log(m)/m) logarithmic convergence follows; Z_1
is bounded away from0. No optimization-limit interchange is used.

For K<=nu the selection is eventually empty by(4.3). Equation(2.1)
instead gives lambda^m/(1+2*S_m)->exp(-tau)/2, and the ratio between
the two empty-selection terminal factors tends1. This proves(1.6).
There is a real factor-lambda discontinuity between this empty rule
and the K downarrow nu limit of(1.7). Removing the exceptional
prefix to hide that discontinuity would change the calculation.

## 7. Existing exact enclosure of Z_1 and elementary constants

The public interval table certified in Section8 of accepted fact
04219554e41d9750 is
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/audit_c78/input_interval_table.csv,
SHA256 2a7585bc0bade9f85461456c13d60bbe618f85c7cc816e1ddc5b4c1a1d611e68.
The hash was checked; only saved rows were read. Its full original-split
certificate was NOT recomputed. At denominator

 D80=1208925819614629174706176

the saved entries include

 L1023=19205851554343006584447327614 <= D80*B_1023,
 D80*B_1024 <= H1024=19229705732238461978760749948.

Direct comparison of these fixed integers gives

             15880*D80<L1023, H1024<15910*D80.        (7.1)

These are two comparisons of already certified endpoints, not a new
benchmark evaluation. Further,

 140/99<sqrt(2)<99/70,
 lambda^10=3363+2378*sqrt(2),
                 6725<lambda^10<6727.               (7.2)

The square checks for the first line are19600<19602 and9800<9801;
substitution gives the last line. Apply(1.1) at j=10. Equations(7.1),
(7.2) imply

 Z_1>15880/6727>59/25,
 Z_1<(sqrt(15910)+3/100)^2/6725
     <15918/6725<237/100.                            (7.3)

For the middle upper comparison, sqrt(15910)<127, so its numerator
is below15910+381/50+9/10000<15918. For the lower rational comparison,
15880*25=397000>396893=6727*59. For the last upper comparison,
15918*100=1591800<1593825=6725*237. This proves(1.2).

We also use the elementary exact bounds

 25/8<pi<22/7, 7/8<log lambda<1,
 12/5<lambda<17/7, q_rc<14.                             (7.4)

Here are proofs of the less immediate constants, to avoid treating
an uncomputed decimal as evidence. The tangent addition formula shows
4 arctan(1/5)-arctan(1/239)=pi/4: the tangent is1 and the angle is
in(0,pi/2). For example tan(4 arctan(1/5))=120/119 and subtraction
of1/239 gives tangent1. The integral bounds
x-x^3/3<arctan x<x for x in(0,1) therefore give

 pi>16*(1/5-1/375)-4/239=281476/89625>25/8.

Polynomial division and integration give
22/7-pi=integral_0^1 x^4(1-x)^4/(1+x^2) dx>0.
For the logarithm put t=(lambda-1)/(lambda+1)=sqrt(2)-1>41/99.
Integrating the geometric series for 1/(1-t^2), or just its first
two terms as a strict lower bound, gives

 log lambda=2*integral_0^t du/(1-u^2)
      >2*(t+t^3/3)>2548888/2910897>7/8;
 8*2548888-7*2910897=14825>0.

Also exp(1)>1+1+1/2>lambda, so log lambda<1. The lambda bounds in
(7.4) follow by squaring7/5 and10/7 against sqrt(2); q_rc<14 follows
from sqrt(2)<3/2. We will also use exp(1)>8/3, obtained by retaining
one additional positive term in its power series.

## 8. Two regimes, exact success conditions, and no global optimality claim

### 8A. Regime I: the present parameter restriction

Write p_rc=pi+2 and b_rc=p_rc*nu. For K>nu and E>=K, the exponent increment
in(1.7) obeys

 (q_rc*tau-pi*K-2E)*(1-nu/K)
       <=(q_rc*tau-p_rc*K)*(1-nu/K)
       <=[(sqrt(q_rc*tau)-sqrt(b_rc))_+]^2,                (8.1)

where x_+=max(x,0). To prove the second bound, if q_rc*tau<=b_rc, its
left side is nonpositive. Otherwise set y=K/nu>1 and write it as
q_rc*tau+b_rc-b_rc*y-q_rc*tau/y. The inequality
(sqrt(b_rc*y)-sqrt(q_rc*tau/y))^2>=0 proves(8.1).
In this latter case equality is attained among these fixed parameters
by E=K=sqrt(q_rc*tau*nu/p_rc)>nu. This is an analytic maximization of an
already proved scalar limit in a named scaling. It asserts neither
optimality of the checkpoint rule nor permission to exchange a
varying-parameter optimization with the limit.

By(7.4), b_rc>[(25/8)(41/8)]/2=1025/128>8 and q_rc<14.
For every 0<tau<=1,

          [(sqrt(q_rc*tau)-sqrt(b_rc))_+]^2 < tau.         (8.2)

If the positive part is zero this is immediate from tau>0. Otherwise,
put x=sqrt(tau)<=1. The inequality
x*(sqrt(q_rc)-1)<=sqrt(q_rc)-1<sqrt(14)-1<sqrt(8)<sqrt(b_rc)
gives(8.2) after rearranging and squaring positive sides. The
middle comparison follows by squaring, equivalently5<4sqrt(2).
Thus(1.7),(8.1) imply, throughout this tau range,

 nonempty-rule limit < Z_1/(2lambda)<237/480<1/2.    (8.3)

At the ORIGINAL tau=1, the empty-rule limit also obeys

 Z_1/(2exp(1))<(237/200)*(3/8)=711/1600<1/2.         (8.4)

Together(8.3),(8.4) prove the stated failure for every fixed
0<K<=E at tau=1. The empty-rule limit for general tau is exactly
(1.6); for small tau it can exceed1, so(8.3) has deliberately not
been claimed for that case.

There is nevertheless a concrete positive scalar conclusion for NEW
words. Select analytically K=E=2nu, so theta=1/2. Since
gamma-1=3+2sqrt(2)=lambda^2, (1.7) becomes

 L_valid(tau)=Z_1/(2lambda)
                       *exp[lambda^2*tau-(pi+2)*nu]. (8.5)

For tau>=2, (7.4) gives

 (pi+2)*nu<3168/343<10,
 lambda^2>29/5,
 lambda^2*tau-(pi+2)*nu>8/5,
 Z_1/(2lambda)>(59/25)/(34/7)=413/850.

Since exp(8/5)>1+8/5=13/5,

                 L_valid(tau)>5369/4250>1.          (8.6)

This concerns each fixed tau>=2 and its own enclosing words, not the
original tau=1. The EXACT crossing for this fixed choice is

 tau_valid=[(pi+2)*nu+log(2lambda/Z_1)]/lambda^2,
                       1<tau_valid<2.               (8.7)

Indeed (8.5) increases strictly, fails at1 by(8.3), and exceeds1 at2
by(8.6). It equals1 at the displayed value. The already certified
Z_1 enclosure yields the exact containing interval with endpoints

 [(pi+2)*nu+log(200lambda/237)]/lambda^2,
 [(pi+2)*nu+log(50lambda/59)]/lambda^2.

For arbitrary fixed parameters in Regime I, the full constant-level
test is already explicit: if K<=nu use tau<=log(Z_1/2); if K>nu use

       (q_rc*tau-pi*K-2E)*(1-nu/K)
                     >=tau+log(2lambda/Z_1).         (8.8)

These are necessary and sufficient for the respective SCALAR LIMIT
to be at least1, with equality retained. They do not claim anything
about the finite-depth sign of the difference at equality.

### 8B. Regime II: a conditional decoupled target with an exact margin

Freeze, without numerical tuning,

 K=14/5, E=1/100,
 theta=5pi/(28log lambda), P_rc=14pi/5+1/50.

These satisfy E<K and therefore are OUTSIDE the frozen geometric
domain. Their scalar limit is exactly

 L_dec(tau)=Z_1/(2lambda)
                *exp[-tau+(q_rc*tau-P_rc)*(1-theta)].       (8.9)

The constant-level gain at tau=1 can be certified with elementary
rational inequalities, without evaluating an exponential or a
benchmark. From(7.2),(7.4),

 1-theta>123/343,
 q_rc-P_rc>8+560/99-44/5-1/50=23941/4950.

Both lower bounds are positive. Hence

 -1+(q_rc-P_rc)*(1-theta)
 >-1+(23941/4950)*(123/343)
 =1246893/1697850>73/100.                            (8.10)

The positive terms of the exponential series give

 exp(73/100)>1+73/100+(73/100)^2/2+(73/100)^3/6
                        =12367717/6000000.

Using Z_1/(2lambda)>413/850 again,

 L_dec(1)>5107867121/5100000000>1001/1000.            (8.11)

Finally q_rc*(1-theta)-1>13*(123/343)-1=1256/343>0.
The logarithm in(8.9) is therefore strictly increasing with tau.
Equation(8.11) holds for every fixed tau>=1. For all tau>0 the exact
crossing is

 tau_dec=[P_rc*(1-theta)+log(2lambda/Z_1)]
                               /[q_rc*(1-theta)-1],
                         0<tau_dec<1.                (8.12)

This is a definite positive scalar diagnostic, NOT a proof that
W_n is bounded below by it in Regime II. In particular the constraint
epsilon>=kappa has not silently been erased from the frozen lemma.

## 9. Exact geometric interface needed to use the conditional gain

The following is a conditional implication, not an additional scaling
regime or a claim that the necessary geometry exists. It specifies
which effective costs a future extension must actually prove.

Fix tau>0. Suppose a future construction supplies costs, as m grows,

 epsilon_m=E/m, E>0,
 m*log chi_m -> C,
 log A_m=alpha*m+O(log m), 0<alpha<log lambda,
 a checkpoint threshold T_m>=1 with
                  log T_m<=alpha*m+O(log m).         (9.1)

Define the SAME increasing-record pattern by the exact rule

 R_m=max{A_m/epsilon_m,T_m-1},
 J_m=max{1,ceil(log(R_m/a)/log c)}.                   (9.2)

If necessary one may include1 in the maximum without changing any
asymptotic assertion. Here a,c are still the exact enclosing-word
parameters, not geometric costs. The bounds(9.1) imply
J_m/m->theta=alpha/log lambda in(0,1), and every selected physical
step meets b_j>=T_m and epsilon_m*b_j>A_m. For the original frozen
threshold, T_m=2(1+epsilon_m^(-2)), this is precisely(1.5).

The REQUIRED geometric theorem must supply, simultaneously:

 (G1) ONE finite-dimensional convex differentiable L-smooth function
      f_m with an attained minimum, fixed before the run; an initial
      point x_0 outside its minimum set; and its actual GD trajectory using EVERY original
      physical step of H_(m,tau), in order, including its actual
      terminal point. Old and future components must remain part of
      that same function. All original directed interpolation rows,
      including the minimum and terminal rows, must hold.

 (G2) Positive scales D_rc_0,...,D_rc_k on that very trajectory, with
      transfers for the exact incoming sums in(2.2) bounded below by

      D_rc_(i+1)>=D_rc_i*(b_i-1)
          /{[chi_m*s_i+max(epsilon_m*b_i,A_m)]*eta_(m,i)},

      eta_(m,i)>=1, sum_(i=0)^(k-1)log eta_(m,i)->ell<infinity. (9.3)

      Thus any norm, gluing, or overlap losses must be included in
      the EFFECTIVE chi_m,epsilon_m,A_m or the SUMMED eta losses.
      Separate local trajectories without their global realization
      do not satisfy this requirement.

 (G3) An actual terminal value bound with the original smoothness L,

      2*(f_m(x_n)-min f_m)>=L*D_rc_k^2/(1+2*S_(m-1)).    (9.4)

 (G4) The TRUE squared initial distance satisfies

      dist(x_0,argmin f_m)^2<=r_rc_m*D_rc_0^2,
                       r_rc_m->r_rc in(0,infinity).         (9.5)

      A declared unit input coordinate alone does not establish(9.5).
      An exhibited minimizer with this distance bound suffices;
      otherwise the actual closest-minimizer argument is required.

These hypotheses are enough: multiply the ACTUAL inequalities(9.3),
use(9.4),(9.5), and normalize by L and the true radius. Then

 W_n(H_(m,tau)) >= F5/[r_rc_m*product_i eta_(m,i)^2].     (9.6)

Here W_n is the original supremum of
2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L dist(x_0,argmin f)^2), equivalently allowing any
minimizer and taking the closest for this instance. A function with
Lipschitz gradient at most L has the required L-smoothness. If the
construction changes its smoothness normalization, that change must
already be reflected in (G1)--(G3), not dropped after this step.

The proof of Sections2--6 applies to these costs as well. To see that
this is not an unproved transfer of an estimate, note that (9.1),(9.2)
give J=theta*m+O(log m); the exact mass estimates(5.1) are unchanged;
chi_m->1 is bounded away from0; (5.3) still follows from the exact
activation cutoff; and

 m*log(chi_m+epsilon_m*v)->C+E,
 m*log v->gamma*tau.

There are at most m factors, so the assumption m log chi_m->C
itself gives a summable o(1) error in the final logarithm. No stronger
pointwise-only estimate is substituted. Thus (9.6) proves

 liminf_m B_n W_n(H_(m,tau))
 >=Z_1/(2lambda*r_rc)
       *exp[-tau+(q_rc*tau-2C-2E)*(1-theta)-2ell].        (9.7)

Alternatively a geometry can supply transfers
b_i/[chi_m*s_i+2A_m+epsilon_m*b_i] and terminal factor
1/[2(1+S_(m-1))], with the same radius and accumulated eta costs.
Equation(5.4) proves that this source-format version has exactly the
same limit(9.7). This alternative preserves its actual numerator
and terminal normalization.

For the concrete conditional target of Section8B the necessary
nominal budgets are

 E=1/100, C=7pi/5, alpha=5pi/28,
 theta=5pi/(28log lambda),
 polynomial threshold, for example2(1+10000*m^2).

The nominal scalar activation(4.1) has exactly this alpha and its
log m prefactor was paid in the cutoff. A future theorem may have a
different allowance, but it must prove the displayed exponential
budget and threshold (or recalculate theta in(9.7)). With these
effective costs, (8.11),(9.7) reach the benchmark for every fixed
tau>=1 whenever

                         r_rc*exp(2ell)<1001/1000.       (9.8)

This is a conservative sufficient margin; the exact available budget
is r_rc*exp(2ell)<=L_dec(tau). For an unchanged activation phase but
additional limiting horizontal/tilt coefficients DeltaC,DeltaE>=0,
a sufficient paid version of(9.8) is

 r_rc*exp[2ell+2(DeltaC+DeltaE)*(1-theta)]<1001/1000.     (9.9)

If the activation phase or threshold exponent changes, use(9.7)
with its ACTUAL theta instead. No free activation, transfer, radius,
or smoothness normalization is hidden in this interface.

More generally the exact sufficient constant-level condition in(9.7)
is

 -tau+(q_rc*tau-2C-2E)*(1-theta)-2ell-log r_rc
                                    >=log(2lambda/Z_1). (9.10)

If this scalar expression is below the right side, this PARTICULAR
guarantee is insufficient; it does not upper-bound the constructed
function's actual error and does not prove that the geometry cannot
do better. The raw example-specific value, or another checkpoint
selection, could be larger.

## 10. Scope, remaining interface, and retrieval record

The exact all-depth products(3.1),(3.2), summable bounds(5.1),(5.3),
and limits(1.6),(1.7) resolve the allocated scalar diagnostic in one
joint UNVERIFIED draft. The calculations retain the actual physical
indices, unchanged enclosing parameters, first prefix, every gap,
terminal block, b_j-1 terms, thresholds, exponential activation and
true benchmark constant. No pointwise error has been applied to an
uncontrolled number of checkpoints.

The current-domain tau=1 certificate failure is only a failure of
the named selection rule in the fixed K/m,E/m scalings. It says
nothing about the optimal checkpoint set, formula(4)'s sharper
radius, other geometric parameters, or the unrestricted W_n. The
success at tau>=2 uses expressly NEW words. The success at tau>=1
with E<K is conditional on (G1)--(G4) at the actual costs; proving
those conditions is the precise remaining geometric obligation.

Even a complete geometric extension satisfying that interface for
H_(m,tau) would give a family-specific lower bound only. The original
goal requires all sufficiently large physical horizons and EVERY
nonnegative schedule, with a threshold independent of the schedule.
That uniform all-n/all-h theorem remains OPEN.

Skills used: query-memory, direct-proving, search-math-results. The
retrieval stage was active_program/repair/background, aimed at the
fixed checkpoint-product constants. Matlas returned three irrelevant
neural-network optimization results, none used. The primary page
https://arxiv.org/html/2609.09152v2 was opened for source context and
normalization; no published lemma is imported here. No new literature
branch is rejected: the selected work is elementary analysis of the
fully read frozen quantities. The source geometric audit and extension
belong to other workers and are not presumed complete.

Only accepted fact04219554e41d9750 supplies a mathematical premise.
All other new identities and inequalities used in this draft are
derived jointly above. Formal verification, new benchmark computation,
numerical tuning and additional worker rounds remain unallocated.

## Glossary for this joint finding

H_j: enclosing(m,tau) ruler subword, length2^j-1.
S_j: exact sum of H_j.
beta,c,a: enclosing-word parameters from Section1, fixed across children.
b_j: the original level-j physical step1+a*c^j.
t_j: zero-based position2^j-1 of its first occurrence.
chi,d,T_sp,A,Theta: explicitly defined geometric COST NUMBERS in(1.3).
F5,F5a: scalar checkpoint functionals(1.4); not definitions of W_n.
R_m,J_m: cutoff budget and first selected level in(1.5).
k: exact numberm-J_m of checkpoints when nonempty.
zeta,v: beta/[c(c-2)] and c(c-2), respectively.
delta_l,u_rc_j: exact normalized mass and numerator corrections in Section3.
D_first,D_j,d_first,d_bulk: scalar normalized denominators, Sections3,5.
V_j: additive activation relative cost2A/(chi*s_j+epsilon*b_j).
K,E: fixed scaling coefficients for kappa=K/m,epsilon=E/m.
nu,theta,gamma,q_rc: pi/(2log lambda),nu/K,4+2sqrt2,2gamma.
p_rc,b_rc: pi+2 and p_rc*nu in Section8A; b_rc is not a checkpoint.
L_valid,L_dec: limits for the specific parameter choices(8.5),(8.9).
P_rc: total conditional coefficient14pi/5+1/50 in Section8B.
tau_valid,tau_dec: scalar-limit crossing parameters(8.7),(8.12).
Z_1: accepted original benchmark ray limit(1.1).
D80,L1023,H1024: already certified rational-enclosure integers, Section7.
C,alpha,T_m: effective log-transfer coefficient, activation exponent,
  and physical checkpoint threshold in the CONDITIONAL interface.
D_rc_i: actual trajectory amplitude scales hypothesized ONLY in Section9.
eta_(m,i),ell: per-transfer loss and its total limiting logarithm.
r_rc_m,r_rc: upper squared-radius multipliers in(9.5) and their positive limit.
DeltaC,DeltaE: extra effective first-order transfer coefficients in(9.9).
rho: log_2(lambda), used in the accepted explicit ray-error estimate.

# Independent audit of the frozen all-word spiral checkpoint lemma

obstruction. The original uniform exact-profile comparison remains a GAP,
as the frozen draft explicitly acknowledges. No mathematical defect requiring
a repair was identified. This is a joint UNVERIFIED audit, not a verifier
acceptance, an accepted fact, or a solution of the original minimax problem.**

The audited object is exactly finding cdd7f057606aeb18, frozen at

The whole object was read, including Sections6A--8. It was not edited or
replaced by a later manuscript. Extensions outside its parameter range are

or sub-agent was used. Programs only retrieved/read/wrote text and checked
file integrity. The assignment allows this one audit and no renewal.
